Replaces all four direct reads of the build-time `VITE_API_BASE_URL` (`api/client.ts`, `api/liveJoin.ts`, `api/publicWrite.ts`, `pages/Login.tsx`) with one function; falls through to `VITE_API_BASE_URL` (kept as the local-dev path) then same-origin, so our own cloud's behaviour is unchanged. Separately, `services/llm/registry.ts`'s silent DeepSeek-fallback-on-provider-failure now checks `config.deploymentMode` first: `onprem` throws immediately instead of retrying on a different provider, since a silent cross-provider reroute is exactly the undocumented data path an on-prem customer's ИБ audit exists to catch (§3.5) — `saas`/`dedicated` keep the existing behaviour unchanged. 8 new unit tests total (4 + 4).

  **Sized but deliberately not started in the same pass: 2.3, prompts → versioned content packs.** Investigated before committing to it — prompts/rubric templates/criteria/FGOS data are inline across 20+ service files, the actual core grading/generation logic, not a same-session item alongside three small ones. Tracked as TODO #19 with the full reasoning rather than silently dropped.

  **Also blocked, not skipped: 2.9, `platform_admin` gating in onprem mode.** The plan doc itself calls this an unresolved governance question ("Decide before go-live," §15.6) and it depends on 2.7's not-yet-built licence file — building gating logic against an undecided design would mean guessing at the wrong shape twice.

  **Also found and fixed while in these files: three more leftover "ИИ" instances the earlier sweep's `pages/`/`data/` scope missed** — `index.html`'s meta description, `lib/useDocMeta.ts`'s default description (same string, mirrored), and two `api/client.ts` toast messages (`AI_SERVICE_ERROR`, `AI_RATE_LIMITED`) — plus a fuller sweep of `components/` (not covered by the original pass at all) across 8 files: grading result/confidence/recomputation UI, the long-review bullet actions, the cross-teacher-example tooltip, the shared-RAG toggle copy, and the revision-comparison label. `components/layout/PublicFooter.tsx`'s "Этика ИИ" link label and `Ethics.tsx` itself remain deliberately unswept, matching the standing legal-docs exception.
- **`DEPLOYMENT_MODE` + boot-time model-endpoint readiness check — first Track 2 item, on-prem-deployment.md §16.** New `config.deploymentMode` (`saas | dedicated | onprem`, default `saas` — our own cloud never sets it, so its behaviour is unchanged by construction). Gate crossed on "high confidence a deal is imminent," not a signature — Track 0 (discovery questionnaire, technical spike) hadn't run and Track 1's "several weeks in production" bar hadn't been met either (2 days in); explicit call recorded in the plan doc rather than silently overridden, and scoped to only the deal-agnostic slice of Track 2 as a result. New `backend/src/lib/deploymentReadiness.ts`'s `verifyDeploymentReadiness()` — separate from the existing synchronous `validateConfig()` (which `app.ts` shares with supertest, so it can never do a real network call) — does an actual HTTP round-trip to the configured model endpoint's `/models` route before `index.ts` calls `app.listen()`, no-op for saas/dedicated. Catches two failure modes that are silent today: `onprem` set with `DEEPSEEK_BASE_URL` left unset (calls would silently go to the public DeepSeek cloud API instead of the local deployment — exactly the cross-border transfer on-prem exists to avoid) and `onprem` set against an unreachable/non-responding endpoint (a config typo that would otherwise surface as "ИСПУМ не работает" three days later per the plan doc's own words, not at boot). 11 new unit tests, all four real branches of the readiness check covered via a mocked `axios`. Also drafted (not yet sent) a translated, externally-worded discovery questionnaire `.docx` from the plan's internal §12 bullet list, so Track 0.1 can go out in parallel.
- **`/docs` — infrastructure-requirements article for on-prem/BYOC, deliberately scoped to what's real today.** `docs-site/articles/on-prem/resource-requirements.md`. Requested as "two articles — local and BYOC resource requirements, including model/GPU hosting" but investigated before writing rather than drafted from the request as given: `on-prem-deployment.md` §2's dependency inventory shows the entire portable-deployment layer (S3-compatible storage instead of Yandex's, runtime-configurable frontend instead of build-time-baked, local OCR) is still planning-stage, not built — there is no working self-hosted/BYOC deployment mode today, so "local" vs "BYOC" isn't yet a real distinction to write two articles about. GPU/model-hosting sizing specifically is explicitly gated in the plan on a not-yet-run technical spike (Track 0.3) — publishing numbers now would mean inventing exactly what that spike exists to measure. Shipped one honest article instead: scale-tiered guidance (pilot / mid-size / large-scale university) derived from our real production footprint and `docs/scaling.md`'s actual capacity tiers, but **not quoting our literal box specs** — a first draft did (2 vCPU/2GB RAM/20GB SSD), caught in review as a bad idea twice over: it exposes how modest our own production infra actually is, and it's a number this doc would silently go stale against the moment we resize without anyone remembering to update the public article. Also documents Node 20/Postgres 15+pgvector/Docker as the real prerequisites, and a concrete network gotcha worth knowing before assuming "self-hosted" means air-gapped — embeddings currently always route through Yandex Cloud regardless of which chat model is used (CLAUDE.md rule #9). GPU/model sizing stays explicitly unpublished, deferred to a per-engagement technical assessment — a direct decision, not an oversight.
- **`/docs` — troubleshooting article; TODO #17/#18 opened for two real bugs found while writing the docs series.** `docs-site/articles/integration/troubleshooting.md`, TODO #16 item 5 (the last of the five setup/reference articles). Symptom-first triage, not a sixth copy of the other four articles' error tables — routes to the right deep-dive, and owns only the two categories none of them cover: plain password-login failures and file uploads. Documents `GET /api/health` as the one self-serve way to rule out a platform-wide issue before assuming it's local configuration, and a confirmed, real discontinuity in upload error quality: files 20–21 MB get ИСПУМ's own clean message, files over ~21 MB hit nginx's `client_max_body_size` first and get its raw default error page instead. Two things found during this article series' research were real product bugs, not docs gaps, and are now tracked as TODO #17 (single-invite and bulk-invite count differently against the teacher seat cap) and #18 (an expired/invalid invite link fails completely silently instead of telling the registering teacher anything went wrong) rather than just described as current behaviour and left there.
- **`/docs` — real teacher-provisioning article (invite/deactivate/kafedra).** `docs-site/articles/integration/teacher-provisioning.md`, TODO #16 item 4. States plainly that invite/deactivation is centrally IT-owned, not delegated per subtree, matching the `project_it-owns-provisioning` decision. Documents two gotchas found only by reading the actual route code, both easy to be bitten by from the UI alone: single-invite and bulk-invite count differently against the seat cap (single counts only active members; bulk also counts outstanding pending invites), and an expired/invalid invite link shows no error at all — it silently degrades to a normal org-less self-registration, which is the real explanation the next time a teacher says "I registered but don't see the organisation." Deactivation's actual effect (blocks every subsequent request rather than the login screen itself; already-published assignments/grades stay visible to students; fully reversible; self-deactivation blocked) is verified against `middleware/authenticate.ts`'s `ACCOUNT_DISABLED` check rather than assumed from the button label.
- **`/docs` — real access-model article (org tree, roles, domains).** `docs-site/articles/integration/access-model.md`, TODO #16 item 3 — the article flagged upfront as the trickiest to get right, since the domain axis has already caused real confusion internally (CLAUDE.md's own Org Tree Authorisation section). Written entirely in the grant form's actual Russian labels (Наблюдатель/Редактор/Администратор; Все области/Платформа/Учебно-методическая работа/Учебный процесс/УМУ) — confirmed via `InstitutionStructure.tsx` that raw domain slugs (`curriculum`, `umu`, etc.) are never shown to an admin, so the article never introduces them either, avoiding a vocabulary the reader will never actually see in the product. The domain-axis rationale is the Заведующий кафедрой (department head) example — not invented for the article, sourced from a near-identical comment repeated verbatim across `orgUnits.ts`/`rpdMonitor.ts`/`rpdApprovals.ts` and cross-checked against `docs/ACCESS-MATRIX.md` §4's compliance table. Documents the grant form's built-in guardrails (root-only admin+all, the governance/admin_office institution-wide-programmes warning, redundant-grant highlighting) and states plainly which actions stay permanently root-admin-only (invite/deactivate, primary-department reassignment, LTI/model/RAG settings) regardless of any domain grant.
- **`/docs` — real SAML SSO setup article.** `docs-site/articles/integration/saml-setup.md`, TODO #16 item 2, rewritten from `docs/saml-testing.md` ("how we test it") into "how it works" — turned out to need a genuinely different structure from the LTI article, not a find-replace: confirmed via the code (`requireAdmin` gates `admin.ts` entirely, vs. LTI's institution-admin-scoped `institution.ts`) that SAML has **no self-serve institution UI** — it's configured by ИСПУМ's platform admin on the customer's behalf via a panel in `AdminInstitutions.tsx`, so the article frames it as a two-sided value exchange coordinated out of band (what ИСПУМ hands the IT team, what the IT team hands back), states plainly there's no settings page to look for, and covers the first-login flow and the "only SSO, no password" toggle. Troubleshooting table's error codes/messages (`SAML_CONFIG_INCOMPLETE`, `SAML_NOT_CONFIGURED`, `SAML_VALIDATION_FAILED`, `SAML_NO_PROFILE`, `SAML_NO_EMAIL`, `SSO_REQUIRED`, `ACCOUNT_DISABLED`) grepped verbatim from `services/saml.ts`/`routes/sso.ts`/`routes/auth.ts` and independently re-verified against the source before publishing, same discipline as the LTI article.
- **`/docs` — real LTI 1.3 (Moodle) setup article.** `docs-site/articles/integration/lti-setup.md`, TODO #16 item 1 — the highest-priority gap identified when the docs shell shipped: LTI 1.3 incl. deep linking and NRPS was live with zero public documentation. Covers both registration paths (dynamic registration and manual field-by-field, with the exact field labels from `InstitutionLti.tsx` — not paraphrased), the mandatory email-claim requirement (the most common first-launch failure), why a manually-pasted External Tool link doesn't work for students (Deep Linking is required), course mapping, and a troubleshooting table mapping every real `LTI_*` error code from `routes/lti.ts`/`services/lti.ts` to a plain-language cause and fix — grepped directly from the code rather than recalled, so codes and Russian error messages match exactly.
- **Public documentation site (`/docs`) — shell, content pipeline, and 3 scaffold articles.** New `docs-site/` at repo root, deliberately separate from internal `docs/` and from `frontend/src/pages/`'s marketing content — this one is public and written for university IT teams, organised by reader (Интеграция и настройка / Безопасность и соответствие / Локальное развёртывание) rather than mirroring our own architecture, since integrators, ИБ/procurement, and (eventually) on-prem operators are three readers with almost no overlap in what they want.

  Markdown + frontmatter in, compiled at build time by `frontend/scripts/buildDocs.mjs` (new `marked` dependency) into a gitignored `frontend/src/generated/docs/` — a `manifest.json` the sidebar/index pages import directly, and one rendered HTML fragment per article, lazy-loaded per route via `import.meta.glob` so the bundle only carries the article actually being viewed. Wired as `predev`/`prebuild` hooks so generated output can never drift from the markdown source.

  **Crawlability, addressed deliberately rather than deferred**: the app is a pure client-rendered SPA on Object Storage with no server to render on request, so a plain SPA route would show crawlers an empty `<div id="root">`. `frontend/scripts/postbuildDocs.mjs` (new `postbuild` hook) writes a real static `dist/docs/<section>/<slug>/index.html` per article — correct per-article `<title>`/`<meta description>`, the rendered HTML inlined in a `<noscript>` block — real browsers still boot the normal SPA on top and never see the static markup, only crawlers that don't execute JS do. Also emits `dist/sitemap.xml` (marketing routes + every docs URL) and a new `frontend/public/robots.txt` pointing at it.

  **UI/UX pass, same day**: breadcrumbs (`DocsBreadcrumb.tsx`) on the section and article pages — the docs hub is 3 levels deep (Документация → раздел → статья), past the point breadcrumbs earn their keep. Article body capped at `max-w-[720px]` independent of the sidebar, since the previous full-bleed width (~900px inside the 1200px shell minus sidebar) ran past the ~75-character line-length that keeps body text readable. A custom `marked` heading renderer now slugifies every `##` into an anchor id and collects them per article at build time (`headings` in `manifest.json`) — powers an auto-generated table of contents, right-rail on desktop (`lg:` sticky) and inline above the body on mobile/tablet, with no second markdown pass or hand-maintained anchor list. The sidebar nav, which on mobile was previously always-expanded and pushed the entire article below the fold, now collapses behind a native `<details>`/`<summary>` disclosure below `md:` — no JS state, keyboard-operable and screen-reader-navigable by default, exactly the "prefer native controls over custom ones" default. Added `focus-visible` rings (amber, matching the brand accent) to every docs nav link, card, and breadcrumb link — beyond the search input, nothing had an explicit focus treatment before.

  Shipped with 3 scaffold articles (one overview per section, proving the pipeline end-to-end) rather than the full article set — the on-prem section is explicitly a stub ("available under a separate agreement") since [`docs/on-prem-deployment.md`](docs/on-prem-deployment.md)'s Track 2/3 hasn't shipped yet, and an unverified install guide would be worse than no guide. Real articles (SAML setup, LTI 1.3 setup, the org-role/domain access model, teacher provisioning, troubleshooting) are TODO.md-tracked follow-ups, written from the existing internal `docs/saml-testing.md` and `docs/ACCESS-MATRIX.md` rather than from scratch. Verified end-to-end in-browser (not just build success) — sidebar nav, title-only client-side search filter, per-article version stamp, and the static per-page `<title>`/meta/sitemap output were all checked against real rendered output, not assumed from a clean build log.
- **Second backend replica, zero-downtime rolling deploys — `deploy/docker-compose.cloud.yml`, `deploy.sh`, `deploy/rollback.sh`, `deploy/nginx/gradeassist.conf`.** [`docs/on-prem-deployment.md`](docs/on-prem-deployment.md) §16 TODO #13. Gate was three ordinary, independently-verified production deploys on the unchanged single-replica pipeline since the real stdin-eating fix (`780be08`, `efdbdd5`, `9bb644e`) — confirmed satisfied via `git log`, not assumed.

  The compose file gained a second `api2` service (PORT 3001) alongside `api` (3000), sharing everything else — image, `network_mode: host`, env file, restart policy, the bind-mounted `uploads/` volume — through an `x-api-common` YAML anchor, the compose-spec's own sanctioned "extension field" convention for this. The healthcheck was generalised from a hardcoded port to reading `process.env.PORT` at runtime so one definition covers both replicas. nginx gained `upstream ispum_api { least_conn; server 127.0.0.1:3000; server 127.0.0.1:3001; }` — `least_conn` over round-robin because AI-grading request duration varies wildly (a quick quiz call vs. a multi-minute ВКР map-reduce pass), so routing to whichever replica has fewer in-flight requests balances load better than blind alternation.

  `deploy.sh`/`rollback.sh` do a genuine **rolling** restart — recreating both containers simultaneously would reproduce the exact all-at-once outage a second replica exists to eliminate. `api2` force-recreates first; the script polls Docker's own `.State.Health.Status` (not a single curl) for up to 120s before touching `api`, so nginx's upstream always has a live backend to route to throughout. The image-match assertion (from the earlier stdin-eating-bug fix) now checks both containers, and the final health check hits `:3000`/`:3001` directly rather than only through nginx, which would happily report healthy off one working replica while the other silently failed its own recreate.

  **Verified, not assumed, twice over**: the compose file's YAML anchor/merge syntax was actually parsed (`js-yaml` with the YAML 1.1 schema — the default schema silently does *not* resolve merge keys, which would have hidden a real bug) to confirm both services resolve to the intended per-replica config, not just visually inspected. Both new heredocs in `deploy.sh`/`rollback.sh` were rendered with placeholder values (substituting `cat` for the real `ssh` invocation, matching the exact escaping bash performs) and `bash -n`-checked as the literal multi-line script that reaches the remote shell — the same class of check that would have caught the stdin-eating bug on day one, applied proactively here rather than after an incident forced it.

  **Rolled out to production, same day.** CI green on `1c94525`. The manual nginx step turned out to need only one edit, not two — certbot had added `listen 443 ssl` onto the original block rather than duplicating it, so the live VM config had a single `proxy_pass` to point at the new `upstream ispum_api`. `nginx -t && systemctl reload nginx` came back clean, `curl https://ispum.ru/api/health` confirmed traffic still flowed before touching the app. `./deploy.sh` then exercised the new rolling-restart path for the first time under a real condition it hadn't hit before — `api2` didn't exist yet, so this was creation, not recreation — and the output matched the intended sequence: `api2` created and confirmed healthy, then `api` recreated and confirmed healthy, both image-matched, `[8/8]` passing `:3000`, `:3001` and the public URL. Independently re-checked afterward rather than trusting the script's own report: `docker ps` on the VM showed both containers `Up ... (healthy)` on the new tag, and `pm2 list` showed no app process at all — ruling out the exact PM2-port-conflict failure mode that caused a real incident earlier the same day.
- **New Non-Negotiable Rule #12 — migrations are expand/contract, never rename or drop in the release that stops using it.** [`docs/on-prem-deployment.md`](docs/on-prem-deployment.md) §16 Track 1.8, §7.7. Closes out Track 1 — the last item on the foundations list. Policy, not code: `CLAUDE.md` gets the rule, `docs/CONVENTIONS.md` mirrors it with Why/How-to-apply and folds the sequencing requirement into the existing "Adding a migration" recipe (which already required forward-only/additive SQL — this adds the specific drop/rename timing rule that wasn't previously spelled out). The reasoning: `deploy/rollback.sh` repoints to a previous image tag *without* re-running migrations, which is only actually safe if the previous release's code still works against whatever the schema currently is — a same-release drop means rolling the code back without rolling the schema back, and the old code breaks against a column that's already gone. Adopted now specifically because it gets harder to retrofit once multiple deployments can be on different schema versions at once, not easier — the same "do it while there's one deployment to be wrong about" reasoning behind the control-plane and fleet-dashboard work above.
- **New admin page: Развёртывания (`/admin/deployments`) — the fleet overview reading from the control-plane tables.** [`docs/on-prem-deployment.md`](docs/on-prem-deployment.md) §16 Track 1.7, §5.4. One card per deployment: a connectivity dot (live/stale/offline, §5.5 — computed from `last_heartbeat_at` against a 45-minute threshold, 3× the agent's 15-min tick, enough slack for one missed tick without a false flag), version, active seats, DB health, pg-boss queue depth, 24h error count, and a relative "last signal" line. New `listDeploymentsSummary()` (`db/queries/controlPlane.ts`) — one row per deployment, lateral-joined against its most recent heartbeat straight out of the envelope JSONB (no second round trip) plus a 24h count from `deployment_incidents`; new `GET /api/admin/deployments` on the existing `routes/admin.ts` router, same `requireAdmin` platform-only gate as every other admin route. Polls every 60s — this page *is* the freshness signal, so it must not itself go stale silently; every figure is paired with the freshness line it belongs to rather than shown in isolation, enforcing §5.5's "a stale number shown as live is worse than a blank" rule by construction, not by convention. **Scoped deliberately narrow**: this is purely additive — reworking the four *existing* admin pages (`AdminOverview`, `AdminUsage`, `AdminCapacity`, `AdminErrors`) to actually read from the control-plane tables is real rework on pages people already use daily, so it's left as its own follow-up rather than folded in here. Typecheck (both workspaces) and a full production frontend build are clean; live in-browser click-through wasn't completed this session — the browser-automation tooling denied navigation against an otherwise-confirmed-running dev server, unrelated to the code itself.
- **Control-plane skeleton — signed telemetry heartbeats, migration 113, new `/api/control-plane/ingest` route, `services/controlPlaneAgent.ts`.** [`docs/on-prem-deployment.md`](docs/on-prem-deployment.md) §16 Track 1.6, §5. Every future ИСПУМ deployment — including on-prem customers eventually — is meant to push the same aggregate telemetry envelope to one control plane, so the admin dashboard reads only that, never production data directly, and our own cloud deployment is the one the code is developed and tested against. **Phase 1, shipped now**: our own cloud pushes to *itself* over localhost — proves the whole sign → ingest → verify → store path end to end with zero on-prem risk, before any real remote deployment exists.
  - **Four tables** (`deployments`, `deployment_heartbeats`, `deployment_usage_monthly`, `deployment_incidents`) — `deployment_heartbeats` stores the raw envelope as JSONB (append-only, so derived tables can be recomputed if their shape changes later); `deployment_usage_monthly` is literally `institution_rollup_monthly` plus a `deployment_id` column; `deployment_incidents` is grouped counts by deployment **and app version** (`production_incidents.code` only — never `.message`, matching the design's "aggregates and identifiers only" rule; there's no message-classification field yet to bucket by finer than that, a known simplification, not an oversight). Seeds one `deployments` row for `ispum-cloud` with a fixed, well-known id.
  - **Per-deployment signing, not one shared key.** Every deployment gets its own RS256 keypair (`backend/scripts/generateControlPlaneKeypair.ts`, same `jose` pattern as the existing SAML/LTI keypair scripts — RS256 for consistency, not because it's the best available choice) — the private key stays in that deployment's own environment, the public key lives on its `deployments.public_key` row. The envelope **is** the signed JWT payload (`services/controlPlane/signing.ts`, `SignJWT`/`jwtVerify`, `sub` = deployment id) rather than a separate envelope-plus-detached-signature scheme — one less thing to invent, reusing exactly the mechanism LTI's launch JWTs already use. Verification is necessarily two-step: `decodeDeploymentId()` peeks at the unverified `sub` claim only to know which deployment's public key to fetch, then `verifyEnvelope()` does the real cryptographic check — nothing else from an unverified payload is ever trusted. One deployment's key can never forge another's telemetry; two dedicated regression tests cover exactly that (cross-deployment forgery, tampered-byte rejection), verified with a real generated keypair pair, not mocked.
  - **The ingest route has no JWT auth** (`routes/controlPlaneIngest.ts`) — a deployment agent has no teacher session, so it authenticates purely via the envelope's own signature, same shape as the existing T-Bank webhook route (`webhookLimiter`, no `authenticate` middleware, verification inline). An unknown deployment id and a deployment with no `public_key` on file get the *identical* 401 — distinguishing them would let a caller enumerate which deployment ids are real.
  - **`buildEnvelope()`** assembles the envelope from things this deployment already computes for itself regardless of telemetry: `app_version` from the existing `VERSION` file, `schema_version` from the latest applied migration filename, active-model call counts and error rates from the already-existing `getUsageByModel()` query, usage rows straight from `getInstitutionRollupForMonth()`, incident counts grouped from `production_incidents`. `queueDepth` degrades to `0` rather than throwing if pg-boss isn't started yet (`getJobQueue()` throws synchronously by design) — one degraded health signal must never crash the whole heartbeat; covered by an integration test that deliberately runs with no job queue started at all, since that's exactly the integration harness's own condition. `failedJobs24h` was scoped out of the envelope entirely rather than guessed at: pg-boss's public `getQueueSize()` API can only count jobs *before* a given state in an ordered sequence, which excludes failed jobs rather than isolating them, and there's no time-windowed variant — getting it would mean querying pg-boss's own internal table directly, a real fragility risk given this pg-boss version is already deliberately pinned to v10.x specifically because v12 changed too much.
  - **Cluster-safe from day one** — the agent schedules via the Track 1.4a lease (`scheduleWithLease`), so a second replica later doesn't double-send a tick's heartbeat.
  - Inert until configured: `CONTROL_PLANE_PRIVATE_KEY` unset (the default) means `startControlPlaneAgent()` returns immediately and `validateConfig()` warns once at boot — shipping this code is safe regardless of whether anyone has generated a keypair yet.
  - **Found and fixed a real, if minor, pre-existing gap while testing this**: none of the eight rate limiters (`middleware/rateLimits.ts`) had a test-mode exemption, and the integration suite runs `fileParallelism: false` (required for `DB_POOL_MAX=1`'s transaction-per-test isolation), meaning every test file shares one in-memory rate-limit counter for the whole run, never resetting between files. The suite's cumulative request count was always going to cross `generalLimiter`'s 200/15min ceiling eventually as more integration tests get added; this feature's own new test files were what tipped it over first, intermittently failing an unrelated SSO test with no code connection to it. Added a shared `SKIP_IN_TEST` guard (`NODE_ENV === 'test'` only — `.env.test` sets it, dev/prod never do) to all eight limiters. **Note:** this did not fully explain a separate, lower-frequency flakiness also observed in the full suite (a different, unrelated test failing under added connection contention, non-deterministically) — that looks like pre-existing latent fragility in the test suite itself (a `Date.now()`-based fixture collision is the leading hypothesis), confirmed pre-existing via a clean 3-run baseline before this feature's files existed. Flagged separately rather than fixed here, since it's out of scope for this feature and the control-plane code's own tests are 100% reliable in isolation and combined.
  - **Activated live in production, same day.** Keypair generated, private key added to the VM's `.env`, deployed, public key stored on the seeded `ispum-cloud` row. The very first tick legitimately failed — it fires before the deployment row's public key can possibly be set, since that row doesn't exist until the migration running *during the same deploy* that starts the agent — and, correctly per the scheduler-lease design, held the `control_plane_heartbeat` lease for its full 10 minutes even on that failure; confirmed directly via the `scheduler_leases` table rather than assumed. A restart after that lease naturally expired produced a genuine, fully verified heartbeat: `deployment_heartbeats.envelope->platform->appVersion` matches the deployed commit exactly, and `deployments.first_seen_at`/`last_heartbeat_at`/`current_version` all populated correctly. Confirmed against real production infrastructure, not just tests.
- **`deploy.sh` now deploys the backend as a container, not a source rsync (`deploy/docker-compose.cloud.yml`).** [`docs/on-prem-deployment.md`](docs/on-prem-deployment.md) §16 Track 1.4b — the deploy half of the source→artifact inversion started in 1.3. The backend's source no longer goes to the VM at all; only a small compose file does, and it pulls the exact image CI already built and tested for that commit. **One replica** to start (deliberately, over two — proves the pipeline with the fewest moving parts; the scheduler-lease work above already makes either count safe, this is purely a complexity/throughput trade-off, documented in the plan). `network_mode: host` was the key design choice: the container's `localhost:5432` genuinely is the VM's Postgres (which stays on the host, unchanged), so `DATABASE_URL` needed zero edits, and the container binds `127.0.0.1:3000` directly, so nginx (which already proxies there) needed zero edits either — the whole migration is additive, nothing existing was touched. `uploads/` is bind-mounted to the *same* path PM2's process used, not a fresh named volume, so any files already there aren't silently orphaned. A `migrate` one-shot service (`profiles: [tools]`, run via `docker compose run --rm migrate` before `up -d api`) uses the *same* image as `api` — migrating with the exact code about to serve traffic, closing the gap where a customer's upgrade could otherwise migrate with one build and serve with another. `deploy.sh` gained a **CI gate**: it now polls `gh run list` for the exact commit and refuses to deploy until CI has actually passed — a tag is only a promise if nothing deploys before the promise is checked, and CI's ~2-minute runtime makes a push-then-immediately-deploy a real race, not a hypothetical one. The unpushed-commits check was **escalated from a warning to a hard block** for the same reason: rsync used to ship the working tree regardless of git state; a pull-based deploy can only fetch what CI actually built, so an unpushed commit now fails fast with a clear message instead of failing confusingly three steps later at `docker pull`. New `YC_REGISTRY_ID` in `.env.example` (deploy-only, same sourcing convention as `CDN_RESOURCE_ID`). **One-time, deliberately manual cutover**: `pm2 delete gradeassist-api && pm2 save`, documented in `deploy.sh`'s header rather than automated — an unconditional `pm2 delete` baked into every future run would itself fail loudly the moment PM2 no longer manages anything, breaking every deploy after the first. Expect a few seconds of downtime at every future single-replica restart, a real behaviour change from PM2's zero-downtime `pm2 reload` across 2 workers — a second replica later is what buys that back. Registry setup (Container Registry with upload + weekly vulnerability scanning enabled, two scope-separated service accounts — CI can push, the VM can only pull) was done by hand via the YC console, not scripted. **Not yet run end-to-end against production** — written and syntax-verified locally; the operator still needs to trigger the actual cutover deploy.
- **Cluster-safe schedulers — the PM2 `NODE_APP_INSTANCE` worker-0 gate replaced with a Postgres lease (migration 112, `services/schedulerLease.ts`).** Prerequisite for running the API as a container ([`docs/on-prem-deployment.md`](docs/on-prem-deployment.md) §16 Track 1.4), and a latent hazard regardless. Five schedulers — `renewals`, `activation`, `activationDigest`, `usageRollup`, `resourceSampler` — decided "am I the one process that runs the cron?" by reading `NODE_APP_INSTANCE`, **a variable only PM2 sets**. In a container it is unset, so every replica falls through to the `?? '0'` default and believes it is worker 0: two API containers would have fired the daily renewal sweep and the 5-minute payment reconciliation twice per cycle. Replaced with a short lease claimed **per tick** rather than leader election at start-up: every instance keeps its timers, each tick races for the lease, only the winner runs. Per-tick claiming means a holder that dies mid-tick costs nothing — the lease lapses and the next tick anywhere picks the work up, whereas start-up election would stall every background job until a restart. The claim is a single `INSERT … ON CONFLICT … WHERE expires_at <= NOW()` statement, so concurrent claimants resolve atomically in the database (a read-then-write version would race), and it avoids holding a pool connection in an open transaction across a multi-second renewals sweep the way an advisory lock would. The lease is deliberately **not** released when a job finishes — holding it to expiry is what enforces "once per interval" — and it is retained when a job *throws*, so one broken job can't hot-loop across every replica. `jobQueue.ts` is untouched: pg-boss's `SKIP LOCKED` polling is already safe multi-process. Two intentional behaviour changes: a restart no longer re-runs the renewal sweep 60s after boot (a 23h lease means at most one sweep per day whatever we deploy — the old behaviour was wasteful rather than harmful, since `runRenewals` only acts on subscriptions actually due), and resource-sampler RSS/heap figures now come from whichever instance won the tick rather than always PM2's worker 0 (both are one worker's memory, so the capacity signal is unchanged). Six integration tests cover the exactly-one-winner property, expiry-based failover, lease retention after both success and failure.
- **Backend container image built in CI (`backend/Dockerfile`, `.dockerignore`).** [`docs/on-prem-deployment.md`](docs/on-prem-deployment.md) §16 Track 1.3 — the source→artifact inversion. Today `deploy.sh` rsyncs the working tree and runs `tsc` **on the production VM**; an immutable image means the compiler never runs in production, rollback is repointing a tag, and the same artifact can later be handed to a self-managed customer. Multi-stage: a builder stage runs `npm ci` + `npm run build --workspace=backend` and asserts `dist/backend/src/index.js` exists (tsc's `rootDir: "../"` puts the entry point there, not at `dist/index.js` — a silent layout change would otherwise surface only as a container that exits immediately); the runtime stage reinstalls with `--omit=dev` and copies just the build output, `migrations/`, `scripts/migrate.js` and `assets/` (the PT Sans/Serif fonts the PDF exporters load from `process.cwd()/assets/fonts`). Migrations ship **inside** the image so an operator migrates with the exact code that will serve traffic — the on-prem upgrade path depends on it. **Debian `node:20-slim`, not Alpine**, deliberately: `@resvg/resvg-js` and `pdfjs-dist`'s `@napi-rs/canvas` ship `linux-x64-gnu` prebuilds, so musl would silently fall back to a source build or fail — breaking PPTX formula rendering and PDF rasterisation, both of which have tests that pass locally. Build context is the repo root (the backend imports `../shared/types.ts` and needs the root lockfile), cwd is `/app/backend` so `../VERSION` and `assets/fonts` resolve exactly as they do under PM2, and the container runs as the unprivileged `node` user. Config comes from the container environment, so the image bypasses `npm start` (which passes `--env-file=../.env`, a file that must never exist in an image). CI builds the image on every run — which is what catches a broken Dockerfile — and pushes only from `main`, only when `YC_REGISTRY_ID` and `YC_SA_JSON_KEY` are configured; the repo name falls back to a local-only name when they aren't, since an empty registry id would render the invalid tag `cr.yandex//ispum-backend` and fail the *build*, not just the push. Yandex Container Registry rather than ghcr.io or Docker Hub because production is in Yandex Cloud and customers behind a RU perimeter can't be assumed to reach foreign registries. Image tags are `{semver}-{short-sha}` (immutable) plus a moving `main`. Not yet consumed by `deploy.sh` — that's Track 1.4 — and no container is booted in CI yet (Track 3.7).
- **CI — integration tests against a real Postgres + pgvector, and a semantic version.** [`docs/on-prem-deployment.md`](docs/on-prem-deployment.md) §16 Track 1.1b/1.2. Second CI job runs the 199-test DB-backed suite against a `pgvector/pgvector:pg15` service container — **PG 15 to match the production VM**, same reasoning as the Node 20 pin (`vm-setup.sh` pins PG 15 from the PGDG repo because Ubuntu 24.04 ships no `postgresql-15-pgvector`). It creates the database, enables pgvector and applies all 111 migrations with the same runner production uses, so a migration that only works against an already-populated schema now fails in CI rather than on a customer's box later. `.env.test` is gitignored (it holds real local SAML/LTI keys), so CI synthesises a disposable one via the new `backend/scripts/generateTestEnv.ts` (`npm run test:env`) — random JWT secret, freshly generated SAML SP keypair + self-signed cert, LTI RS256 keypair, fake T-Bank credentials. Useful beyond CI: a new contributor gets a working integration environment in one command instead of hand-assembling a dozen vars, and it refuses to clobber an existing `.env.test` without `--force`. Separately, the deploy now stamps a **semantic version** — single-sourced from the root `package.json` (`1.5.0`), so `VERSION`, `/api/health` and the sidebar footer read `1.5.0 (2026-08-15+abc1234)`. The semver is what a customer, a support matrix and (later) a licence file refer to; the date+SHA stays as build metadata. Deploy warns when no matching `v{semver}` git tag exists.
- **CI — the repository's first pipeline (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`), plus a working-tree guard on `deploy.sh`.** Groundwork for [`docs/on-prem-deployment.md`](docs/on-prem-deployment.md) §16 Track 1.1/1.2, but owed to the cloud product regardless: until now nothing ran automatically on push, so a green local run was the only gate. The workflow typechecks and unit-tests both workspaces on push/PR to `main` (775 backend + 41 frontend tests) and builds the frontend so Vite/PWA build failures surface in CI rather than mid-deploy. Pinned to **Node 20** to match the production VM (`vm-setup.sh` installs nodesource 20.x) rather than whatever is current on a dev laptop — a version skew between CI and prod is exactly the class of bug CI should be catching. New `typecheck` scripts (`tsc --noEmit`) on both workspaces and aggregate `test`/`typecheck` scripts at the root, so CI and humans share one entry point. Integration tests are deliberately excluded for now (they need a Postgres + pgvector service container and `test:integration:setup`) — next slice. Separately, `deploy.sh` now **refuses to deploy a dirty working tree**: it rsyncs the working tree but stamps `VERSION` from `git rev-parse HEAD`, so deploying with uncommitted changes put code in production that no commit contained, labelled with a commit that didn't describe it. Tolerable with one server; fatal once telemetry from multiple deployments is keyed on version. `ALLOW_DIRTY_DEPLOY=1` overrides for emergencies and marks the version `+dirty` so the discrepancy is at least visible; unpushed commits warn rather than block.
- **PPTX export — formulas rendered as images instead of flattened Unicode (`services/formulaRenderer.ts`).** A teacher reported that the RANS/Navier-Stokes equations on her exported deck looked nothing like her source notes — `\left[`/`\right]`/`\overline{}` etc. weren't in `latexToPlainText()`'s command table, so the fallback dropped the backslash and left literal words (`left[`, `overlineu_i'u_j'`) glued onto the formula. Root cause was the export path, not how she got her notes into ИСПУМ (paste vs. upload never touches the LaTeX — it's model-generated from the slide prompt, and the web viewer already renders it correctly via KaTeX). `addFormulaSlide()` in `presentationExport.ts` now renders each formula through MathJax (headless, TeX → SVG, no browser) → `@resvg/resvg-js` (SVG → PNG) and embeds it as a picture, matching the KaTeX web viewer's typesetting. `latexToPlainText()` stays as the fallback if rendering fails for a given formula (packages missing, MathJax parse error) rather than the export failing outright. New deps: `mathjax-full`, `@resvg/resvg-js`.
- **Presentation form — upload a PDF/Word conspectus instead of only pasting it, with formulas recovered from `.docx` (`services/ommlToLatex.ts`).** Follow-up to the export fix above: a teacher's *input* conspectus was also losing its formulas — pasting from Word into the "Свой конспект" `<textarea>` can only ever carry the `text/plain` clipboard flavour, and a Word equation object (OOXML `<m:oMath>`) has no plain-text form, so it just vanished, leaving blank gaps before equation numbers like `(2.9)`. `documentExtractor.ts`'s DOCX path previously had the same gap — `mammoth.extractRawText()` silently drops `m:oMath` too. Added a custom `word/document.xml` walker (`extractDocxTextWithFormulas`) that converts the common OMML constructs (`\frac`, sub/superscript, `\sqrt`, `\left..\right` delimiters, `\overline`/`\underline`, accents, n-ary sums/integrals, named functions, equation arrays, matrices) to LaTeX and inlines them as `$...$`/`$$...$$`; unhandled OMML falls back to raw text rather than dropping the node. Only takes over from mammoth when it actually finds a formula — every existing docx-extraction call site (grading, RPD, programs, FGOS) is unaffected for the (overwhelming majority of) documents with no math. New `POST /api/presentations/extract-text` endpoint (gated by the existing `documentUpload` plan feature) and a file-upload button next to the textarea in `PresentationForm.tsx`. PDF math intentionally stays out of scope — PDF equations are usually drawn glyphs, not structured/extractable math, so PDF upload gets plain text only, same as before. New dep: `fast-xml-parser`.

- **Presentations — «Строго по конспекту» mode.** New checkbox under the conspectus field: the deck is assembled *only* from the teacher's own text, with the model forbidden to supplement it. Teachers reported decks where the model quietly invented definitions, examples and numbers that were nowhere in the material they supplied. Threaded end-to-end as `strict_source` → `GenerateParams.strictSource`, and applied in four places, because a single prompt line wasn't enough: a shared `STRICT_SOURCE_RULES` block injected into **both** the outline and expansion prompts (otherwise the plan promises slides the writer is then forbidden to fill), a reinforcing clause in both system prompts, a replacement speaker-notes spec (the default one demands "конкретный пример с реальными числами" and a typical misconception — a direct instruction to invent), and a relaxed outline slide count so a thin conspectus legitimately yields *fewer* slides instead of padded ones. `isStrictSource()` ignores the flag when no conspectus was supplied, so a stray checked box can't forbid the model from writing anything at all. Web grounding and RAG retrieval were already suppressed whenever `source_text` is present, so a strict deck is grounded solely in the teacher's text.

- **Presentations — denser default slide count, and the ceiling raised to 60.** Teachers reported decks coming out far too sparse: `estimateSlideCount()` was `minutes / 2`, i.e. 2 minutes per slide, so a 45-minute lecture defaulted to **23** slides where they expect 30–45 (their own figure is 1–1.5 min/slide). Now `minutes / MINUTES_PER_SLIDE` at 1.5 — 45 min → 30, 90 min → 60 — with the cap raised 50 → 60 so a 90-minute lecture's estimate is actually reachable rather than being clamped. `MINUTES_PER_SLIDE`, `MAX_SLIDE_COUNT` and `estimateSlideCount()` now live in `shared/types.ts` and are imported by the generator, the validator and the form, because the ceiling previously existed as three independent hardcoded `50`s that could drift. The slide-count field's placeholder now shows the live estimate ("Авто — 30") so the automatic number isn't a black box. Cost impact is negligible: measured against real usage logs, a 60-slide deck is ~$0.018 and ~2 minutes of generation.

### Changed
- **Dropped the "ИИ" abbreviation from user-facing copy across 21 files (marketing pages, admin/institution settings, help articles).** Explicit branding feedback: "ИИ" makes ИСПУМ read as a thin wrapper around a model the company doesn't even own, undersells the platform's non-AI-wrapper features, and doesn't match the roadmap (self-hosted models planned). Product-as-actor phrasing ("ИИ [verb]") became "ИСПУМ [verb]" or "Система [verb]"; provider/technology references ("ИИ-провайдер", "Модель ИИ") became "провайдер"/"модель" without the qualifier. The institution settings page at `/institution/model` was renamed "Модель для проверок" (was "Модель ИИ для проверок"), with its nav label, toast, and confirm-dialog copy updated to match, and the corresponding help article (`model-sovereignty` in `helpArticles.ts`) updated in lockstep so it doesn't point at a UI label that no longer exists. **Deliberately left untouched**: `pages/legal/*` (`Terms.tsx`, `Privacy.tsx`, `AcceptableUse.tsx`, `Ethics.tsx`, `legalDocs.ts`) — these describe a real named subprocessor (DeepSeek) and cross-border data transfer under 152-ФЗ; rewording binding legal disclosures isn't a copy-polish task and goes through the same review discipline as `docs/legal/security-overview.md` (TODO #16 item 6), not a branding pass. Sharpens the existing `feedback_no-ии-say-ispum` house rule, which previously carved out an exception for underlying-provider mentions — that exception is now gone.

### Fixed
- **`getLatestResourceSample()` / `getResourceSamplePeaks()` threw "integer out of range" on any machine with >2 GB free RAM (`db/queries/resourceSamples.ts`).** Found by the new CI integration job on its first run — a genuine production bug, not a test artifact. The `resource_samples` byte columns are `BIGINT` (correctly — they hold `process.memoryUsage().rss`, `os.freemem()`, `pg_database_size()`), but both read queries cast them to `::int`, which is **int4, max 2,147,483,647 ≈ 2.0 GiB**. `free_mem_bytes` crosses that on any host with more than ~2 GB free — i.e. the production VM in normal operation — so the AdminCapacity resource panel would fail rather than render. It escaped notice because macOS reports a small `os.freemem()` (the OS holds free memory as cache), so the suite passed locally and only failed on a 16 GB Linux runner: exactly the CI-vs-laptop skew the Node 20 / PG 15 pins exist to catch. Now `::float8`, which node-pg parses straight to a JS number (unlike `bigint`, which arrives as a string) and is exact for integers below 2^53 ≈ 9 PB, so the `number` typings stay honest. `sample_count`/`peak_conn` stay `::int` — a row count can't approach 2.1 billion. Same latent overflow fixed pre-emptively on `total_tokens` / `overhead_tokens` in `db/queries/usageRollup.ts` (BIGINT, not reachable today but the identical failure). Added a regression test that inserts an explicit 8 GiB value so it fails on macOS too, rather than only on Linux — verified to fail against the old `::int` casts and pass against the fix.
- **`docker compose up -d api` never actually ran — three separate deploys, no error, because `docker compose run --rm migrate` silently ate the rest of the deploy script as its own stdin.** [`deploy.sh`](deploy.sh). **Correction of the entry below** (same day): `--force-recreate` and a post-deploy image assertion were added first, on the theory that Compose's own change-detection was unreliable — that theory was wrong, and the very next deploy proved it: the fixed script still produced zero output for `docker compose up -d --force-recreate api` and its assertion, with `[7/8] nginx guard…` starting immediately after the migrate step's own output ended. Nothing errored; the commands simply never executed. Root cause: `docker compose run` (like plain `docker run`) forwards its **own stdin** into the container by default, even without a real TTY. Inside `ssh ... bash -s <<REMOTE`, the remote script's stdin *is* the rest of the heredoc — so `docker compose run --rm migrate`, run partway through that heredoc, was consuming every line after it (the `up -d`, the assertion, the prune) as data fed to the migrate container, and whatever the container didn't read before exiting was simply discarded, never reaching bash as commands. This is why the very first scripted deploy never created a container at all, and why the "clean" docs-only deploy left the old container running: the same silent consumption, every time, immediately after `docker compose run`. **Reproduced and verified the mechanism directly** (not inferred): `bash -s <<REMOTE / echo A / read stolen / echo B / echo C / REMOTE` — `read` (any stdin-consuming command) silently eats the following line, "B" never executes, only "A" and "C" print; redirecting that command's stdin from `/dev/null` fixes it completely, confirmed with the same reproduction. Fixed with `docker compose run --rm -T migrate < /dev/null` — `-T` disables TTY allocation (the documented flag for scripted use) and the explicit redirect makes stdin consumption physically impossible regardless of that flag's exact behaviour across Compose versions. `deploy/rollback.sh` was never exposed to this bug (it has no `docker compose run` call) — its `--force-recreate` + assertion, kept as defense-in-depth below, should already have been correct.
- **Post-incident hardening — retry-with-diagnostics health check, and a real rollback script (`deploy.sh`, new `deploy/rollback.sh`).** Two follow-ups from the container-cutover incident below. The `[8/8]` local-API health check now retries for up to 30s instead of firing once immediately (`docker compose up -d` returns once the container is *created*, not once Node has bound the port, so a single-shot check risked a false-negative on a legitimately slow cold start), and on genuine failure it dumps `docker logs ispum-api --tail=30` **inline** — today's incident took roughly ten back-and-forth messages to get from "curl failed" to "here's the actual crash reason"; that now happens automatically in the deploy's own output. `deploy/rollback.sh <tag>` finally implements the "rollback = repoint tag" promise from Track 1.3 — pulls and starts a previously-pushed image tag without rebuilding or re-running migrations (forward-only/additive by policy). A pre-flight "is port 3000 already in use" check was considered and deliberately rejected: with `network_mode: host`, the *previous* container is expected to be listening right up until `up -d` replaces it, so a naive check would false-positive on every normal second deploy, not just catch a bad one.
- **`deploy.sh`'s final health check could report "✅ Deploy complete" while the API was actually down — found live, during the first container cutover (`deploy.sh`).** `curl ... && echo "✓ ..."` looks like it gates the deploy under `set -e`, but it doesn't: POSIX explicitly exempts "any command of an AND-OR list other than the last" from `-e`, and `curl` here is never the last command in its list (`echo` is) — so a failing `curl` never aborted the script. This has been silently non-functional since before this session; nothing had exercised it until a genuine outage did. What actually happened live: the old PM2 process got restarted as an emergency fallback mid-incident and was never stopped again, silently holding port 3000 for hours and blocking every subsequent `docker compose up -d api` (the container's `network_mode: host` means both processes compete for the same port) — including the very first scripted cutover attempt, whose "success" this bug then falsely reported. Rewritten as explicit `if ! cmd; then exit 1; fi` — the one construct `set -e` genuinely does not trigger on, used here on purpose rather than by the accident the `&&` form was. Verified both directions in isolation (not just read): the old pattern exits 0 with a failing check inside it; the new one correctly propagates exit 1. **No code or infrastructure was actually broken by this incident** — the compose file, Dockerfile, and CI pipeline all worked correctly on the first real attempt once given a clear port; the entire incident was two processes contending for one port, now confirmed resolved: `curl https://ispum.ru/api/health` returns `1.5.0 (2026-08-15+b71fe95)`, the new container, serving traffic.
- **CI image push failing with "Cannot read manifest data" against Yandex Container Registry (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`).** First real push attempt after wiring the registry secrets: every layer pushed fine, but the final manifest push failed. `docker/build-push-action@v6` attaches a provenance/SBOM attestation manifest by default (buildx ≥0.11) — a multi-manifest OCI structure Docker Hub/GHCR understand but that Yandex Container Registry rejects outright, a known class of incompatibility with several non-Docker-Hub registries. Disabled explicitly (`provenance: false`, `sbom: false`) rather than relying on a registry-dependent default.
- **Two stale §7.10 domain-axis tests in `routes/domainAccess.integration.test.ts` — no production bug; both encoded intent that was deliberately reversed.** Surfaced by wiring the integration suite into CI (above); they had been failing against any fully migrated test DB. Diagnosed rather than skipped, since both look security-adjacent. **(1)** `a curriculum/edit grant at root reaches RPD monitor…` expected the РПД monitor to answer a `curriculum` grant, but the monitor was moved to its own `umu` domain (`routes/rpdMonitor.ts`, `docs/ACCESS-MATRIX.md`) precisely so a Заведующий кафедрой can author criteria without seeing institution-wide filing compliance — ACCESS-MATRIX lists «ЗК не видит Мониторинг РПД» as a *required* property, so the 403 was correct behaviour. Rewritten to assert criteria/rubrics 200 + РПД 403, and **split** so a companion test grants `umu:view` and asserts the monitor answers 200 — without it the 403 could pass for the wrong reason (a route 403-ing for everyone). **(2)** `the grant-role validation refuses an admin-level grant scoped to a non-all domain` encoded the Phase 1 rule that `role='admin'` always forced `domain='all'`. That rule was removed on 2026-07-29 *because it caused a real incident*: forcing `domain='all'` meant an admin grant on a `program` unit conferred institution-wide access. The guard was **inverted**, not dropped — `admin` now scales by subtree like edit/view, `curriculum:admin`/`umu:admin` are actively issued (РОП, РУМЦ), and `orgUnits.ts`'s `POST /roles` instead rejects `admin`+`domain='all'` on any non-root unit. Rewritten to assert both halves of the rule that actually exists: a domain-scoped admin grant at root is accepted (201), and `admin`+`all` on a department is refused (400). Also corrected the stale line in `CLAUDE.md` that still placed the РПД monitor on `curriculum` — the thing that made both tests look like real failures. Integration suite now green: 27 files / 200 tests.
- **Presentations — decks past ~44 slides had their outline silently truncated.** `outlineMaxTokens()` was capped at 4000 output tokens while the outline needs ~90 tokens/slide, so anything larger ran out of budget mid-array; `normaliseOutline()` then accepted the short result and forced its last entry to `summary`, so the teacher just got fewer slides than they asked for with no error anywhere. Reachable in production, since validation already permitted 50. Ceiling raised to the provider output cap (8192, the same constant `expansionBatchMaxTokens()` clamps to), which covers the whole supported range with headroom. Note this remains a single outline call, so ~82 slides is the real architectural wall — going beyond it needs the outline chunked across calls the way expansion already is, not a larger constant.
- **Image-only .docx uploads produced a hallucinated deck instead of an error (`services/documentExtractor.ts`).** Teachers often build a "conspectus" by pasting SCREENSHOTS of a textbook or handwritten notes into Word. The file is a valid .docx with a near-empty text layer, so `mammoth` (and the new OMML walker) returned essentially nothing, the generator saw "no material supplied", and wrote the whole deck from the topic string alone — invented content presented as the teacher's lecture. The DOCX branch now applies the same trigger the scanned-PDF branch has always used (`countRealWords() < 50`, now a shared helper rather than duplicated inline): too little real text → OCR the images embedded under `word/media` via Yandex Vision and merge the result with whatever genuine text layer existed. Only raster formats Vision can decode are sent (EMF/WMF vector art is skipped), images below 8 KB are skipped as icons/bullets/logos, and the run is capped at 40 images to bound worst-case cost on a pathological upload. The presentation form's extract-text endpoint now also names scans/screenshots explicitly when even OCR recovers nothing, instead of a generic failure.
- **PPTX export — "PowerPoint found a problem with content...[Repaired]" on every multi-slide deck.** Surfaced by the same teacher's export, unrelated to the formula-image work above (repro'd it on a plain 5-slide text-only deck too). Root cause: pptxgenjs 4.0.1's `createContentTypesXml()` writes one `<Override .../ppt/slideMasters/slideMasterN.xml>` entry per SLIDE (using the slide's index, not the actual master count), while ИСПУМ decks never call `defineSlideMaster()` and only ever have one real `slideMaster1.xml` — so `[Content_Types].xml` referenced `slideMaster2.xml`, `slideMaster3.xml`, etc. that don't exist in the zip, which is exactly what PowerPoint's strict validator flags and silently strips content over. `generatePresentationPptx()` now post-processes the written zip (`fixDanglingSlideMasterEntries`, using `jszip` — already a transitive dep of pptxgenjs, now a direct one) to drop the dangling Overrides before returning the buffer. New regression test unzips a real generated deck and asserts declared vs. actual `slideMasterN.xml` parts match.
- **PPTX export — "PowerPoint found a problem with content" caused by negative shape heights on formula slides.** A second, distinct cause of the same dialog, introduced by the formula-image work itself and only reachable on slides with several formulas (the earlier `slideMaster` packaging fix was real but not the whole story). A rendered formula image is up to 1.8" tall where the flattened text line it replaced was 0.7", so `addFormulaSlide()`'s layout cursor ran past the bottom of the 5.63" slide — four captioned formulas pushed it to 9.57" — and the explanation textbox below was then sized `SLIDE_H - y - 0.4`, i.e. negative (a real teacher's deck contained `cy="-3966630"`, about -4.34"). DrawingML requires cx/cy > 0, so PowerPoint treated the package as corrupt and offered to repair it. `addFormulaSlide()` now budgets the available vertical space across the formulas on the slide (reserving room for the explanation) instead of applying a flat per-formula cap, and every dynamically-computed height (formula slide + diagram slide) goes through a `clampH()` guard so a non-positive or non-finite extent can never reach the XML again. New regression test asserts the invariant across the whole generated package — no shape anywhere may have a non-positive extent — rather than just re-checking the one box that broke.
- **PPTX export filename was a run of underscores that grew with the topic length.** `routes/presentations.ts`'s export route sanitised the (Cyrillic) `presentation.topic` with `.replace(/[^\w.-]/g, '_')` — JS's `\w` only matches ASCII, so every Cyrillic character and space became `_`, turning e.g. "Осредненные уравнения..." into `_________....pptx`. Now sends an ASCII `filename="presentation.pptx"` fallback plus the real UTF-8 name via the RFC 5987 `filename*=UTF-8''...` parameter, the same pattern already used for program document downloads in `routes/programs.ts`.


- **Feature AL — usage rollup scheduler (`services/usageRollup.ts`'s `startUsageRollupScheduler`, wired into `index.ts`).** Closes a gap surfaced live on the VM: `usage_rollup_monthly`/`institution_rollup_monthly` are snapshots (`getCapacityOverview` reads them directly), not a live query, so `AdminCapacity`'s tier/margin numbers only ever advanced when someone remembered to re-run `npm run rollup:backfill`. Every 6h now recomputes current + previous UTC month (idempotent upsert; previous month catches usage landing just before a calendar boundary). PM2-worker-0-gated, same precedent as `renewals.ts`/`resourceSampler.ts`; a failed run logs and retries next tick rather than crashing the process. `rollup:backfill` remains necessary once per new environment (cold-starting the VM/production DB, which is entirely separate from dev) — the scheduler only keeps already-backfilled data current, it doesn't replace the first run. 1 new unit test (`monthsToRefresh`).

- **Feature AL Phase 4 — per-feature spend cap + in-process resource sampler (TODO.md). All five phases of Feature AL are now shipped.** The last two pieces: a guardrail scoped to the correct blast radius, and the first-ever visibility into process memory and peak (not just live-snapshot) resource usage.
  - **Per-feature spend cap** (`services/featureSpendCap.ts`) — the third variant of a pattern that already existed twice (`spendCap.ts` per-teacher monthly, `globalSpendCap.ts` platform daily). Checked at the same choke point as both (`llm/registry.ts`'s `chat`/`chatJSON`). **Variant-aware**: `FEATURE_SPEND_CAP_PRESENTATION_DEEP_USD` is checked on top of (not instead of) `FEATURE_SPEND_CAP_PRESENTATION_USD` — a deep-mode-specific ceiling can trip while standard-depth presentations and every other feature keep working. Directly motivated by "imagine deep mode becomes very popular; I wouldn't want the numbers to sink the whole business" — today's `globalSpendCap.ts` tripping would take grading down along with whatever actually ran away; this gives the correct blast radius. Both env-var-gated and disabled (`Infinity`) by default, same posture as `globalSpendCap.ts`. New `FeatureSpendCapExceededError` (503).
  - **`resource_samples`** (migration 108) — an in-process sampler on PM2 worker 0 (same gating precedent as `services/renewals.ts`), ~60s interval: `process.memoryUsage()` RSS/heap (previously **zero** visibility into process memory anywhere in ИСПУМ), `os.loadavg()`, `os.freemem()`, `pg_database_size()`, `pg_stat_activity` connection count, and the pgvector-reindex-gating row count (reuses `db/queries/capacity.ts`'s existing live-read functions unchanged). Self-prunes to a 30-day retention window every tick — a cheap indexed range delete, no separate cleanup job needed at ~43,200 rows/month. `getResourceSamplePeaks()` reports the PEAK over a trailing window, not the mean — catching the worst moment is the entire point of sampling, something Phase 2's headroom model had no choice but to approximate with a single live snapshot before this existed. Deliberately backend-only in this pass, matching the same "ship the substrate, then the page" sequencing Phase 1→2 already used — `AdminCapacity` isn't wired to it yet; the natural follow-up is showing peak-24h connections instead of today's live snapshot.
  - **Deliberately did not integrate the Yandex Cloud Monitoring API** — it's blind to every constraint that actually binds this platform (DB pool exhaustion, pgvector row counts, per-worker rate-limit state, provider account health), and Yandex's own console already shows CPU/RAM for free. The high-value metrics are the ones only visible from inside the app.
  - 6 unit tests (`featureSpendCap.test.ts`) + 6 integration tests (`featureSpendCap.integration.test.ts`, including the case proving a variant-specific cap trips independently of the broader feature-level one) + 5 integration tests (`resourceSampler.integration.test.ts` — real measurement, peak-not-mean aggregation, retention pruning). Verified live against the real dev server: the sampler auto-started on the already-running `tsx watch` process with no manual restart, and had written 5 real samples within 5 minutes — values consistent with Phase 2/3's own live readings from earlier the same session (`embedded_assignments=23` matching exactly, `db_size≈29MB` matching the ~30MB reading taken earlier).

- **Feature AL Phase 3 — capacity report refinement + provider ceilings (TODO.md).** Corrects Phase 2's biggest acknowledged gap (mean-based headroom estimates for a concurrency-bound resource) and separates the three ways a DeepSeek account can actually fail, since they need different mitigations.
  - **Peak-to-mean ratio** — `services/providerCeilings.ts`, derived from hourly `api_usage_log.created_at` buckets over a trailing 30-day window (mean computed across every hour in the window, including silent overnight ones, not just active hours — that would understate the ratio). Wired into `capacityModel.ts`'s `db_connections` headroom row as a new `breaksAtTeachersPeakAdjusted` field, shown alongside the original mean-based estimate on `AdminCapacity` rather than replacing it — this fulfils the note Phase 2 explicitly left as "Phase 3 will refine." Not applied to `pgvector`/`db_size`, which are cumulative totals rather than concurrency-bound.
  - **Rate-limit (429) ceiling, derived empirically rather than guessed.** `computeRateLimitKnee()` brackets the real ceiling between the largest hourly DeepSeek volume that stayed clean and the smallest that actually tripped a 429. When production has never been rate-limited in the window (true for this platform today), reports that explicitly instead of fabricating a number — TODO.md's own framing ("if production has never rate-limited there's no knee to find yet") is now the page's literal empty-state copy.
  - **Balance (402) burn rate + pool-depth historical proxy**, keyed off the `account` column Phase 0 added: $/day burn rate per account (real cost, not a balance we don't track — ИСПУМ has no visibility into an account's actual remaining credit, only DeepSeek's own dashboard does, so this is presented as a top-up-cadence *input*, not a "days until broke" prediction), 402/failure counts, last success/failure timestamps. Pool depth is explicitly labelled a **historical proxy, not live state** — `llm/deepseek.ts`'s `downUntil` cooldown map is in-memory and per-PM2-worker, confirmed not centrally queryable, so "possibly unhealthy" means "last event was a failure with no success since," not "currently cooling down."
  - **Yandex embed single point of failure** recorded as a static risk note on the page, not a metric — invariant #9 forces all embeddings through Yandex and `llm/yandex.ts` has no multi-account pool (DeepSeek got one after a real 402 incident; Yandex has the same exposure and no mitigation, and can't fail over to another provider by architectural design).
  - New `db/queries/providerCeilings.ts` (3 queries, all against `api_usage_log`'s existing columns — no new tables) + `services/providerCeilings.ts`. Folded into the existing `GET /api/admin/capacity/overview` response rather than a second endpoint; rendered as a new "Провайдеры и пиковая нагрузка" section on `AdminCapacity`, shared identically between operator and investor modes.
  - 11 unit tests (`providerCeilings.test.ts`) + 7 integration tests (`providerCeilings.integration.test.ts`, against a real Postgres DB). Verified live against the real dev database: peak-to-mean computed a genuine 248.1× ratio (extreme, but mathematically correct — this platform's dev traffic is low-volume and concentrated in short testing bursts, exactly the kind of noise a young product's capacity numbers should show honestly rather than smooth over), the 429 knee correctly reported "not observed" with its bracket, and the accounts table correctly showed its own honest empty state (no `account`-tagged rows exist yet in dev — that column only started being populated after Phase 0 shipped).

- **Feature AL Phase 2 — the `AdminCapacity` page (TODO.md).** Phase 1's rollup tables finally get a UI, plus a headroom model for the infrastructure side. Reuses `AdminLayout` — same table/card conventions as `AdminUsage`/`AdminInstitutions`, no new design language.
  - **Two framings, one data layer**: an operator mode (real institution names, dense) and an investor mode (institutions pseudonymised as "Организация #N" by default, revealed via an explicit checkbox — 152-ФЗ, teachers are natural persons and this page is shown to outside parties). A scenario input ("сценарий: активных преподавателей") live-recomputes every projection on the page.
  - **`services/capacityModel.ts`** — the headroom engine, deliberately scoped to what's honestly computable today: `pg_database_size`, `pg_stat_activity` connection count, and embedded-`assignments` row count (the pgvector reindex trigger — 50,000 rows, `docs/scaling.md`'s real number, correcting an imprecise "≈10k" paraphrase this doc originally carried). `computeBreaksAtTeachers()` projects each linearly (coefficient × scenario vs. ceiling) — explicitly a MEAN-based estimate, not a peak one, and the DB-connections row says so in its own UI note (peak-to-mean correction is Phase 3). DB size carries no ceiling at all — disk % isn't queryable from inside Postgres, that's Phase 4's resource sampler — and renders as informational only rather than a fabricated threshold.
  - **Unit economics rendered**: the tier-distribution table (n/mean/p50/p95/max) and per-institution seats/utilization/cost/revenue/margin/cost-per-seat, in both modes.
  - **Fixed/variable cost split** (investor mode) — variable cost/teacher computed live from the rollup; fixed infra cost reads a new `MONTHLY_INFRA_COST_USD` env var, honestly showing "—" with a "не задан" note when unset instead of a guessed number, matching the Yandex-pricing-placeholder precedent from Improvement #13.
  - New backend route `GET /api/admin/capacity/overview` (`routes/adminCapacity.ts`), one call per page load rather than fragmenting into per-section requests.
  - 13 unit tests (`capacityModel.test.ts` — pure distribution/outlier/headroom-projection math) + 4 integration tests (`capacityModel.integration.test.ts`, against a real Postgres DB). Verified live end-to-end in the browser against the real dev database: both modes render numbers matching the Phase 1 CLI report exactly; the pseudonymisation toggle correctly swaps the real institution name for "Организация #1" and back; the scenario input live-recomputes every projected value (verified at N=500: pgvector 23→11,500 rows, connections 6→3,000 — correctly shown blowing past the 50-connection ceiling, the honest signal a real capacity page should give, not a smoothed-over one).

- **Feature AL Phase 1 — unit economics: cost/revenue rollup tables + two CLI reports (TODO.md).** Backend + CLI only, deliberately no page yet — Phase 2 is where this becomes `AdminCapacity`. Answers the two questions Phase 0's true ledger made askable: does a free/pro teacher cost more than they pay, and is an institution contract profitable.
  - **`usage_rollup_monthly`** (migration 106) — one row per (month, teacher_id): effective tier, institution snapshot, calls, tokens, cost, amortized personal-payment revenue, frozen FX rate + date. Teacher-month grain, not a single pre-aggregated row per tier, because percentiles (the number that actually matters for freemium — the p95 free user, not the mean) can only be computed from per-teacher figures.
  - **`institution_rollup_monthly`** (migration 107) — one row per (month, institution_id): active seats, seats purchased (from `institution_contracts`), overhead cost, amortized institution revenue, frozen FX rate. Active seats = distinct teachers with usage that institution that month — a leading churn indicator and the input to a net-revenue-retention story.
  - **`services/usageRollup.ts`** — the ETL, `computeUsageRollupForMonth(month)`. Uses `lib/planTier.ts`'s `computeEffectiveTier` (not raw `teachers.plan_tier`, which misclassifies every institution member and lapsed Pro subscriber). `amortizedRevenueForMonthRub()` spreads a `pro_annual` payment across the 12 calendar months starting at `confirmed_at` (`pro_monthly` stays un-amortized, it's already a one-month unit) — pure function, unit tested against the exact "fake January profit spike, then nothing" bug a naive sum-by-confirmed-month would produce. `OVERHEAD_FEATURES = ['rpd_reminder']` routes that feature's cost to institution overhead instead of the triggering teacher (the УМЦ/УМУ head who runs Мониторинг РПД benefits the whole institution, not personally) — `cohortSynthesis.ts` and institution-pool RAG retrieval are conceptually the same kind of shared work but log as `feature: 'grading'`, indistinguishable from real per-student grading; **documented as a known gap**, not silently misattributed and hidden.
  - **Platform admins excluded** from every aggregate (`teachers.is_platform_admin = TRUE`) — the only DB-level signal available; ad-hoc test-teacher accounts without that flag can't be automatically filtered, a residual gap for any backfilled month containing heavy founder testing (like the two months this feature itself was built in).
  - **Two new CLI scripts**, `npm run rollup:backfill` (computes/recomputes the last N months, default 2, idempotent upsert) and `npm run rollup:report` (prints cost distribution per effective tier — n/mean/p50/p95/max, plus "how many free teachers cost more than $1/$3/$5" — and per-institution seats/utilization/cost/revenue/margin/cost-per-seat). Same script-first pattern as `evalPresentations.ts`; labels "tracking since &lt;month&gt;" and warns explicitly below 3 rolled-up months, since a 2-point line presented as a trend is worse than no line at all.
  - **Two more `pg` driver default-type gotchas found and fixed**, same class as Phase 0's NUMERIC/DATE surprises: `BIGINT` columns (`total_tokens`) come back from `pg` as a JS string, not a number — caught immediately by a unit test asserting `.toBe(2500)` against `"2500"`, fixed by casting to `::int` in every read query (token counts never approach actual bigint range here). Also: both new CLI scripts were hanging well past their own logic finishing — an open `pg.Pool` keeps Node's event loop alive indefinitely, so neither exited on its own. Fixed by adding explicit `process.exit(0)` on the success path, matching the convention `evalPresentations.ts` already established.
  - 10 unit tests (`usageRollup.test.ts` — pure amortization/percentile/overhead-classification logic) + 9 integration tests (`usageRollup.integration.test.ts`, against a real Postgres DB — idempotent upsert, platform-admin exclusion, overhead-cost routing, effective-tier computation, seat utilization, missing-contract null-handling). Verified live end-to-end against the real dev database: backfilled the last 2 real months, then ran the report — it printed a correct institution margin computed from the real test contract created during Phase 0's own live browser verification (1,200,000₽/year → $1,260/mo at the day's ЦБ РФ rate, against $0.07 of real measured usage cost).

- **Feature AL Phase 0 — make the usage ledger true enough to build a capacity/margin dashboard on (TODO.md).** Designed 2026-07-29 out of the Feature AG scaling conversation ("will the system hold at 1000 concurrent presentations?"); this phase is the prerequisite every later phase (unit economics, capacity headroom) depends on, so it shipped as one unit rather than being sliced further.
  - **`variant` and `account` columns on `api_usage_log`** (migration 104). `variant` tags presentation depth (`'standard'|'deep'`) — flows through the existing `CallContext`/`...opts.context` spread in every `LLMProvider` with zero per-provider code changes, just a type addition — deliberately a column, not a new `feature` value, since `getDailyUsage` and friends filter on `feature = 'presentation'` and a `'presentation_deep'` value would silently split that aggregate everywhere it's read. `account` tags which DeepSeek account (of up to 5, see the 2026-07-24 multi-account incident) actually served the call, so a 402/429 burst is now attributable to a specific account instead of only visible in aggregate. Fixed a related gap while wiring this: `services/presentations.ts`'s outline and expansion `chatJSON` calls were each building their own inline `{ teacherId, feature: 'presentation' }` context literal instead of reusing the function's own `context` variable — meaning `institutionId` (and now `variant`) was silently missing from those two specific calls. 2 new tests in `deepseek.test.ts`.
  - **Fixed `activeThisWeek`** — `routes/admin.ts`'s `/overview` was counting `teachers.created_at >= NOW() - INTERVAL '7 days'`, i.e. signups this week, not active users (`newThisMonth` was the same metric on a wider window — that was the tell). Now `COALESCE(last_seen_at, created_at)`, matching the convention `db/queries/activation.ts` already uses — `last_seen_at` has existed since migration 073 and is touched on every authenticated request. This was the single most load-bearing fix in the phase: every coefficient a capacity/margin view would compute divides by this number, and showing an investor "N active teachers" backed by a signup count is a diligence risk, not just untidy data.
  - **`institution_contracts`** (migration 105) — the platform's first record of institution revenue. `payments` is `teacher_id NOT NULL` (plans `'pro_monthly'|'pro_annual'` only) and `institutions` has just `name`/`plan_tier`/`max_teachers` (a licensing cap, not a price) — institution deals are negotiated offline via 44-ФЗ procurement, so a manual record is the correct model here, not a payment integration. One row per contract term (`annual_value_rub`, `seats_purchased`, `term_start`, `term_end`, `notes`, `created_by`), so a renewed/renegotiated institution has real history rather than one row being overwritten. New `db/queries/institutionContracts.ts` (list/current-term-lookup/create/update/delete) + routes on `routes/admin.ts` + a platform-admin UI panel on `AdminInstitutions.tsx` — a second expandable-row toggle ("Контракт") alongside the existing SAML config panel, same interaction pattern; the contract whose term covers today shows a "действует" badge. 12 integration tests against a real Postgres DB, including the CHECK constraints (term end after start, positive seats) and cascade-delete-with-institution behavior.
