  - **Two bugs caught during implementation, both about trusting the `pg` driver's default type mapping instead of verifying it**: `annual_value_rub` (NUMERIC) is actually parsed to a real JS `number` by `connection.ts`'s existing global type parser (OID 1700) — the first draft's doc comment claiming it "comes back as a string" was simply wrong, carried over from habit rather than checked. And `term_start`/`term_end` (DATE) come back from `pg` as a JS `Date` at UTC midnight by default — a well-known one-`.toLocaleDateString()`-away timezone display bug for a date-only field — fixed by explicitly casting both to `'YYYY-MM-DD'` text in every query (`to_char(...)`) rather than leaving them as an aspirational `string` type that wasn't true at runtime. Caught immediately by the integration tests, not shipped.
  - **Also fixed a real, pre-existing layout bug the new table column exposed**: `AdminInstitutions.tsx`'s table wrapper used `overflow-hidden` instead of `overflow-x-auto` — the institutions table was already wider than its `max-w-4xl` container before this change, silently clipping (not just visually truncating) the SSO column and its "Настроить" button on any viewport narrower than the table's natural width. Adding the Контракт column made the clipping worse and surfaced it during live verification; fixed for both columns, not just the new one.
  - Verified live end-to-end against a real dev database (not just tests): logged in as platform admin, opened the Контракт panel, created a real contract (1,200,000₽/year, 200 seats, 2026 term), confirmed it rendered with the correct amount formatting, date range, and "действует" badge, confirmed the underlying `POST` returned 201 and the list `GET` returned 200. Also caught and fixed en route: the new migrations (103–105) had only been applied to the integration test database (`gradeassist_test`) via `test:integration:setup`, not the local dev database the running server actually reads from — a reminder that `npm run migrate` against dev is a separate, easy-to-forget step from the test-DB setup script.
  - All 55 backend test files (683 tests), all 5 frontend test files (41 tests), `tsc --noEmit` on both — all passing. The 2 pre-existing failures in `domainAccess.integration.test.ts` were confirmed (via `git stash`) to predate this session and are unrelated to this change.

- **`api_usage_log.cost_usd` now reflects real platform AI cost — every Yandex-billed call is priced instead of logged at `costUsd:0` or not logged at all (TODO.md Improvement #13, found while designing Feature AL).** Before this, only DeepSeek and Qwen calls carried a real cost; `llm/yandex.ts`'s chat path hardcoded `costUsd:0` with a `// cost tracking TBD` comment, and `embeddings.ts`/`yandexVision.ts`/`yandexImages.ts`/`yandexSearch.ts` never wrote a usage row at all — meaning the entire RAG substrate (invariant #9 routes every embedding through Yandex) and Feature AG Phase 2/3a's auto-image search + web grounding calls cost real money the ledger reported as zero. Both `spendCap.ts` (per-teacher) and `globalSpendCap.ts` (platform-wide) sum `cost_usd`, so both circuit breakers were structurally blind to this spend.
  - **New `services/fxRate.ts`** — fetches the daily USD/RUB reference rate from **ЦБ РФ** (`cbr.ru`'s public XML endpoint, the canonical Russian accounting source), cached 24h, `AbortController` + 8s timeout, fail-open to the last known rate (or a conservative `FALLBACK_RATE=100` if never once reachable) — an FX lookup must never break a grading or presentation call. `parseCbrUsdRate()`/`rubToUsd()` are pure and unit-tested (8 tests in `fxRate.test.ts`).
  - **`api_usage_log` gained `cost_native` / `currency` / `fx_rate_used`** (migration 103) — purely additive, so every existing query (`getDailyUsage`, `getTodayCost`, both spend caps) keeps reading `cost_usd` unchanged. DeepSeek/Qwen rows now also populate `currency:'USD'`/`cost_native` (equal to `cost_usd`, no conversion) for schema consistency, so a row's currency is never ambiguously `NULL` going forward.
  - **New Yandex pricing in `config/planLimits.ts`, confirmed same-day against the real Yandex AI Studio console pricing tables** (`calculateYandexChatCostRub`/`EmbedCostRub`/`VisionCostRub`/`WebSearchCostRub`/`ImageSearchCostRub`, each backed by a `YANDEX_RATE_*` env var — **every rate is now a real confirmed price, no placeholders remain**). Corrections the console tables forced versus the first placeholder pass: **chat and image search were both far more expensive than guessed** — chat landed on the non-Flash "Alice AI LLM" synchronous tier (500₽/1M in, 1200₽/1M out — `CHAT_MODEL='yandexgpt'` in `llm/yandex.ts` has no `-lite` suffix, the pre-rebrand naming for the standard/pro tier, not Flash) — and **web search and image search turned out to be different Cloud Search API products priced ~30× apart** (0.0305₽/call async web vs 0.915₽/call sync image search), previously conflated into one `searchPerCall` constant, understating every image-search cost logged before this fix. Vision OCR corrected downward to the real "Распознавание печатного текста" rate (0.1321₽/page, was guessed at 0.9). Embeddings corrected to 10.1₽/1M tokens ("Получение эмбеддингов текста", VAT-inclusive) — briefly priced in USD off an AI Studio pricing-assistant answer mid-session, reverted once the static console table (the more authoritative of the two sources, matching every other product here) turned up the real ₽ figure, keeping every Yandex product on the same ₽/FX basis rather than one oddly USD-denominated outlier. 7 tests in `planLimits.test.ts`.
  - **Every Yandex call site now costs out**: `llm/yandex.ts`'s `chat()`/`embed()`; `services/yandexVision.ts`'s `yandexVisionOCR()` (one row per document, billed by page count, threaded through `documentExtractor.ts`'s `extractText()`); `services/yandexImages.ts`'s `yandexImageSearch()`; `services/yandexSearch.ts`'s `webSearch()`. All take an optional `CallContext` — a caller that doesn't pass one simply stays uninstrumented (same posture as every other `if (context)` fire-and-forget site in this codebase), rather than the feature breaking.
  - **Wired at the call sites that carry teacher/institution context**: presentation auto-image search + web grounding (`services/presentations.ts`), the manual image-search route (`routes/presentations.ts`), grading's citation web-search (`citationChecker.ts`), topic generation's grounding search (`topics.ts`), document upload OCR (`services/documents.ts`), РПД upload OCR (`services/rpdSubmissions.ts`), and post-approval embedding (`services/embeddings.ts`, called from `grading.ts`'s `approve()`). New `document_extraction` feature bucket (`services/llm/types.ts`) so OCR cost shows up distinctly in the existing by-feature admin breakdown rather than folded into something else.
  - **Known residual gap, not done in this pass**: the institution-admin bulk-import OCR paths (`routes/programs.ts`, `routes/adminFgos.ts`, `services/institutionStrategyDoc.ts`) don't pass a `CallContext` yet, so document extraction on those specific routes stays uninstrumented — lower volume than the teacher-facing upload paths, and institution-admin document imports are arguably overhead cost rather than per-teacher cost anyway. `extractText()`'s `context` param is already there for whoever picks this up.
  - **Fixed a latency bug found during implementation**: the first draft of every cost-logging call awaited `getUsdRubRate()` (and therefore `createUsageLog()`) directly in the response path — on a cache miss that's a real network round-trip blocking the actual OCR/search/chat result, violating the same "fire-and-forget, never block a user-facing response" rule `createUsageLog`'s own doc comment states. Restructured every call site (`logYandexChatUsage`/`logYandexEmbedUsage` in `yandex.ts`, `logVisionUsage`, `logSearchUsage`, `logWebSearchUsage`) to run un-awaited, consistent with every other usage-logging call in the codebase.
  - All 55 backend test files (680 tests), `tsc --noEmit` — all passing.

- **Phase 3 + two gaps for presentation generation (TODO.md Feature AG) — grounding, eval harness, "add image to any slide" affordance, and non-diagram images in PPTX.**
  - **Phase 3a — web-search grounding when RAG is thin or absent.** New pure function `shouldUseWebGrounding()` in `services/presentations.ts`: grounds when there's no `source_text` AND the course either has no RAG chunks (`hasAnyChunksForCourse()` — new `db/queries/chunks.ts` helper) or no `courseId`. When true, `fetchWebGrounding()` calls `services/yandexSearch.ts`'s existing `webSearch` (previously wired in topics/curriculum analysis but not here), picks `WEB_GROUNDING_RESULTS=5` results, and feeds them into both the outline call (`buildOutlinePrompt`) and every expansion batch (`buildExpansionPrompt`) via `renderWebGroundingBlock()`. Best-effort only: a search failure leaves grounding empty rather than bubbling an error — the same fail-open posture as RAG retrieval. Six new `presentations.test.ts` cases covering the decision function and deduplication.
  - **Phase 3b — eval harness.** New `services/presentationEvalHarness.ts`: `scoreSlides(slides, depth)` pure metric function (unit-testable, no DB calls), tracking `avgNotesWordCount`, `minNotesWordCount` (the Phase 1 regression guard — if expansion is good, no slide except the title should be below target), `notesBelowTargetShare`, `bulletsShare`, `typeDistribution`, `imageCoverageAmongEligible` (title + summary excluded from eligibility), and `citedSlideShare`. `runPresentationEval(topics, onProgress?)` is the orchestrator: runs `generatePresentation` under `EVAL_CONCURRENCY=2`, catches per-topic failures without stopping remaining topics, and aggregates scores. New CLI at `scripts/evalPresentations.ts` with 8 default Russian engineering + humanities topics; `npm run eval:presentations` (added to `backend/package.json`). 13 tests in `presentationEvalHarness.test.ts`.
  - **Gap 1 — "Add image" entry point for any slide.** `SlideContent.tsx` now shows a `+ Добавить изображение` link below any non-title/summary slide that has neither an auto-generated `image_query` nor a teacher-picked `image`. Opening it launches `SlideImagePicker` with an empty query, so the teacher can search and attach a visual to any slide the model judged "no image needed" — the backend route has always accepted this override; the frontend just wasn't surfacing it.
  - **Gap 2 — PPTX export embeds non-diagram slide images.** `services/presentationExport.ts` gained two new helpers: `contentRegion(hasImage)` returns `{ x, w }` for the text region, narrowed from `10"` → `7"` when a right-column image is present (`SIDE_IMAGE_W=3.0"`, `SIDE_IMAGE_GAP=0.3"` right-aligned); `addSideImage(pptxSlide, image)` fetches the image URL via the existing `fetchImageAsDataUri`, embeds it as a `3" × 3"` right-column element, or draws a labelled dashed-border placeholder on any fetch failure. Five slide types now support this layout — bullets, concept, formula, comparison, discussion — and call `await addSideImage(s, slide.image)` after laying out their body content. Title and summary slides carry no `image` field and are unchanged. 17-test suite `presentationExport.test.ts` still passes with no new mocking required (existing `fetch` mock in that suite covers `fetchImageAsDataUri`).
  - All 53 backend test files (666 tests), 5 frontend test files (41 tests), `tsc --noEmit` on both — all passing.

- **Auto-images for presentation generation (TODO.md Feature AG Phase 2) — images stop being manual-only.** Before this, `image`/`image_query` lived only on `DiagramSlide.body`, always started `null`, and got filled in only if the teacher opened the picker and searched by hand — the other 7 slide types couldn't carry an image at all. `shared/types.ts`'s `SlideBase` now carries optional `image_query`/`image` fields inherited by every slide type; `DiagramSlide` deliberately keeps its own `body.image_query`/`body.image` untouched rather than migrating them up, since every already-persisted diagram slide is stored as JSONB in the old shape — moving the fields would silently orphan every existing teacher's already-picked images.
  - **Generation now searches automatically.** `services/presentations.ts`'s expansion prompt (`buildExpansionPrompt`) gained an instruction for an optional top-level `image_query` on any non-title/summary/diagram slide "when a visual meaningfully helps — most slides should NOT have one"; diagram's own instruction was tightened toward "object + technical word (разрез/схема/чертёж), not the lecture topic." New `autoFillImages()` runs after all expansion batches complete: bounded-concurrency (`IMAGE_SEARCH_CONCURRENCY=3`) `yandexImageSearch` calls for every slide with a query (including diagram, previously always left `null`), best-effort (a search failure just leaves that slide's slot empty — same degrade-gracefully posture as RAG retrieval), capped at `MAX_AUTO_IMAGES=20` for a very large deck.
  - **Cheap ranking in `yandexImages.ts`.** New `rankImageCandidates()`: drops results under 500px when both dimensions are known (unmeasured thumbnails are kept, not penalised for missing metadata), and stable-sorts known stock-photo hosts (shutterstock/istockphoto/gettyimages/etc.) to the bottom instead of dropping them — sometimes stock is genuinely the best result, it just shouldn't win by default.
  - **Backend routes relaxed from diagram-only to any slide.** `routes/presentations.ts`'s image-search endpoint now resolves the right query via new `getSlideImageQuery()` regardless of type, and accepts a teacher-supplied override even for a slide the model suggested no image for (add an image to any slide manually). `db/queries/presentations.ts`'s `setSlideImage` (and the mirrored write in `SlideContent.tsx`'s `handleImageChange`) now branch on type: diagram writes `body.image`, everything else writes the top-level field.
  - **Frontend**: `SlideContent.tsx`'s diagram-only image UI extracted into a shared `SlideImageBlock`, now also rendered below any other slide type that carries `image_query`/`image`. `slideText.tsx`/`slideHtml.ts` copy-to-clipboard renderers updated to include the same top-level image line.
  - **Known gap, not done in this pass**: the empty-image UI slot only appears on a slide the model already suggested a query for — there's no "+ add image" entry point yet for a slide it didn't, even though the backend route now supports that override. Also, per Phase 0/1's existing scope note, PPTX export (`presentationExport.ts`) still only embeds diagram images — the other 5 slide layouts each have bespoke fixed-position math that a supplementary image would need designed in, not just wired through; left as a follow-up.
  - Verified live end-to-end against the dev stack (real DeepSeek + Yandex Images calls, not just tests): a generated deck's diagram slide came back with a real auto-picked schematic (`fs.znanio.ru`) with a working "Заменить" swap, and non-diagram slides correctly stayed image-free where the model judged no visual was needed.
  - 17 new tests (`yandexImages.test.ts`'s `rankImageCandidates`, `presentations.test.ts`'s `getSlideImageQuery`/`withSlideImage`/`autoFillImages`).
- **Presentation generation rewritten as outline + parallel expansion (TODO.md Feature AG Phase 1) — the actual fix for teacher feedback that ungrounded decks were shallow.** The old single `chatJSON` call budgeted the whole deck in one completion (~220 tokens/slide including JSON overhead) — nowhere near the ≥1min30s-of-speech-per-slide teachers asked for, and RAG fed the prompt only 6 chunks truncated to 280 chars each. `services/presentations.ts`'s `generatePresentation` now runs two passes: a cheap outline call (`buildOutlinePrompt`) decides slide count/order/type + a one-line content brief per slide (no RAG, no full writing yet), then parallel expansion calls (`buildExpansionPrompt`, `EXPANSION_BATCH_SIZE=5` slides/call, `EXPANSION_CONCURRENCY=3`, via a local `mapWithConcurrency` mirroring `longReview.ts`'s) each write the full body + speaker-script notes for their batch with their own full token budget (`expansionBatchMaxTokens`: ~700 tokens/slide standard, ~1000 deep — replacing the now-removed `presentationMaxTokens`, whose whole-deck-in-one-call premise no longer applies). Type is decided once by the outline and never re-chosen during expansion, shrinking the expansion prompt's instruction surface (and error space) versus the old combined "choose type AND write it" prompt.
  - **RAG retrieval moved from once-per-deck to once-per-expansion-batch**, queried on that batch's own slide titles/briefs instead of the whole topic, and the prompt now gets each chunk's full untruncated text (`SOURCE_EXCERPT_LEN`'s 280-char truncation is now only applied to the persisted popover excerpt, not the generation prompt). New `SourcePool` (`createSourcePool`) dedupes chunks retrieved by more than one batch — common for adjacent slides on related material — keyed by `document_id:chunk_index`, handing out one stable idx per distinct chunk so `[N]` citation markers stay consistent across a deck whose sources were actually gathered in pieces. `MAX_SOURCES_PER_BATCH=4` (was a single `MAX_SOURCES=6` for the entire deck — batching multiplies total retrieved sources per deck even though the per-call limit is smaller).
  - **New depth selector** (`PresentationDepth`, `'standard' | 'deep'`, `shared/types.ts`): `standard` (default, every tier) targets 180–220 words of speaker notes per slide (~1min30s, the floor the feedback named); `deep` targets 260–320 words and is gated Pro+ via a new `presentationDeepMode` plan flag (`planLimits.ts`, wired through `routes/auth.ts`'s `features` payload and `routes/presentations.ts`'s `buildGenerateParams` — same silent-downgrade-not-403 convention as grading's `thorough`/`checkCitations`). `PresentationForm.tsx` gained a two-button toggle; picking "Углублённая" on a free plan opens the upgrade modal instead of silently no-op'ing, mirroring `GradingForm.tsx`'s thorough-mode gate.
  - **`slide_count_target`'s validated ceiling raised from 30 to 50** (`presentationValidation.ts`, `estimateSlideCount`, `PresentationForm.tsx`) — the old cap existed only because the whole deck had to fit one 8192-token completion (the 2026-07-15 incident this file used to document); batching removes that wall, so the ceiling is now a product/cost decision rather than a technical one.
  - New speaker-notes structure baked into the expansion prompt: why-it-matters → explanation → concrete example with real numbers → common misconception → transition — replacing the old "2–4 sentences, at least one hook" instruction, which was itself part of why notes came back thin.
  - 27 tests in `presentations.test.ts` (was 16): `outlineMaxTokens`/`expansionBatchMaxTokens` sizing, `normaliseOutline`'s defensive coercion (unknown-type fallback, forced first/last type, empty-outline shell, runaway-array ceiling), `chunkArray`, and `SourcePool` dedup/cross-document/full-text behavior. `filterCitations` tests unchanged.
  - **Not yet done — Phase 2/3 of Feature AG remain**: images are still `null` at generation (only manually picked afterward via the existing picker), and there's no eval harness yet to validate that "depth" actually reads as deeper rather than just longer.
- **Presentation generation moved off the request thread onto pg-boss (Phase 0 of a larger depth/images rework — see TODO.md Feature AG).** Prompted by teacher feedback that generated decks were shallow (speaker notes too short to talk from) and had almost no images for engineering content; before improving depth (which multiplies LLM calls per deck ~5x in the planned Phase 1) the generation path itself needed to survive concurrent load, since `POST /api/presentations/generate` ran `generatePresentation()` synchronously inline with nothing bounding how many could run at once — a burst risked OOM on the 2GB app VM and cascading retries from clients hitting their own HTTP timeout while the backend kept working. New `presentation_jobs` table (migration 102, mirrors `grade_jobs`) + `services/presentationJobWorker.ts`: the route now enqueues (`POST /api/presentations/generate-jobs`, 202) and the client polls (`GET .../generate-jobs/:id`), same shape as grading's `/grade-jobs`. The old synchronous `POST /generate` stays live temporarily for cached frontend bundles, same convention as grading's legacy `/grade` route — remove after a deploy cycle. Concurrency note: pg-boss v10 dropped `teamSize`/`teamConcurrency`, and every worker handler in this codebase (grading, ФОС, long-review) destructures the fetched batch as `[job]` — raising `batchSize` with that handler shape would silently drop the rest of the batch as "done" without running it. `registerPresentationJobWorker` instead registers `boss.work()` multiple times (`PRESENTATION_WORKER_CONCURRENCY`, default 4 per PM2 process), each an independent single-job polling loop — the safe way to get real concurrency in this pg-boss version. Frontend: `PresentationForm.tsx` switched from a single awaited request to enqueue-then-poll (mirrors `GradingForm.tsx`'s `pollGradeJob`, 2.5s interval, 10min ceiling); no localStorage resume-after-refresh yet (`usePersistedState` is grading-draft-specific — AES-GCM-encrypted and wiped by `clearGradingDrafts()` on logout — reusing it for presentation form state would misuse that lifecycle; a lighter resume mechanism is a follow-up if teachers hit it in practice).
  - **New platform-wide daily spend circuit breaker**, distinct from the existing per-teacher monthly cap (`spendCap.ts`) — a burst across *many* teachers each individually under their own cap has no aggregate ceiling today, and Phase 1's planned ~5x token multiplier makes that a real cost exposure. `services/globalSpendCap.ts` + `GlobalSpendCapExceededError` (503), checked in `llm/registry.ts`'s `chat`/`chatJSON` alongside the per-teacher check — applies platform-wide, not just to presentations. Disabled by default (`GLOBAL_DAILY_SPEND_CAP_USD` unset → no-op); an operator sets it once actual traffic volume is known.
  - 11 new unit tests (`presentationJobWorker.test.ts`, `globalSpendCap.test.ts`).
- **Programmes can be edited/deleted after creation — a typo'd code or misspelt specialty name at intake had no fix.** `code`/`specialty_name`/`education_level`/`profile`/`forms_of_study` were only ever settable at import; getting one wrong silently broke sveden-page discipline matching (`matchDiscipline`) and ФГОС-code lookups keyed on `code`, with no visible reason why the import kept failing. `updateProgramRules` (`validation/programValidation.ts`) gained validation for the four header fields — `updateProgram`'s DB query (`db/queries/programs.ts`) already supported them, only the route's validator didn't. New shared `EditProgramModal` (`components/programs/EditProgramModal.tsx`), same fields as the intake form; keeps `name` and `specialty_name` in sync on save since the detail page header reads `name` while the list/cards read `specialty_name` — editing only one would silently desync the two titles. Wired in two places: a «Редактировать» button next to Save/Analyze on the programme detail header, and a quick edit/trash icon pair per card on the programme list (`InstitutionPrograms.tsx` — the list `<button>` card became a `div[role=button]` since it now nests the action buttons, which a real `<button>` can't). Delete (`DELETE /:id`, already existed) is now also reachable from the list, not just buried at the bottom of the Конструктор tab.
- **§13 «Образовательные технологии» coverage check, extending `syllabusReview.ts`'s existing engine — no new pass, new requirement kind.** Added `'technology'` to `RequirementKind`; the structure parser (`parseSyllabusStructure`) now also extracts §13's declared teaching methods (кейс-метод, проблемное обучение, …). Scored by the same `scoreCoverage` call as goals/competencies/ЗУВ, but with a hard restriction new `ALLOWED_SECTIONS` map enforces server-side (not just prompted for): a technology requirement can only cite `independent` (СРС) or `control` (контроль/ФОС/КСР) content — never lectures/practicals/labs — so a declared technology that's never reflected in how independent work or assessment is actually run gets flagged, per the УМЦ head's ask. Same citation-validation contract as every other requirement kind. `CurriculumConformance.tsx` renders it as a new «Образовательные технологии (§13)» group; `ParsedSyllabusReport` gained `technologies_count`.
- **§12 «Материально-техническое обеспечение» check (migration 101) — the УМЦ head's literal complaint: sections that list nothing but «мел, доска и парта».** New `services/mtoReview.ts`. Phase 1 only, no licensed-software registry (the actual "база программного лицензионного обеспечения" lives in the university's own procurement/IT system, not ИСПУМ — a real registry is a separate, larger feature gated on whether that data can be supplied): parses §12 into named software vs. generic classroom items (мел/доска/парта/проектор/…), flags a §12 that names zero software at all, and cross-checks named tools the discipline's own лабораторные/практические content actually mentions (via `parseSyllabusContentSections`, already built for the topology substrate) against §12's declared list — an undeclared-tool finding carries a verbatim quote from the content itself, not just from §12. New `POST /:id/disciplines/:disciplineId/mto-review` + `GET /:id/mto-reviews`; result persisted in `program_mto_reviews` (mirrors `program_placement_reviews`). UI: a «Проверить МТО» chip next to «Проверить место в структуре» on the Documents tab. Refactored the placement-review panel's severity styling (`SEVERITY_META`, `FindingCard`, `VerdictBanner`) into shared components reused by both panels rather than duplicated — `PlacementSeverity` aliased to a shared `ReviewSeverity` type since it's no longer specific to §2.
  - **Follow-up: general vs. specialized software split + cross-discipline suggestion.** The screenshot that motivated this — a §12 naming a dozen items (ABBYY FineReader, three MS Office versions, 7Zip, Notepad, Yandex Browser) — passed the original check trivially (it names *some* software) while being pure institution-wide boilerplate, saying nothing about what the discipline itself needs. Each software item now carries a `category: 'general' | 'specialized'` (classified in the same `parseMtoSection` pass); a new `generic_software_only` finding fires when §12's software is entirely 'general' AND the discipline has substantial лабораторные/практические content (≥200 chars) — i.e. it's clearly hands-on but names no discipline-specific tool. For the "what's missing" half, deliberately did NOT add an LLM guess at "what this field usually needs" — an ungrounded claim would be the one finding in this feature not backed by a citation, undermining trust in the rest. Instead, new `missing_specialized_tool` (mirrors `placementReview.ts`'s D7 exactly): embeds this discipline's content, compares against sibling disciplines' `program_content_units` topics (same embedding substrate as §2 and topology), and for any sibling above the affinity threshold whose own latest МТО review declares a `specialized` item this discipline doesn't, suggests it — citing the sibling's own verbatim quote, deduped by name and capped to the top 3 by affinity. `reviewMto`'s params grew `discipline`/`allDisciplines`/`siblingReviews` (was just `disciplineName`) to support the cross-discipline lookup, same shape as `reviewPlacement`'s. UI splits the software list into «Специализированное ПО» / «Общее ПО» columns instead of one flat list.
- **«Место дисциплины в структуре ОП» check (migration 100) — parses РПД §2, previously read by nobody.** `services/placementReview.ts`: extracts declared предшествующие/последующие disciplines + stated направление/профиль from a discipline's uploaded working programme, then cross-checks against three independent sources rather than trusting the section alone: the real plan (`program_disciplines.semester` — inversion), the programme header (`Program.code`/`.specialty_name`/`.profile` — catches a РПД copy-pasted from another programme), and other disciplines' own latest placement review (asymmetry — A declares B as предшествующая but B's own §2 doesn't reciprocate; skipped, not flagged, when B hasn't been reviewed yet, same "не проверено ≠ ошибка" convention as the existing coverage table). Two best-effort AI findings reuse the topology substrate's embeddings + `program_content_units` (migration 099): a declared prerequisite with low content affinity to this discipline is flagged as a weak rationale, and an earlier-semester discipline with high affinity that §2 never names is suggested as a possibly-missing prerequisite — both degrade silently (not an error) when content units haven't been extracted yet for a candidate. Matched, non-inverted declared predecessors sync into `program_prerequisites` as a new `origin='declared'` (`PrerequisiteOrigin`, `db/queries/programTopology.ts`'s `replaceDeclaredPrerequisites`) — ranked above the whole-plan `extracted` inference but below a human `manual`/`confirmed` edge, densifying the topology graph for free per docs/topology-spec.md's open question #2. New `POST /:id/disciplines/:disciplineId/placement-review` + `GET /:id/placement-reviews` (`routes/programs.ts`); result persisted one row per run in `program_placement_reviews`, latest per discipline read back on reload (mirrors `program_document_reviews`, migration 051). UI: a «Проверить место в структуре» chip next to «Проверить соответствие компетенциям» on the Documents tab's per-discipline row, expanding to the declared predecessor/successor list (colour-coded internal/external/unmatched) and findings with severity + recommendation. Every declared link and every D1/D2/D3/D5 finding carries a verbatim quote from §2, validated the same way as grading/coverage citations (`documentReview.ts`'s `validateEvidence` — a quote must appear in the document, case/whitespace-insensitive, else it's dropped rather than shown as fabricated evidence) — so a teacher doubting a finding sees the actual sentence instead of having to open the source document and compare by hand. D3 asymmetry additionally surfaces the counterpart discipline's own quote when it mentions this discipline at all (just under a different framing), rather than a bare "doesn't match".

### Fixed
- **Готовность УМК was reachable — and institution-wide — for any `curriculum`-domain grant, not just УМУ/УМЦ (`routes/umcDashboard.ts`, `InstitutionLayout.tsx`).** Two stacked issues, both found live while walking a plain-РОП test account rather than from a report. First: `requireDomain('curriculum', 'view')` let *any* content-facing role reach the page at all — a РОП's ordinary `curriculum:admin` grant on their own `program` unit (needed for `/programs`) was enough, when this is meant to be the УМУ/УМЦ head's own stats page (ACCESS-MATRIX.md §6 already said other leadership only sees these numbers once РУМЦ deliberately shares them — never by grant). Regated to `requireDomain('umu', 'view')`, same domain as Мониторинг РПД/Согласование РПД, which resolves to exactly {УМУ, РУМЦ} today — not МУМЦ (staff, no `umu` grant), not any of ДИ/ДЕК/ЗК/РОП/РПГ. Second, underneath that: `requireDomain` resolves and attaches `req.domainScope.pathPrefixes`, but the route never read it — `getUmcDashboard(institutionId)` always queried every programme in the institution regardless of who was asking. New `resolveUmuPrefixes` (mirrors `routes/institution.ts`'s `resolveTeachingPrefixes`: root-anchored grants stay unrestricted, a genuine subtree grant gets a real filter) threaded through `getUmcDashboard` → `findReadinessRows`, filtering on `programs.org_unit_id`'s path the same way `getInstitutionOverview` already does for teachers — moot for today's root-anchored УМУ/РУМЦ grants, but keeps the dashboard honest if a `umu` grant is ever scoped to a sub-unit. Both `GET /` and `/export.xlsx` fixed; `docs/ACCESS-MATRIX.md` Table B/§4 updated to match.
- **Критерии/Рубрики institution curation had the same over-broad `curriculum`-domain gate — any content-facing role (РОП/РПГ/УМУ/РУМЦ/МУМЦ included) could read AND create institution-shared criteria/rubrics, when it's department/institute leadership work (ЗК/ДИ/ДЕК), plus read-only oversight for top leadership (РЕК/ПР).** Unlike Готовность УМК, this one couldn't be fixed by swapping domains — `curriculum` is deliberately shared across every content-authority role (a РОП legitimately needs it for `/programs`), so domain+level alone can't tell "ЗК's grant on a `department` unit" apart from "РОП's grant on a `program` unit." New unit-type-aware resolver: `TeacherRoleScope`/`DomainGrantRow` now carry the granting unit's `type_code`/`unitType` (`db/queries/orgUnits.ts`, `services/accessScope.ts`); new `resolveGrantOnUnitTypes` + `requireDomainOnUnitTypes` middleware additionally require the qualifying grant to sit on a unit whose type is in an allow-list (a grant on the institution root itself always qualifies regardless — that's the root/`all`-domain admin's expanded grant, must never be narrower than a type-specific policy). `routes/institution.ts`'s `/criteria` and `/rubrics` (GET+POST) now use `CRITERIA_READ_UNIT_TYPES` (`governance`/`division`/`department` — the read-only oversight width) and `CRITERIA_CREATE_UNIT_TYPES` (`division`/`department` only) instead of a flat `curriculum:view`/`curriculum:edit`. Frontend: new `criteria_access` flag on the auth payload (`routes/auth.ts`'s `adminFlags`, mirroring the existing `curriculum_access`/`teaching_access`/etc. pattern) drives the «Критерии»/«Рубрики» institution-nav entries specifically (`InstitutionLayout.tsx`'s NAV gained a `'criteria'` pseudo-domain); `Sidebar.tsx`'s and `App.tsx`'s «Организация»-panel visibility checks swapped from `curriculum_access` to `criteria_access` since no institution-nav item is gated on the broad `curriculum` domain anymore — a plain `curriculum`-only holder would otherwise still see the panel link and land on an empty page inside. 5 new unit tests for `resolveGrantOnUnitTypes`; existing `accessScope`/`orgScope` test fixtures updated for the new required field. `docs/ACCESS-MATRIX.md` §1 (domain rationale), Table B, and §4 updated to match — ДИ/ДЕК structurally can't create either way (their role bundle only grants `curriculum:view`, unchanged).
- **Same over-broad pattern found a third time, in the «Организация» overview surfaces (Обзор/Использование/Преподаватели-чтение) — narrowed the same way.** `teaching:view` doesn't distinguish a РОП's grant on their `program` unit (or a РПГ's on `cluster`) from a ЗК/ДИ/ДЕК's on `department`/`division`, so any programme or polygroup head reached institution-wide overview/usage/roster surfaces meant for department/institute leadership — their own subtree's activity already has a home at `/leadership`, an unrelated gate this doesn't touch. New `TEACHING_OVERVIEW_UNIT_TYPES` (`services/accessScope.ts`, same shape as `CRITERIA_READ_UNIT_TYPES`, kept as a separate constant since the two policies may diverge) plus `requireDomainOnUnitTypes('teaching', 'view', TEACHING_OVERVIEW_UNIT_TYPES)` on `/overview`, `/usage/daily`, `/usage/export`, `/teachers` (`routes/institution.ts`) — the only four routes gated on plain `teaching:view`, confirmed by grep before changing anything. New `org_overview_access` flag on the auth payload (mirrors `criteria_access`); `InstitutionLayout.tsx` gained an `'orgOverview'` pseudo-domain for those three NAV entries; `Sidebar.tsx`/`App.tsx`'s panel-visibility checks swapped `teaching_access` → `org_overview_access` for the same reason `curriculum_access` → `criteria_access` swapped earlier in this pass.
- **Root cause of why the affected test account (a plain РОП) ended up with far broader access than any of the above in the first place: granting `role: 'admin'` on ANY unit is hard-locked to `domain: 'all'` (`validation/orgUnitValidation.ts`'s `grantRoleRules`, a deliberate Phase 1 restriction, explicitly rejects `admin` + a scoped domain) — but ACCESS-MATRIX.md's Table A promises `curriculum:admin` for РОП and `umu:admin, curriculum:admin` for РУМЦ, which are consequently impossible to actually grant.** Whoever set up the affected account almost certainly picked «Администратор» in Преподаватели-и-роли (the closest label to "give them control of their programme") and got the institution-wide wildcard silently substituted instead — expanding into every domain (`platform`/`curriculum`/`teaching`/`umu`, all at `admin`) on that one grant's path. A DB audit found it had happened twice (`daniel@knity.ru` on a `program` unit, `daniel@boadtech.com` on a УМЦ `admin_office` unit — the latter left alone, out of scope for this pass). Fixed the one account this conversation was about: revoked the `admin`/`all` row, replaced with `curriculum:edit` + `teaching:view` on the same unit — the real Table A bundle, losing no capability (`admin` and `edit` are already functionally identical everywhere except the `platform:admin` Structure gate, per ACCESS-MATRIX.md's own §2 footnote). The systemic trap in the role-assignment UI now has a warning: `InstitutionStructure.tsx`'s `grantWarning` already flagged «Администратор» on the institution root and on `governance`/`admin_office` units — extended to flag it on every OTHER unit type too (department/division/cluster/direction/programme), explaining that the domain silently becomes «Все» rather than staying scoped to that unit, and suggesting «Редактор» + an explicit domain instead (functionally identical today per the `admin`≈`edit` equivalence above). Verified live: selecting «Администратор» on the affected programme's own unit now shows the new warning text; the institution-root and `admin_office` warnings still fire correctly (no regression).
- **Root-caused and removed the Phase 1 restriction itself, rather than just warning about it.** `role: 'admin'` no longer auto-forces `domain: 'all'` — it now scopes exactly like `edit`/`view` do, to whichever domain and unit are actually chosen (the only functional gap between `admin` and `edit` remains the `platform:admin` Структура gate, per ACCESS-MATRIX.md's own footnote — nothing is lost). `domain: 'all'` keeps its "every axis" meaning, but is now only accepted for `role: 'admin'` when the target unit **is** the institution root — `routes/orgUnits.ts`'s `POST /roles` rejects the combination anywhere else (`ValidationError`), removing the exact trap that produced the `daniel@knity.ru`/`daniel@boadtech.com` incident: picking «Администратор» for a scoped role no longer silently becomes institution-wide. `validation/orgUnitValidation.ts`'s old `grantRoleRules` custom validator (the inverse, now-wrong rule — "admin must be domain='all'") removed; the root-only check moved into the route handler, where the unit is already loaded via `unitInScope`. `InstitutionStructure.tsx`: the domain selector is no longer hidden when `role === 'admin'`; `grantWarning` reworked to take `domain` as a parameter (the institution-wide-programme-access warning was previously firing for irrelevant domains like `teaching`/`platform`/`umu` — narrowed to `domain IN ('all', 'curriculum')`, matching `services/programAccess.ts`'s own query); «Назначить» is disabled client-side for the one combination the server now rejects, instead of round-tripping to an error.
- **Учебный план и РПД's three tabs (`Curriculum.tsx`) wiped their analysis on tab switch, and everything wiped on refresh.** Each tab (`OverlapTab`, `CurriculumConformance`, `CurriculumStudio`) held its picker selection and result in local `useState`, and the parent only rendered the active tab (`{tab === 'x' && <Tab/>}`) — switching tabs unmounted the other two and discarded their state. New `hooks/useSessionStorageState.ts` (plain sessionStorage mirror, no encryption — this is course/programme-level content, not student PII, so it doesn't need `usePersistedState`'s localStorage+AES-GCM treatment) backs the picker/result state in all three tabs; `Curriculum.tsx` also now keeps all three tabs mounted (CSS `hidden` instead of conditional rendering) so switching tabs no longer unmounts them in the first place. `CurriculumStudio`'s course selection also falls back to the sessionStorage value when there's no `?course=` deep link, so a plain refresh no longer drops back to an empty picker.
- **Admin/institution "Проверок" (checks) usage columns counted logged LLM calls, not real grading actions — overstated activity by anywhere from 2x to 7x+.** `getDailyUsage`/`getUsageByTeacher` (`db/queries/usageLog.ts`) and `getInstitutionDailyUsage` (`db/queries/institutions.ts`) derived `grade_count` from `COUNT(*) FILTER (WHERE feature = 'grading')` over `api_usage_log` — but `feature: 'grading'` is a shared LLM-cost bucket reused by ~15 unrelated services (`documentReview.ts`, `mtoReview.ts`, `placementReview.ts`, `programDiff.ts`, `programAnalysis.ts`, `programContentUnits.ts`, `programImport.ts`, `syllabusReview.ts`, `policyMemo.ts`, `cohortSynthesis.ts`, `docChat.ts`, `feedbackChallenge.ts`, `evalHarness.ts`, `longReview.ts`), and even a single real grading click can log several rows under it (the feedback critic pass, calc verification, citation check, and — in "тщательная проверка" mode — every confidence-ensemble sample plus a reconciliation call all inherit the same context). All three queries now derive `grade_count` from `assignments` (one row per submission actually graded, via `services/grading.ts`'s `grade()` — includes completed long reviews, since `longReview.ts` calls `createAssignment()` on completion, so no separate count was needed there) joined/unioned against the token-cost aggregation from `api_usage_log`, preserving the institution-scoped subtree-filtering (`unitPathPrefixes`) the institution query already had. The platform-admin "Проверок сегодня" stat card (`AdminOverview.tsx`, backed by `routes/admin.ts`'s `/overview`) was already correct — it queried `assignments` directly — only the table columns beneath it, `AdminUsage.tsx`'s day/teacher tabs, and `InstitutionUsage.tsx` (+ its CSV export) were affected; no frontend changes needed since they already consume the same `grade_count` field name. Verified against real production data: one account that showed 161 "Проверок"/$0.81 now correctly shows 22 (feature-testing activity across other AI tools, not 161 actual grades); a day that showed 417 now correctly shows 0. 7 new integration tests (`usageLog.integration.test.ts`, `institutions.usage.integration.test.ts`) assert `grade_count` reflects `assignments` even when several `feature:'grading'` rows are logged for the same action, and that institution scoping doesn't leak another institution's grades.
- **Three more findings from the same external security audit.**
  - **LTI OpenID discovery `fetch` had no timeout and is SSRF-adjacent (`services/lti.ts`'s `fetchPlatformOpenIdConfiguration`).** `GET /api/lti/register` is gated only by a one-time registration-session token, not full auth, and passes the `openid_configuration` query param straight through to `fetch()` with no bound — a slow or hanging target could tie up the request indefinitely, and the token only gates *who* can trigger the fetch, not *what* URL it points at. Now wrapped in the same `AbortController`/10s-timeout pattern already used in `services/labourMarket.ts` and `presentationExport.ts`, surfacing a `504 LTI_DISCOVERY_TIMEOUT` instead of hanging.
  - **SPA's CSP allowed `'unsafe-inline'` scripts (`deploy/nginx/gradeassist.conf`).** `helmet`'s strict CSP in `app.ts` only covers API JSON responses — the actual served HTML (where XSS matters) got its CSP from nginx, which allowed inline scripts because `index.html` had one: the Yandex.Metrika loader. Moved that loader to `frontend/public/metrika-init.js` (external, same-origin, served as a static asset) and dropped `'unsafe-inline'` from nginx's `script-src`. `style-src` keeps `'unsafe-inline'` (React's `style={{...}}` attributes need it) — out of scope for this pass.
  - **Payment webhook (`routes/payments.ts`'s `POST /api/payments/webhook`) had no dedicated rate limiter**, only the app-wide `generalLimiter` (200/15min, shared with every other route hitting that IP). New `webhookLimiter` (120/min, IP-keyed) in `middleware/rateLimits.ts`.
- **Session JWT moved out of LocalStorage into an HttpOnly cookie (external security audit finding).** The teacher session token was persisted to `localStorage` (`ga_token`, `frontend/src/store/authStore.ts`) and attached to every request as an `Authorization: Bearer` header — any XSS bug anywhere on the site would let an attacker read it and fully impersonate the teacher. Backend now sets it as `ispum_session`, `HttpOnly`/`SameSite=Lax`/`Secure`-in-prod (`lib/session.ts`), read by `middleware/authenticate.ts` from `req.cookies` instead of the header; `lib/jwt.ts`'s `signToken` now also mints a `dks` (draft-key-seed) JWT claim so `lib/draftCrypto.ts`'s local drafts-encryption key (previously SHA-256 of the JWT itself, now unreadable by JS) has a non-secret rotating value to derive from instead. `authStore.ts`'s `token` field became a plain `authenticated: boolean` UI signal — the actual session is never client-readable again. SAML SSO and LTI launches (`routes/sso.ts`, `routes/lti.ts`) no longer pass the token via the `?token=` redirect query string (also closes a lesser leak: URL query strings land in server logs, browser history, and `Referer` headers) — the cookie is set directly on the redirect response, and `SsoCallback.tsx`/`LtiCallback.tsx`/`LtiDeepLink.tsx` just call `/api/auth/me` with no token param. New `POST /api/auth/logout` clears the cookie server-side; `Sidebar.tsx`, `Settings.tsx`, `AdminLayout.tsx`, and `InstitutionLayout.tsx` each had their own inline logout that only cleared client state — fixed to also call the new endpoint, since a "logged out" client with a still-valid server cookie was the concrete bug caught during manual verification. New defense-in-depth CSRF check in `authenticate.ts`: mutating requests (POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE) must carry `X-Requested-With: ISPUM`, which a cross-site `<form>` POST can't set and a non-allow-listed origin can't get past CORS preflight to attach — on top of `SameSite=Lax`, which already blocks the cookie on cross-site state-changing requests. Also fixed in the same pass: CORS's hardcoded `localhost:5173`/`4173` origins are now gated behind `NODE_ENV !== 'production'` (were included unconditionally, so any locally-running site/extension on a teacher's machine could make credentialed cross-origin requests against the production API), and every user-controlled value interpolated into `lib/emailTemplates.ts`'s HTML bodies (names, institution names, admin-notification fields) now goes through `escapeHtml()` — previously only `feedbackEmail`/`contactMessageEmail`'s free-text `message` field was escaped.
- **Multi-hat users silently lost their broad access (`services/accessScope.ts`).** `getAccessScope` kept only one level per domain and, when a higher level won, **discarded the lower grant's paths**. Concretely: a Проректор holding `view × teaching × root` who was *also* made Заведующий кафедрой (`admin × teaching × кафедра`) had `admin` win and the root path thrown away — `resolveTeachingPrefixes` then narrowed them to just that kafedra, so the institution overview, usage analytics and roster showed one department instead of the whole university. **Gaining a second role made them see strictly less.** Latent only because nobody held that combination yet; the role model (docs/ACCESS-MATRIX.md) makes it the normal case. `AccessScope[domain]` is now a **list of grants**, never collapsed; new `resolveGrant(scope, domain, minLevel)` returns the grants that actually satisfy the required level with their paths unioned (paths of grants *below* minLevel are deliberately excluded, so a `view`-only grant elsewhere can't widen an `edit`-level route's subtree filter), and `maxLevel()` powers the coarse `*_access` nav flags. `DomainGrant` (what `req.domainScope` carries) keeps its shape, so the path-filtering callers in `routes/institution.ts` / `routes/orgUnits.ts` needed no changes. The old test asserting the collapse ("keeps the highest level per domain") was **encoding the bug as intended behaviour** — replaced, plus 8 new tests including the exact Проректор+Завкафедрой regression.
- **Read-only roles got zero access to Образовательные программы (`services/programAccess.ts`).** `getProgramAccessScope` filtered its grant query to `role IN ('admin','edit')`, ignoring `view` entirely — so a Ректор or Проректор holding `curriculum:view` resolved to `kind: 'none'` and couldn't see a single programme, despite oversight being their whole job. `view` grants now yield read-only access at the breadth their unit type implies (`governance`/`admin_office`/`institution` → `all-ro`; subtree units → read-only `specific`). To avoid the obvious trap, `specific` gained a separate `editableUnitIds` alongside `programUnitIds`: a teacher holding `view` on one subtree and `edit` on another would otherwise have had the union treated as writable, silently granting edit over the view-only subtree.

### Added
- **Topology graph substrate (docs/topology-spec.md, Increment 0) — invisible foundation, no UI.** `services/programAnalysis.ts`'s LLM passes already infer prerequisite edges and competency formation-stage links, then discarded them into a cached `program_analyses` JSONB blob. They're now also persisted as id-based, queryable rows: new `program_prerequisites` / `program_competency_links` / `program_content_units` / `program_verification_links` tables (migration `099`). Edges used to be matched by discipline **name** with unmatched names silently dropped (`analyzeSequencing`'s `norm()` lookup) — `resolveSequencingEdges` now resolves each endpoint to its real `program_disciplines.id` and surfaces unmatched names instead of swallowing them (`SequencingResult.unmatched_names`). Every persisted edge carries an `origin` (`extracted`/`manual`/`confirmed`) so re-running the analysis can safely replace its own prior output without ever touching a row a future sandbox UI (Increment 4) marks `manual`/`confirmed` — the `ON CONFLICT ... WHERE origin = 'extracted'` guard in the new `db/queries/programTopology.ts` is the whole mechanism. `program_competencies` gained a nullable `fgos_competency_id` FK onto the platform's canonical ФГОС registry (`fgos_competencies`, migration 088): new `services/fgosMatch.ts` resolves УК/ОПК codes onto it (ПК can't resolve — the registry's `type` CHECK excludes them by design, ПК being programme-owned not federal). New `services/programContentUnits.ts` extracts individual lecture/practical/lab/СРС units below the whole-section blob `syllabusReview.ts`'s existing parser produces (reused via a new exported `parseSyllabusContentSections`), preferring a discipline's *approved* РПД (`rpd_submissions.status = 'approved'`, new `findApprovedDocumentForDiscipline`) over merely the latest upload. All of this runs as a best-effort side effect of the existing `POST /:id/analyze` — no new route, no new user-visible surface; a failure anywhere in it is logged and never blocks the analysis report a РОП is waiting on. 33 new/extended unit tests + a 6-case integration suite against a real Postgres DB (confirms the re-analysis-preserves-confirmed-edges invariant end-to-end, and that removing a discipline cascades cleanly with no orphaned rows). **Latency fix found during live verification against a real 59-discipline programme:** content-unit extraction (one LLM round trip per uploaded РПД) was originally awaited inline, which on that real programme pushed `/analyze` well past its 180s client timeout — `persistContentUnits` is now its own function, fired-and-forgotten by the route (same posture as `services/embeddings.ts`'s `generateAndStoreEmbedding`) while the cheap edge/link/ФГОС persistence stays awaited (~40s total, matching the pre-existing baseline).
- **Topology Increment 1 — read-only «Топология» tab, landed as an actual node-and-edge graph.** The first visible surface over Increment 0's substrate, through three shapes before it matched what was asked for. Pass 1: a competency×semester matrix mirroring the Отчёт tab's existing `ProgressionRow` heatmap — rejected live as a near-duplicate, chips overflowing on long discipline names. Pass 2: a competency-centric "roadmap" (one progress track per competency) — better, but still had no discipline nodes, no ФОС, no edges, no practicals; shown a reference network-topology diagram (servers/DBs as nodes, labeled colored connections, clear flow toward an endpoint), the user named precisely what was missing. Pass 3, what shipped: new `frontend/src/components/programs/CurriculumGraph.tsx` — disciplines and competencies as actual nodes on a deterministic grid (x = semester, y = position within it; no force-directed layout, no graph library — none exists in this codebase and physics-based placement isn't reproducible run to run), `prerequisite` edges always drawn between disciplines (red if inverted), a compact competency "landmark" column (code + status dot) always visible as the throughline's destination, with a dashed ФОС placeholder next to every competency — no ФОС data exists yet (Increment 3a), so the gap is drawn as a visible fact rather than hidden. Click a discipline or competency to reveal its specific `contributes-to` edges (stage-colored) and open a detail panel; selecting a discipline also lazy-fetches its content units via a new `GET /:id/disciplines/:disciplineId/content-units` (reusing `listContentUnitsByDiscipline`, extracted since Increment 0b but never read until now) — grouped by lecture/practical/lab/СРС/control, directly answering "I don't see practicals." `OutcomeDeliveryCard` stays promoted to the top of the tab (does the student graduate fully formed, answered before anything else); the six findings tiles lost their now-orphaned click-to-highlight (superseded by node selection) and are plain counts again. Explicitly still out of scope, confirmed with the user rather than assumed: dragging nodes to see recomputed outcomes (R3, the sandbox), the suggestion engine (§4.3), and real ФОС data (R2/Increment 3a) — none of this pass's node/edge work is wasted when those land; the skeleton is the substrate a sandbox would edit, and placeholders become real nodes without a layout change. Verified live against the same real 59-discipline, 24-competency КНИТУ programme: clicking a discipline fires the new content-units request and shows its real extracted lecture/lab/practical titles; clicking a competency reverse-highlights every discipline feeding it with a real stage-colored edge, confirmed both visually and via DOM query.
- **РПД approval workflow — phases 4c + 4d (docs/RPD-WORKFLOW.md).** 4c: `rpd_submissions.status` joined into Готовность УМК's `findReadinessRows` (LEFT JOIN — NULL means "never entered the workflow," distinct from `has_syllabus`/`reviewed`, which only say a file exists and was AI-checked, not where it sits in the human approval route). New «Согласование» column on the dashboard table (reusing `SubmissionStatusBadge`, the same component `/my-syllabi` and both queues already use) and a matching column in the XLSX export. 4d: `services/rpdNotifications.ts` — three emails (submitted → every РОП, not just one cached holder: `listRoleHoldersForUnit` finds every `admin`/`edit` `curriculum` holder on the programme's org unit or its ancestors, same ancestor-or-self path match as `teacherCanActOnUnit`, so a co-РОП or covering institute director isn't left out; returned → the responsible teacher with the actual comment; approved → the responsible teacher), dispatched from `services/rpdSubmissions.ts` after every committed transition, always fire-and-forget (a failed lookup or send must never affect whether the underlying action succeeded). Verified end-to-end against the real database and real Unisender delivery (submit → return → resubmit → forward → approve, all three emails actually sent).
- **«Ответственный за дисциплину» assignment screen (docs/RPD-WORKFLOW.md phase 4a, closing the gap left after 4b).** The `PUT .../responsible` endpoint existed with no UI to call it. New inline control on the programme's Documents tab, under each discipline's name — current responsible teacher (or «не назначен») + «Изменить» toggle → a dropdown of institution teachers. New lean `GET /api/institution/programs/teachers`, deliberately not a reuse of `institution.ts`'s `/teachers` (gated `teaching:view`): a РОП's own bundle (docs/ACCESS-MATRIX.md Table A) is `curriculum:admin` + `teaching:view`, but that pairing isn't yet enforced by any assignment UI, so in practice a РОП can easily hold `curriculum` access without `teaching` — reusing that gate would 403 exactly the caller who needs this picker. Scoped institution-wide for now (not filtered by department/subtree); narrows to a smaller candidate list if that proves necessary once used for real.
- **РПД approval workflow — phase 4b (docs/RPD-WORKFLOW.md).** The state machine over a discipline's РПД: `draft → submitted → returned → forwarded → approved`, five states not six (a return is one state; WHICH stage returned it — РОП or УМЦ — is a separate `returned_by_stage` field, fully determined by which status the return happened from). New `rpd_submissions` (one active submission per discipline) + append-only `rpd_submission_events` (migration `098`, same "audit trail survives independently of current state" posture as `approved_revisions`). Pure transition table in `services/rpdSubmissionState.ts` (17 unit tests, mirrors `liveSessions.ts`'s pure-state-machine split) — a РОП can never approve without УМЦ (no branch skips that stage), and every transition is validated against the submission's actual current status server-side, not inferred from which button was clicked. Orchestrator `services/rpdSubmissions.ts`: both submission paths from phase 4a's design (§2.1 — upload a file, or submit straight from a РПД-студия draft rendered to a real `.docx` via new `services/syllabusDraftDocx.ts`) converge on the same `program_documents` row and the same `finishSubmit()`, which runs Feature K's AI coverage check automatically on submit (best-effort — a failed check never blocks the submission) so the РОП opens their queue with the report already attached. New teacher-facing `/my-syllabi` page (status badge, return-comment banner, event history, both submit actions — the draft-submit button only shows when the discipline has a linked `course_id`). New «Проверка РПД» tab in РОП Студия — the `submitted` queue across every programme the РОП holds, aggregated client-side via `useQueries` (a РОП typically holds a handful, so no dedicated cross-programme aggregate endpoint was worth building yet). New `/institution/rpd-approvals` page + router, gated on the `umu` domain (not `curriculum` — same split that already moved Мониторинг РПД, so a Заведующий кафедрой doesn't see institution-wide approval traffic) — the `forwarded` queue institution-wide, with return/approve actions scoped via a new `findSubmissionByIdForInstitution` (deliberately not the bare-id lookup, which would have let a guessed/enumerated submission id from another institution be acted on by anyone holding `umu:edit` anywhere). Full state-machine cycle (create → submit → return → resubmit → forward → approve) and both submission paths verified directly against the real database, including graceful degradation when text extraction fails on an unsupported file type.
- **«Ответственный за дисциплину» + teacher-scoped syllabus surface (docs/RPD-WORKFLOW.md phase 4a).** Groundwork for the РПД approval route: nothing in the schema recorded *whose* РПД a programme discipline is — `program_disciplines.course_id → courses.teacher_id` exists but is nullable, indirect, and answers a different question ("which of my subjects this corresponds to"), so it can't drive "who must submit this for review". New `program_disciplines.responsible_teacher_id` (migration `097`, nullable — existing disciplines surface to the РОП as «без ответственного» rather than silently belonging to nobody). Assigned through its own endpoint (`PUT /api/institution/programs/:id/disciplines/:disciplineId/responsible`, `curriculum:edit` via the existing `assertEdit`) rather than as a field on `replaceDisciplines` — re-saving the учебный план must never silently clear РПД ownership, and since that helper UPDATEs matched rows in place without touching the new column, responsibility survives plan edits for free. This is also what replaces the **rejected `syllabus` access domain** from the access matrix: the right to author a discipline's РПД is an assigned responsibility, not a subtree grant (a grant would have meant "can edit every РПД in the kafedra", which is not what a teacher gets). New teacher-facing `GET /api/my-syllabi` on its own router — deliberately *not* a relaxed gate on `/api/institution/programs`, which is `requireProgramAccess` and which a plain teacher fails by design; widening a broad admin router so one role can reach one thing is the same shape as the cross-domain leaks already caught twice on the §7.10 axis. Every row is filtered by `responsible_teacher_id = <caller>`, so there is no programme id, unit id, or scope parameter the caller could supply to widen it. Query verified directly against real production programme data before wiring.
- **New `umu` access domain + read/write split on Мониторинг РПД (docs/ACCESS-MATRIX.md).** РПД monitoring was gated on `curriculum:edit`, the same grant a Заведующий кафедрой legitimately needs to author institution criteria — and two features behind the same domain+level are indistinguishable, so a kafedra head could not be given criteria authorship without also handing them institution-wide filing compliance. Мониторинг РПД moved to its own `umu` domain (`GET` → `umu:view`, uploads/mapping/reminder letters → `umu:edit`; reminder drafting counts as a write despite being a GET). No migration needed — `org_unit_roles.domain` is free-text since 087, and the only grants in existence are `domain='all'`, which expands to include the new domain, so no holder loses access. Plumbed through as `umu_access` on the auth payload, the institution NAV's domain gating, the sidebar panel gate, and the role-grant domain picker. **Стратегия развития** moved off root-admin-only onto `platform:edit` in the same pass (Структура stays at `platform:admin`), so a Проректор can own the strategy document without being handed org-structure CRUD; it also picks up global audit logging, which it previously escaped by sitting after the `selfAudited` flag without self-auditing.
- **`docs/ACCESS-MATRIX.md`** — the role-model design artifact: role catalog with grant bundles, per-feature gate table, and a generated access matrix (bundles × gates), plus the agreed assignment rules and the implementation plan.
- **«УМУ» sidebar grouping for Мониторинг РПД + Готовность УМК.** Both surfaces were flat, ungrouped NAV entries despite belonging to the same real-world department (Учебно-методическое управление) — no room to signal that connection, or to slot future УМУ-owned features (К's standalone conformance check, РПД-студия, the AC integration) into the same place. `InstitutionLayout.tsx`'s NAV entries gained an optional `group` field purely for a small section-header label above the first visible item of that group — **not a new access-control axis**: both items are still gated exactly as before by the `curriculum` domain grant, so a УМЦ head's sidebar looks the same, just with a labeled section instead of two loose rows. The header only renders when at least one item of the group survives the existing domain filter, so a teacher with no `curriculum` access never sees a dangling "УМУ" label with nothing under it.
- **УМЦ dashboard — institution-wide УМК readiness matrix + XLSX export (TODO Feature V).** "Almost entirely assembly, not new capability" per the original TODO entry — `program_documents`/`program_document_reviews` (Feature K's coverage engine) already carry every signal needed; this is a new aggregation layer over them, no new analysis engine. New `db/queries/umcDashboard.ts`'s `findReadinessRows` — one query, one row per (programme, discipline): `programs` → `program_disciplines` → `LEFT JOIN LATERAL` the current (non-superseded, migration 084) `working_programme` document → `LEFT JOIN LATERAL` the latest `program_document_reviews` row for `overall_coverage`/`created_at`. "Department" is resolved as the parent of the programme's own org-tree unit (`programs.org_unit_id`'s parent) — a programme with no org-tree link rolls into a `NULL`-keyed «Без подразделения» bucket rather than being dropped. New `services/umcDashboard.ts`: pure `aggregateUmcDashboard` (unit-tested, 8 tests, mirrors `cohortAnalytics.ts`'s pure-aggregation split) rolls the flat row list up by department — discipline/syllabus/reviewed counts plus average coverage computed **only over reviewed disciplines** (an unreviewed discipline has no score to average in; treating it as 0 would conflate "not yet checked" with "checked and found lacking"). New `services/umcDashboardXlsx.ts` (exceljs, mirrors `rpdReportXlsx.ts`'s styling and reuses its `STATUS_FILL_HEX` colour tiers — a colour means the same thing everywhere in this app) — `Итоги` rollup sheet + full `Дисциплины` sheet, three-tier row shading (no РПД = danger, uploaded-but-unreviewed = uncoloured, reviewed = tiered on the coverage score). New `GET /api/institution/umc-dashboard` + `/export.xlsx`, gated by `requireDomain('curriculum', 'view')` (same domain-axis precedent as Мониторинг РПД and institution criteria/rubrics — a УМЦ head reaches it without institution-root admin) and a new Institution-tier-only `umcDashboard` plan flag. Frontend: new `pages/institution/UmcDashboard.tsx` (totals cards + by-department table + full discipline matrix with a status dot per row), new nav entry in `InstitutionLayout.tsx`'s domain-gated NAV. Query verified directly against real production programme data (a real institution's Строительство programme, mixed reviewed/unreviewed disciplines) before wiring the route.
- **Don't retry token-truncated LLM responses (TODO Improvement #10).** `chatJSON`'s parse-and-retry loop couldn't tell "malformed JSON, a retry might fix it" from "hit the token ceiling, retrying the identical request truncates again" — a truncated response used to come back as a normal successful `chat()` call, fail `JSON.parse`, and burn a second, identically-doomed call (~60s + double token cost) before finally surfacing an error, muddying incident forensics with two generic failure rows for one event. New `TruncatedResponseError` (DeepSeek provider only, per the TODO's scoped "cheap v1" — not threaded through the shared `LLMProvider` interface, which would touch every AI feature routing through `llm/registry.ts` for a now-rare path): `attemptChat` checks `choices[0].finish_reason === 'length'` and throws immediately instead of returning the cut-off content, writing one accurately-costed usage-log row (`errorCode: 'TRUNCATED'`, real token counts) instead of the old two generic ones. Not an `AxiosError`, so the existing multi-account fallback's `isRetryable()` already treats it as non-retryable without any extra casing — a truncation isn't an account problem, so it fails fast rather than trying another account or attempting `chatJSON`'s repair-retry. 5 new unit tests (`deepseek.test.ts`).
- **Real PPTX export for presentations (TODO Feature D) + a pasted-conspectus source option.** Two independent pieces shipped together while in the same page.
  - **Native PowerPoint download.** New `services/presentationExport.ts` (`pptxgenjs`, lazily imported so the backend still boots without it installed, same posture as `docx` in `fosExport.ts`): renders each of the 8 typed slide layouts (title/bullets/concept/formula/comparison/diagram/discussion/summary) as a real, editable `.pptx` — branded amber-accent header bar, speaker notes via `addNotes`, diagram images fetched and embedded as base64 (`AbortController` + 10s timeout, falls back to a placeholder box on any fetch failure rather than blocking the export). New `GET /api/presentations/:id/export.pptx`, gated by a new `pptxExport` plan flag (Pro+) via `checkFeatureAccess` — separate from `presentationsPerMonth`, since generation stays available on Free and the native file is the paid differentiator. Frontend: `Скачать PPTX` button in `SlideContent.tsx` (blob download via the existing JWT-authenticated `downloadCsv` helper), upgrade-modal prompt on the plan gate.
  - **LaTeX → readable Unicode, not just stripped delimiters.** First cut only stripped `$...$` markers, leaving raw commands like `\rho`, `x^2`, `\frac{a}{b}` sitting in the slide text — caught in review as "formulas look weird." New `latexToPlainText()`: Greek letters, `\frac`→`(a)/(b)`, `\sqrt`→`√(...)`, common operators, and real Unicode super/subscripts (`x^2`→x², digit-only — a non-numeric script falls back to `^`/`_` + the original text rather than silently concatenating, e.g. `a_{max}`→`a_max` not `amax`). Found and fixed a real bug in the same pass: `\frac{P_{полезн}}{P_{затрач}}` (a fraction argument that itself contains a subscript's braces) broke the original brace-free-only regex entirely, degrading to garbage like `fracP_полезнP_затрач` — the pattern now tolerates one level of nested braces. 17 unit tests (`presentationExport.test.ts`); output verified by generating a real `.pptx`, unzipping it, and inspecting the slide XML directly (same verification posture as the ФОС DOCX export).
  - **Pasted conspectus as a generation source**, same precedent as Quizzes' existing `source_text`: a new optional textarea in `PresentationForm.tsx` ("Свой конспект") that, when filled, replaces course RAG retrieval entirely and instructs the model to build the deck strictly from the teacher's own notes (up to 20k chars) instead of inventing content from the topic alone — most teachers already have a conspectus and don't want the model guessing at one.
  - Also fixed in passing: the same-shaped low-contrast `text-ink-secondary` "← Новая презентация"/"← Новый запрос" reset links in `Presentations.tsx` and `Topics.tsx` were barely visible against the TopBar background — swapped for the bordered `Button variant="secondary"` pill Quizzes.tsx already used correctly for the identical action.
- **Per-course/teacher score calibration (TODO Feature AF, Research §10.1) — corrects systematic grading bias against each teacher's own scale.** The AI's raw score was trusted as-is (`clampScore(result.score)`); every teacher runs systematically hot or cold and every course has its own grade distribution, exactly the bias agreement metrics (QWK/MAE) punish hardest. `assignments` already pairs every `ai_score` with the teacher's final `approved_score` over time — enough history to fit a correction with zero extra LLM calls. New `lib/scoreCalibration.ts` (pure, unit-tested): `fitIsotonicCalibration` runs Pool Adjacent Violators over those pairs to produce a monotone non-decreasing map, returning `null` below a minimum sample size or when the fit collapses into one block — callers must treat `null` as "pass the raw score through," never as zero correction; `applyCalibration` interpolates linearly between breakpoints and extrapolates flat beyond the fitted domain. New migration `094_score_calibration.sql` (`score_calibration` table, one row per `(scope_type, scope_id)` — `course`/`teacher`/`institution`) and `db/queries/scoreCalibration.ts`. New `services/scoreCalibration.ts`: `getActiveCalibrationMap` does most-specific-first backoff (course → teacher → institution → none), 5-min cache per scope (mirrors `confidence.ts`'s threshold cache) so grading never does a live fit; `fitCalibration(scopeType, scopeId)` is the explicit trigger, exposed via `GET/POST /api/admin/evals/calibration[/fit]` on the existing eval-harness admin surface, mirroring `fitThresholdsForRun`'s pattern. Wired into `grading.ts`'s `grade()` right after the (possibly AI-aggregated) score is produced and before persistence, wrapped in try/catch so a lookup failure falls back to the raw score rather than blocking a grade. **Leakage-safe validation harness, same day:** `validateCalibrationSplit` fits on the chronologically-earliest slice of a scope's approved history only, then scores MAE/Spearman on the later slice the fit never saw — the same "no future leakage" posture `evalHarness.ts` already uses for RAG replay (`findSimilarAssignmentsBefore`), and needs zero LLM calls since calibration only ever operates on scores already sitting in `assignments`. Exposed read-only via `GET /api/admin/evals/calibration/validate` (never writes `score_calibration` — safe to call before deciding a scope is ready for the real `/calibration/fit`). 14 new unit tests total (5 for the validation split, including one that plants a systematic +15 bias across the whole timeline, fits on the first 65%, and confirms the held-out 35%'s calibrated MAE collapses from ~15 to <2 — proof the map generalises, not just memorises the fit data). Smoke-tested against the real dev DB (course/teacher/institution joins execute correctly; `null` returned cleanly on too-little-data scopes). Deferred: scheduled auto-refitting (v1 is admin-triggered only).
- **Ensemble reconciliation — adjudicate low-confidence disagreement (TODO Feature AG, Research §10.2).** `gradeEnsemble` (`confidence.ts`) already samples strict/lenient/cross-provider variants and computes dispersion, but a `low`-confidence label only flagged the work for closer teacher review — the divergent samples' own scores and the primary's justification were computed and then discarded. New `reconcileDisagreement`, gated on `confidence === 'low'` only (the ensemble is already paid for at that point; this doesn't add a call to every graded work, only the residual hard cases dragging down aggregate accuracy): one bounded `chatJSON` call feeds the model the primary's full justification (sanitised strengths/improvements bullets) plus every sample's persona/temperature/provider/score/grade, and asks it to commit to one defensible score plus a 1–2 sentence teacher-facing note. New pure `buildReconciliationPrompt` (unit-tested separately from the I/O call, matching the calcVerifier/citationChecker pure-vs-orchestrator split) assembles the prompt; the returned score is re-derived through the existing `resolveGradeForScore` contract so the letter can never go stale against a moved score, same as calibration. Best-effort: a failed reconciliation call falls back to the primary's own score/grade, logged, never blocks grading. `AiEnsemble` gained `reconciled`/`reconciliation_note` (both optional, absent when no reconciliation ran) — surfaced in `ConfidenceBadge`'s tooltip and a new line in the low-confidence banner on `GradingResult.tsx`, so the teacher sees *what* the arbiter weighed, not just that the variants disagreed. 4 new unit tests (`confidence.test.ts`) for the pure prompt builder.
- **Analytic (deterministic) aggregation for rubric grades (TODO Feature AI, Research §10.4).** When criteria + weights are used, the model previously free-handed an overall `score` disconnected from its own per-criterion numbers — an entire class of holistic-drift/arithmetic error that's pure arithmetic, not a judgement call. New `aggregateWeightedScore` in `grading.ts` (pure, unit-tested): Σ(criterion score · weight)/100 over the already-normalised `criteriaScores`, matched to the criteria snapshot the same case/whitespace-insensitive way `mergeScoresIntoSnapshot` already does; returns `null` (falls back to the model's own holistic score) when any criterion has no matching AI score by name, so a partial set is never silently aggregated into an understated total. Wired into `gradeOnce` — only overrides the numeric score when criteria are present; holistic grading (no weights to aggregate) is untouched. (**Correction:** this entry originally said "no fixed score→grade cutoff exists in this system to recompute it from" — that was wrong; the bands are canonical and were implemented in the frontend all along. See the score↔grade fix under **Fixed** below.) Ships ahead of the calibration layer above in the same score-production pipeline: aggregation corrects the model's own arithmetic first, calibration then corrects the resulting number against the teacher's scale on top of that. 5 new unit tests; full 491-test backend suite and `tsc --noEmit` both clean after all three changes.
- **DeepSeek multi-account fallback + Telegram alert on failover (prompted by the 2026-07-24 402-payment incident above).** The registry's existing silent-fallback policy only covers Yandex/Qwen → DeepSeek; DeepSeek itself, the default provider, had nowhere to go when it failed, which is exactly what took down every LLM feature platform-wide during the incident. `services/llm/deepseek.ts` now resolves an ordered list of accounts — `DEEPSEEK_API_KEY`/`DEEPSEEK_BASE_URL` (unchanged, zero config for the single-account case) plus optional `DEEPSEEK_API_KEY_2`..`_5` with their own `_BASE_URL_N`/`_ACCOUNT_NAME_N` (separately billed accounts, e.g. an Alibaba Cloud DashScope compatible-mode account — a payment failure on one has no bearing on the others). On a retryable failure (401/402/403/408/429/5xx/network — an account- or transient-provider problem, not a malformed request) it tries the next account; a genuinely bad request (400/404/422-class) fails fast without burning through every account. A per-account cooldown (5 min) deprioritizes a just-failed account on the next call without excluding it outright — if every account is cooling down, the least-recently-failed one is still tried rather than giving up with nothing attempted. A successful fallback fires a Telegram alert (`sendTelegramAlert`, reusing its existing 15-min per-code dedup) — a fallback is a *successful* call from the caller's perspective, so without this the platform could silently coast on its last working account for weeks with nobody noticing until that one failed too, the same quiet-failure shape as the incident itself, one layer up. 9 new tests (`deepseek.test.ts`) covering fallback, fail-fast on non-retryable errors, cooldown ordering, and the alert firing.
- **БРС engine v1 (TODO Feature AE) — the semester ledger governed by the РПД.** A metodist asked for Quizlet/Kahoot-style integration; the useful part of that ask was the бally-rating scheme (БРС) already declared inside the university's own РПД, which ИСПУМ already holds — so instead of integrating third-party quiz platforms (dead APIs, 152-ФЗ conflict for a Russia-resident pitch), this extracts that scheme and makes it the teacher's semester gradebook. New `services/brsScheme.ts` (pure, no DB — same posture as `fgosExtractor.ts`): `extractBrsDraft()` pulls контрольные точки/max баллы/итоговая thresholds out of a course's resolved РПД text (`routes/curriculum.ts`'s `resolveCourseText`, now exported) via one `chatJSON` call, verbatim-checking every checkpoint name against the source (`validateQuoteAgainstSource`) and flagging ✓/⚠ rather than silently trusting or dropping; `computeStudentAccrual()` rescales a 0-100 approved score onto a checkpoint's own point scale, sums + caps multiple scores against the same checkpoint, and resolves the итоговая grade label from the scheme's thresholds (never inventing a default when a total falls below every threshold).
