New migration `093_brs_scheme.sql`: `brs_schemes` (per-course, versioned — re-publishing creates a new `(course_id, version)` row rather than mutating a published one, so `assignments.brs_checkpoint_id` links on old approvals keep resolving to the scheme version that was live when they were scored), `brs_checkpoints`, `brs_manual_entries` (points for non-graded посещение/активность-type checkpoints), plus nullable `brs_checkpoint_id` columns on `assignments` and `approved_revisions` (same `ON DELETE SET NULL` precedent as `published_assignment_id`/`live_participants.assignment_id`). New `routes/brs.ts` (`checkFeatureAccess('brsEngine')`, Pro-gated, per-teacher ownership checks rather than `requireAdmin` since this is course-scoped data, not platform reference data) and `db/queries/brs.ts`. Extraction→review→publish UI at `/brs` (`BrsStudio.tsx`, mirrors `AdminFgos.tsx`'s "nothing saved until you publish" posture) with a manual point-entry affordance per non-graded checkpoint. Checkpoint-linking dropdown added to `GradingResult.tsx`'s approve action and to `LiveSessionHost.tsx`'s «Сохранить в журнал» panel — both funnel through the same `services/grading.ts` `approve()` call, so one link mechanism covers regular AI grading, published-assignment grading, and live-quiz saves uniformly, no separate per-source code path. Per-student running ledger (earned/max per checkpoint + итоговая) on `Students.tsx`'s profile view. 14 new unit tests (`brsScheme.test.ts`); full 463-test backend suite and frontend `tsc --noEmit` both clean. Verified live end-to-end: extracted a real БРС table from a course's syllabus text (3 checkpoints incl. a heuristic-flagged «вручную»-type attendance point, correct verbatim badges, correct thresholds), published, approved a grade with a checkpoint selected, added a manual attendance entry, and confirmed the student ledger showed the correct rescaled/summed running total. One bug caught only by this live pass: the scheme-to-draft response mapping dropped each checkpoint's `id`, so the frontend's checkpoint `<select>` posted the checkpoint's *name* instead of its UUID and every approve-with-checkpoint call 400'd — fixed in `routes/brs.ts` before landing.
- **ФГОС 3++ registry v1 (TODO Feature AA) — single-standard ingestion for the Feature Z pilot.** The platform had no representation of a ФГОС (federal education standard) at all — every downstream competency check ran against hand-typed programme data, not the standard itself. New platform-admin-only flow: upload a ФГОС PDF/Word → `services/documentExtractor.ts` extracts text → one `chatJSON` pass (new `services/fgosExtractor.ts`) pulls УК/ОПК competencies, structural block requirements (з.е.), and the профстандарт appendix → every competency formulation is verbatim-checked against the source text (`lib/citation.ts`'s `validateQuoteAgainstSource`) and flagged ✓/⚠ rather than silently trusted or dropped → editable review screen (`pages/admin/AdminFgos.tsx`) → the admin's confirm is what actually persists anything (`status` stays `'draft'` until then — AI never final, same as every other extraction pipeline in the app). New `routes/adminFgos.ts` (own file, `requireAdmin`-gated — a ФГОС is federal law, identical across every institution, so this is platform reference data, never institution-scoped) and migration `088_fgos_registry.sql`. 13 new tests (6 unit + 7 integration); verified live end-to-end against a real synthetic ФГОС document with a real LLM call.
- **Bulk ФГОС import from fgosvo.ru (extends TODO Feature AA).** Seeding the registry one upload at a time doesn't scale to the ~700 standards fgosvo.ru publishes — this mirrors the "paste a link, get a checklist" pattern already shipped for РОПы' bulk РПД import (Feature AD). Two-level auto-crawl: paste one top listing URL → new `services/fgosvoParser.ts` (pure, dependency-free, verified against real markup — every row on fgosvo.ru is a non-nesting `<div class="item d-flex">` block at both the category and direction level) → the backend follows every category link and returns one combined checklist (`POST /api/admin/fgos/discover`) → admin reviews/unchecks/adjusts level → each checked item is fetched, extracted, and landed as a draft one request at a time (`POST /api/admin/fgos/import-one`, client-driven loop — avoids hundreds of sequential LLM calls inside one HTTP request). `already_imported` dedup against the existing registry by `(direction_code, level)`. fgosvo.ru's domain allowlist is hardcoded (`fgosvo.ru`) — a single fixed platform-controlled source, unlike the per-institution allowlist the sveden importer needs. Also closed a gap in v1: `AdminFgos.tsx` had no way to open an existing draft for review (only delete) — clicking a row now loads it into the review screen, needed since bulk-imported drafts are too numerous to hand-review at import time. 13 new tests (8 unit against real fetched HTML + 5 integration). Verified live against the real fgosvo.ru site: one paste discovered 188 направления across 51 categories; imported two real standards end-to-end (real PDF fetch → real LLM extraction → draft); published one through the reopened review screen.
- **ФГОС registry list: color-coded level badges, pagination, search, and level filter.** The registry crossed 376 rows mid-bulk-import (only bakalavriat done — magistratura/специалитет/ординатура still to come) and the flat list was already unreadable, every level badge the same amber. `db/queries/fgos.ts` gained `listFgosStandardsPage` (LIMIT/OFFSET + count, same shape as `admin.ts`'s `/teachers` pagination); `GET /api/admin/fgos` takes `page`/`limit`/`search`/`level`, responds `{ standards, total }`. Frontend: search input + a level-filter `Select` (house pattern from `AdminTeachers.tsx`), `← Назад / X из Y · N всего / Вперёд →` pager. `LEVEL_COLOR` gives fgosvo.ru's four ФГОС ВО (3++) categories their own color pair instead of one shared amber; `ординатура` was also missing from `LEVEL_LABEL` since v1 — added. 2 new integration tests. Verified live: search 376→4, level filter 4→2, pagination confirmed across pages.
- **РОП Студия v0 — citation-grounded «обоснование актуальности» (TODO Feature Z, Phase 0 pilot).** New standalone page (`/rop-studio`, own nav entry) generating a market-relevance justification section per programme from real trudvsem.ru vacancy data + the direction's профстандарты (ФГОС registry, Feature AA). New `services/labourMarket.ts` (trudvsem client, no SSRF-allowlist needed — single fixed platform-controlled source) and `services/marketEvidenceGenerator.ts` (one `chatJSON` call grounded in structured data, instructed never to invent a number/code outside what's given — the safety net is architectural: generated text always renders next to the raw source data it was built from, not a text-matching validator). New `program_market_evidence` table (migration 089, mirrors `program_analyses`'s cached-latest shape) and `POST/GET/PUT :id/market-evidence` on the existing programs router. AI drafts, РОП edits and explicitly saves — same author-of-record posture as every other studio in the app. 14 new tests. Verified live end-to-end against the real pilot direction (15.03.02) on a real institution's actual programme: real vacancy data, all 16 real профстандарт codes, a citation-grounded generated paragraph with no fabricated numbers, edit+save round-tripped correctly. Source cards + count/salary badges added in a follow-up design pass (color-coded, formatted numbers) after the initial plain-list version shipped.
- **РОП Студия: Plane-2 grounded document (completes the Phase 0 pilot spec).** The pilot's own definition asked for one grounded document — the university's «стратегия развития» — alongside Plane-1 market data; this was the one piece still missing. New institution-admin-only Settings page (`/institution/strategy-document`) uploads exactly one document per institution (`institution_strategy_documents`/`institution_strategy_chunks`, migration 092 — `institution_id UNIQUE`, reupload replaces); reuses `documentExtractor.ts` + embeddings, no new extraction machinery. `services/chunker.ts` gained a pure `splitTextIntoChunks()` (extracted from `chunkDocument`, zero behavior change, all 11 existing tests pass unchanged) so the institution-scoped pipeline can reuse the same splitting logic without a fake course/document ID. On generation, `routes/programs.ts` embeds a query (programme title + profession terms) and applies the *same* cosine-distance refusal gate `services/docChat.ts`'s "Спроси документ" uses — a weak/no match silently omits Plane-2 rather than forcing a citation. `program_market_evidence` gained `strategy_excerpts`, rendered as a third «Источники» block in `RopStudio.tsx` only when non-empty. 9 new tests. **Real bug caught only by live verification:** the new tables shipped with `vector(1536)`, but Yandex's embedding model (the platform's only provider) is 256-dimensional — every chunk insert silently failed the dimension check and was swallowed by the fail-soft `try/catch`, so a document could show "Готово" with zero real chunks underneath. Fixed in place (migration was uncommitted) before this reached anyone. Verified live end-to-end on the real institution: uploaded a Russian strategy PDF, regenerated the real 15.03.02 programme's evidence, confirmed the excerpt appeared correctly in «Источники» and was cited verbatim in the generated paragraph; separately confirmed the refusal gate itself works under real (unmocked) embeddings — an English-language stand-in document's real cosine distance (0.357) landed just above the 0.35 threshold and was correctly omitted rather than forced in.
- **РОП Студия: all ~90 regions + multi-region generation (extends the Phase 0 pilot above).** The pilot shipped with Татарстан hardcoded as the only region; a metodist wants evidence for whichever programme/region combination is in front of them, not just the pilot's one. `labourMarket.ts`'s `SUPPORTED_REGIONS` grew to every Russian federal subject — each code individually confirmed against a live trudvsem query rather than guessed (surfaced real surprises: Chechnya is code 20 not 95, code 95 is Херсонская область, the four post-2022 territories use non-sequential codes 90/93/94/95), exposed via new `GET /api/institution/programs/regions` rather than duplicated client-side. Region selection is now multi-select — new `components/ui/MultiSelect.tsx` (searchable checkbox combobox, sibling to `Select.tsx`, stays open across picks so a metodist can check off several regions in a row) replaces the single-region dropdown. `fetchVacancySnapshot`/`generateMarketEvidenceSection` take `regionCodes: string[]` and build a per-region `RegionSnapshot[]` context so the generated paragraph can cite multiple regions' numbers in one pass; `program_market_evidence`'s `region_code`/`region_name` columns became `region_codes`/`region_names` arrays (migration 090). Updated/added tests across `labourMarket.test.ts`, `marketEvidenceGenerator.test.ts`, `programMarketEvidence.integration.test.ts`; full 443-test backend suite and frontend `tsc --noEmit` both clean. Verified live: single-region and 3-region (Алтайский край + Москва + Татарстан, 31/123/86 vacancies) generations both spot-checked with every number/salary in the generated text matching the raw «Источники» data exactly; MultiSelect's search-then-select flow confirmed to not deselect prior picks.

### Added
- **Evidence-first two-phase grading + selective reasoner (TODO Feature AH, Research §10.3/§10.5).** A single call produced score + feedback + quotes together, letting the model anchor on a first-impression number and back-fill justification. New `extractEvidence` runs first and pulls per-criterion passages while **explicitly forbidden from proposing a score** — if it scored, phase 2 would anchor on *its* number and the problem would just move. Every extracted quote is validated verbatim before it can reach the scoring prompt, so phase 2 can never reason from text that isn't in the work; a failed extraction degrades to plain single-pass grading rather than blocking the grade. `buildEvidenceBlock` renders the table *ahead of* the criteria and submission (model meets the passages before the scoring instruction) and states the list isn't exhaustive so the full work still gets read. Gated behind a new Pro/Institution `evidenceFirst` plan flag **and** the teacher's «тщательная проверка» opt-in, so ordinary gradings and the free tier are unchanged in cost. `shouldUseReasoner` extends reasoning past calc-only to thorough mode and long work — threshold deliberately **40k chars, not the ~12–15k that "long" intuitively suggests**, because the measured corpus average is 15.2k and a lower cut-off would silently route the majority of gradings through the reasoner one day after the DeepSeek 402 balance incident. Verified with a real call end-to-end.

### Fixed
- **Grading review quality: 85% of AI feedback had no anchor in the student's work (TODO Feature AK).** Measured across a real 30-assignment corpus before changing anything: of 269 strengths/improvements bullets only **42 (15%)** carried a verified quote; per-criterion comments averaged 239 chars. The cause was the prompt itself — «Не выдумывайте цитаты — **лучше null**» made grounding opt-out and actively preferred omission, while the one field that *is* mandatory (`verification_questions`) demanded a quote and complied. The anti-hallucination framing was working too well. New shared `QUOTE_RULE` inverts the default: the quote is `ОБЯЗАТЕЛЬНОЕ поле`, the model is told quotes are machine-verified, and crucially it's told to **swap the point for one it can ground** instead of dropping the quote — `validateCitation` remains the safety net (Non-Negotiable #2), which is what makes demanding quotes safe rather than reckless. The `null` escape hatch is kept deliberately for absence-type findings ("no methodology section"), since you can't quote what isn't there and pressuring the model to try is how fabrication starts. Bullet/criterion text instructions also now ask for evidence → judgement → specific action rather than «1 предложение». **Live result: grounding 15% → 89%**, per-criterion feedback 239 → 401–521 chars, with the single remaining ungrounded bullet being exactly an absence-type point.
- **Nothing distinguished "the model offered no quote" from "the quote was rejected" (TODO Feature AK).** Those have opposite fixes — tighten the prompt vs. loosen the matcher for hyphenation/OCR noise — and the split was invisible, so the 15% figure above couldn't be acted on with confidence. New `CitationStats` threads an optional tally through `validateCitation`/`normaliseBullets`/`normaliseCriteriaScores` (optional so every existing caller and test is untouched), and `gradeOnce` emits one `[Grounding]` log line per grading with the full outcome breakdown plus `groundedShare`. On the very first live run it answered the question it was built for: `absent: 0, accepted: 8, rejectedNotFound: 1` — under the new prompt the residual loss is *rejection*, not omission, so further work belongs in matcher tolerance, not prompt wording.
- **The feedback critic was ruling on evidence it could not see, and degraded feedback on 43% of works (TODO Feature AK).** `critiqueFeedback` asks "is this bullet grounded in the submission?" but received `submissionText.slice(0, 4000)` — a blind prefix. Average submission is 15,228 chars (max 117,383) and **13 of 30 works exceeded that window**, so any bullet correctly grounded in the later pages looked unsupported and was dropped or rewritten vaguer. Since the pass is gated on `feedbackCritic` (Pro+), paying users on long submissions were hit hardest. New pure `buildCriticContext` windows ±400 chars around each bullet's already-validated quote, merges overlapping windows, marks elided regions with «[…]», keeps a head excerpt for framing, and caps total length; the critic is additionally shown each bullet's own quote and told those are pre-verified, so it can no longer discard a point merely because its passage fell outside the excerpt.
- **Criteria had no per-level anchors, so the model guessed what each grade meant (TODO Feature AK).** The `criteria` table was `name` + free-text `description` — nothing said what a 90 looks like versus a 70 *for that criterion*, which drives score noise and generic prose at the same time. Migration `096_criterion_level_descriptors.sql` adds `level_descriptors JSONB` keyed by grade letter (`{"5": …, "4": …}`, all optional) — keyed by letter because the bands are already canonical in `shared/grades.ts` and a teacher thinks «на пятёрку», not «[87,100]»; JSONB rather than a child table for the same reason as `brs_schemes.grade_thresholds` (fixed-shape ≤4-entry map, always read/written with its parent). Snapshotted into `criteria_snapshot` so a past grading stays reproducible after the teacher edits their anchors. `buildLevelDescriptorLines` renders them highest-grade-first, skips blanks, and applies `sanitiseForPrompt` (Non-Negotiable #1); the "score by these levels, not by overall impression" instruction is added only when a criterion actually carries anchors. `sanitiseLevelDescriptors` at the route whitelists keys to the four canonical letters and caps each value at 600 chars. Editable on `Criteria.tsx` behind a collapsed «Уровни оценки» disclosure that auto-expands when anchors exist, with an «N из 4» badge and per-level placeholders; new `chevron-down` icon added to the design system. **Live result:** per-criterion feedback now names which level the work actually met and what's missing to reach the next one.
- **Grading accuracy integrity: score and grade letter had come apart, and it nullified QWK (TODO Feature AJ).** The 5-point bands (`5: 87–100, 4: 73–86, 3: 60–72, 2: <60`) were declared in four grading-prompt strings *and* implemented as `scoreToGrade` in `frontend/src/lib/grades.ts` — whose own comment admitted they were "mirrored from the grading prompt" — but the backend **never applied them**: it told the model the bands and persisted whatever letter came back. Both features shipped the previous day then moved the *score* independently of the *letter* (deterministic weighted aggregation, then per-scope calibration), so a weighted sum of 71 could persist alongside «Отлично», and the teacher's result panel rendered that contradiction on load (the frontend's own score↔grade sync only fires when the teacher *edits* a field). The sharper consequence: `evalHarness.ts`'s `summariseRun` computes **QWK — the headline agreement metric — on grade letters**, so aggregation and calibration improved MAE while contributing *nothing* to QWK. Fixed by lifting the scale into new **`shared/grades.ts`** (one definition for both sides; the frontend module now re-exports it and keeps only the CSS-variable `gradeColor`, leaving all 10 import sites and its 12 tests untouched) and adding pure **`resolveGradeForScore`**, which derives the letter from the final score and regenerates the label only when the letter actually moves — applied in `gradeOnce` (post-aggregation), `scoreOnce`, and `grade()` (post-calibration). 5 new tests incl. exact band boundaries.
- **Score calibration would have trained on its own output (TODO Feature AJ).** `grade()` persists the *calibrated* score into `assignments.ai_score`, and the calibration refit queries read that same column — so once a map went live, every refit would learn "calibrated → teacher" while inference kept feeding a **raw** score: train and inference distributions drift apart and the correction compounds with each refit. Fixed with migration `095_ai_score_raw.sql` (new `ai_score_raw` column), `createAssignment` persisting the pre-calibration score, and the fitting queries reading `COALESCE(ai_score_raw, ai_score)`. No backfill required — rows written before 095 were graded with no map fitted, so their `ai_score` already *is* the raw score; verified against the dev DB (all 28 existing rows have `ai_score_raw IS NULL` and resolve correctly through the fallback). Deliberately **not** applied to the policy memo's delta query (`db/queries/policyMemos.ts`): that one should keep measuring the *residual* disagreement left after calibration, so the prompt-level memo and the calibration map correct complementary things instead of both learning the same bias and double-correcting it.
- **The confidence ensemble was comparing unequally-informed graders (TODO Feature AJ).** `gradeEnsemble`'s cheap score-only samplers (`scoreOnce`) received no grading-policy memo, criteria as name+weight only (no descriptions), and retrieved examples reduced to bare labels (`"4 (78/100)"`) — while the primary (`gradeOnce`) saw the memo, full criterion descriptions, and 600-char submission excerpts with the teacher's feedback. So `score_std` conflated genuine disagreement about the work with an information asymmetry of our own making: dispersion inflated, `low` confidence over-reported, and the `fitThresholds` calibration built on top of it skewed. `scoreOnce` now receives the policy memo, criterion descriptions, and the primary's own `buildExamplesBlock`. `criterionExamples` stays excluded on purpose — `scoreOnce` emits no per-criterion scores, so per-criterion exemplars have no output surface to act on, and they're the bulkiest of the three inputs. **Note flagged in `confidence.ts`:** any `confidence_config` row fitted before this change was fitted against the noisier distribution and reads too wide — refit via a confidence replay rather than trusting it. New `grading.scoreOnce.test.ts` (7 tests) guards prompt symmetry and score/grade consistency. This was also a hard prerequisite for Feature AG (ensemble reconciliation), which is only worth building on a dispersion signal that means what it claims.
- **Production OCR/rasterization crash: `pdf-parse` permanently poisons `pdf-to-img`'s pdfjs-dist worker resolution for the rest of the process, once it has run.** Discovered 2026-07-24 chasing a "OCR still not working after the version-override fix" report — `documentExtractor.ts` always tries `pdf-parse`'s text-layer extraction before falling back to Vision OCR/rasterization, so this hit every single scanned-PDF OCR attempt in production, 100% of the time. Reproduced deterministically on the VM: `pdf-to-img` alone works; run `pdf-parse.getText()` first (in the same process) and every subsequent `pdf-to-img` call throws "API version does not match Worker version", even though both packages resolve to the identical, correct on-disk `pdfjs-dist` version — `pdf-parse`'s CJS build vendors its own inlined pdfjs-dist copy whose Node fake-worker registration collides with the real top-level instance `pdf-to-img` uses. Explicitly resetting `GlobalWorkerOptions.workerSrc` did not fix it. Fixed by isolating the rasterization step in a fresh child process (new `services/rasterizeWorker.ts`, spawned via `child_process.execFile` from `yandexVision.ts`'s `rasterizePdfPages` when a compiled sibling exists; falls back to the original in-process call under local `tsx`, where this was never reproduced) — a fresh process never ran `pdf-parse`, so it never inherits the corruption. Verified against the real compiled production code path before and after.
- **Silent LLM-call error swallowing in two extraction services produced garbage empty drafts indistinguishable from "this document genuinely has no content".** Same incident, deeper cause: after the OCR fix above, ФГОС re-imports were *still* coming back empty. Traced to `services/fgosExtractor.ts` (and, found by the same audit, `services/brsScheme.ts`) wrapping their `chatJSON` call in a bare `catch { return empty }` — a failed LLM call (here, DeepSeek returning HTTP 402, account balance depleted) silently produced a fake-successful empty draft that got persisted as a real (garbage) row, with zero log line anywhere. Audited every other AI-generation service in the codebase (grading, presentations, quizzes, topics, tasks, syllabus authoring, curriculum/program analysis, market-evidence generation) — none of them have this pattern; it was isolated to these two files. Removed the swallow in both — a failed call now propagates and surfaces as a real error to the admin (the frontend's bulk-import loop already handled per-item failures correctly, it just never got the chance before). 2 tests updated to assert the error now propagates instead of resolving to empty.
- **РОП Студия's one pre-existing evidence row crashed the page after the region-array migration.** Migration 090 converted `region_code`/`region_name` to `region_codes`/`region_names` arrays but left `vacancy_snapshot`'s JSONB *contents* in the old flat shape, while the new multi-region frontend expected the nested-by-region shape and called `region.by_profession.map(...)` on it — threw `region.by_profession.map is not a function`, taking down the whole page (caught via live browser verification, not by any test — no test exercised a pre-existing row through the new code path). Fixed with migration `091_market_evidence_snapshot_shape.sql`, which wraps any still-flat snapshot into a single-region `RegionSnapshot` using the row's own already-migrated `region_codes[0]`/`region_names[0]`; guarded idempotent so it's a no-op on rows already in the new shape.
- **Scanned PDFs with CCITT-encoded pages OCR'd to nothing, silently — shared by grading, program import, and ФГОС ingestion alike.** Found while scoping Feature Z's Phase 0 pilot (направление 15.03.02, whose fgosvo.ru document turned out to be exactly this case). Two independent bugs: (1) `services/documentExtractor.ts`'s scanned-PDF detector counted `pdf-parse`'s own per-page markers as "words" (`-- 1 of 23 --` × 23 pages = 115 tokenised "words" from zero real content), sailing past the 50-word OCR trigger — now counts runs of 2+ Unicode letters instead. (2) Yandex Vision's PDF ingestion can't decode CCITT Group 4-encoded images (the standard compression for scanned government documents) — confirmed directly, `batchAnalyze` returned `"Can't decode Image: image: unknown format"` on a perfectly valid PDF. Fixed by rasterizing every page to PNG (`pdf-to-img`, new dependency) before OCR, which also removes the old 8-page-per-call cap Vision silently applied to raw PDF content; replaces the now-unused `pdf-lib`-based chunking. Needed a `pdfjs-dist` version `overrides` pin too — `pdf-parse` and `pdf-to-img` each pull their own copy, and two different versions loaded into the same Node process made pdfjs-dist's internal consistency check fail ("API version does not match Worker version"); applying the override against an already-committed lockfile hit a known npm bug requiring a full clean reinstall (`rm -rf node_modules package-lock.json && npm install`), not just a plain one. 7 new unit tests (`yandexVision.test.ts`, `documentExtractor.test.ts`). **Re-verified end-to-end against the real 15.03.02 PDF**: OCR'd all 23 scanned pages, extracted 25 competencies, 16 профстандарты, and 4 structure requirements — all correctly scoped to the direction (машиностроение standards, not noise).
- **ФГОС review screen was squeezing long fields (competency formulations, block labels) into unreadable slivers, reading as missing/truncated data.** `AdminFgos.tsx`'s shared `inputCls` baked in `w-full`, which silently beat a narrower `w-XX` appended at each field's call site (Tailwind orders `w-full` after the numeric width scale in its generated stylesheet) — the DB always held the full data, this was purely a rendering bug. Fixed by dropping `w-full` from the shared class. Also raised `fgosExtractor.ts`'s `MAX_TEXT_CHARS` from 48000 to 120000 — a real 12-page ФГОС already extracts to ~33-36k chars, leaving almost no headroom for the format's documented 20-40 page range, which could truncate the профстандарты appendix (near the end of the document) away before the LLM saw it on longer documents. Confirmed OCR for scanned ФГОС PDFs already works with no new code — `services/documentExtractor.ts`'s shared `extractText()` (used by grading, program import, and ФГОС ingestion alike) already falls back to `yandexVisionOCR()` when a PDF's text layer yields under 50 words.
- **Integration test harness: `pool.connect()`-based transactions were silently leaking rows into the shared test DB, on every run (found while verifying Feature AA, above).** The per-test `BEGIN`/`ROLLBACK` isolation (`vitest.setup.integration.ts` + `DB_POOL_MAX=1`) didn't nest against query functions that open their own transaction via `pool.connect()` (`createOrgUnit`, `moveOrgUnit`, AA's `createFgosStandardDraft`/`publishFgosStandard`, and others) — with pool size 1, the inner `COMMIT` committed the *outer* test transaction too, so `afterEach`'s `ROLLBACK` had nothing left to undo. Confirmed long-standing and real: the test DB had accumulated hundreds of orphaned `institutions`/`org_units`/`teachers` rows, invisible until now because no prior integration test asserted an exact/empty row count. Fixed with zero production code changes — new `db/__tests__/transactionalTestIsolation.ts` patches `pool.connect()` inside integration test setup only, rewriting nested `BEGIN`/`COMMIT`/`ROLLBACK` (tracked by real transaction depth) into `SAVEPOINT`/`RELEASE SAVEPOINT`/`ROLLBACK TO SAVEPOINT`. New regression test proves a `pool.connect()`-based write is genuinely rolled back; verified via two consecutive full integration suite runs showing row counts flat at 0 (previously grew every run). See TODO.md Feature AA for the full three-bug debugging writeup.
- **Domain-scoped grants, Phase 3 slice B — subtree-scoped org tree CRUD + role grants (Research.md §7.10; TODO P(c)).** An institute director can now hold `admin × all` on their own division and manage their piece of the org structure — create/rename/move/delete kafedry, grant roles — without being an institution-root admin and without touching any other institute. Re-scoped from the original, much wider "platform narrowing" framing after confirming real institutional practice (KNITU, likely others): **teacher invite/deactivation is centrally owned by IT, never delegated per department.** So invites, teacher activate/deactivate, primary-department reassignment, and LTI/model/shared-RAG settings stay `platform`-domain, root-admin-only, institution-wide — permanently decided, not deferred.
  - `routes/orgUnits.ts`'s gate swaps `requireInstitutionAdmin` for `requireDomain('platform', 'admin')` — safe because `role='admin'` is always `domain='all'` (Phase 1 invariant), so every existing root admin is unaffected; it additionally lets a sub-unit admin grant through the door.
  - New `unitInScope` helper (alongside the existing `unitInInstitution`) does the real per-target check via `pathIsAncestorOrSelf`, applied to every write endpoint's target unit(s) — including **both sides of a move** (can't pull a unit in from outside your subtree; can't move one of yours out to somewhere you no longer control).
  - `listOrgUnitsWithCounts` and `listInstitutionMembersWithRoles` gained the same optional `unitPathPrefixes` filtering pattern as slice A's teaching-domain queries — a sub-unit admin's tree view and role-grant picker show only their own subtree/people.
  - `PUT /api/institution/structure/members/:teacherId/primary` gets an explicit `requireInstitutionAdmin` override even though the router's coarse gate now admits sub-unit admins — reassigning which department a teacher belongs to stays centrally-owned, matching the invite/deactivate decision above.
  - No new escalation-guard logic needed for role grants: delegating `admin × all` on a descendant unit only grants power within that descendant's own subtree, by the same path-containment math `canActOnUnit` already does for every other domain check.
  - Frontend: `curriculum_access`/`teaching_access` gating generalised to a third `platform_access` field (same shape, same `adminFlags()` pattern); «Структура» in `InstitutionLayout`'s NAV requires `platform` domain at `admin` level.
  - 7 new integration tests (`domainAccess.integration.test.ts`): in-subtree CRUD succeeds; out-of-subtree CRUD (including the institution root itself) is refused; both directions of an out-of-scope move are refused; role grant/revoke works within scope and is refused outside it; list endpoints return only the granted subtree; the primary-unit-reassignment exclusion holds; a true root admin is unaffected.
  - Verified live in the browser: seeded a division + kafedra-under-it + a division-scoped admin, confirmed the admin could create a kafedra and grant a role within their institute, and was refused acting on units outside it.
  - Still explicitly deferred, unrelated to the narrowing above: RPD monitor subtree scoping (its `dept_code`-based schema has no join path to `org_units` at all — needs new schema) and criteria/rubrics share-target scoping (still hardcoded to always share to root).
- **Domain-scoped grants, Phase 3 slice A — subtree query scoping for `teaching` (Research.md §7.10; TODO P(c)).** A sub-unit grant (e.g. an institute director holding `view × teaching × their-division`, grantable since Phase 2's role-assignment UI) previously saw **institution-wide** data on `/api/institution/overview`, `/usage/daily`, and `/teachers` despite being scoped to their own division — `requireDomain` only checked "does this grant exist," never *where*. The query layer now consumes the `pathPrefixes` a grant carries.
  - `db/queries/institutions.ts`: `listInstitutionTeachers`, `getInstitutionOverview`, `getInstitutionDailyUsage` gain an optional `unitPathPrefixes` param, filtering via `teachers.primary_org_unit_id → org_units.path`.
  - `routes/institution.ts`: new `resolveTeachingPrefixes(req)` decides what to pass — the subtlety that makes this safe: **root-anchored grants (every grant issued to date) stay unrestricted**, not filtered by "root path." Filtering by the institution root's own path would have incorrectly excluded any teacher with no `primary_org_unit_id` (a real, if rare, stale-reassignment edge case) that the unrestricted query has always correctly shown. Verified live in the browser: seeded a division, a kafedra outside it, a teacher in each, and a division-scoped `view × teaching` grant — the roster and Обзор counts correctly showed only the in-subtree teacher.
  - **Two more domain-blindness gaps found and closed alongside** (same class of bug as the Phase 2 leadership-dashboard leak, lower practical severity here): `getProgramAccessScope` (`services/programAccess.ts`) and `canTeacherShareToUnit` (`db/queries/orgUnits.ts`) both matched on `role IN ('admin','edit')` with no domain filter. Since Phase 2's role-grant UI now offers the `teaching` domain at `edit` level too, a hypothetical future `teaching`-domain edit grant would have silently unlocked program-editing and rubric/criterion-sharing rights it was never meant to have (both are curriculum-domain surfaces). Both now require `domain IN ('all', 'curriculum')`.
  - New integration tests (`domainAccess.integration.test.ts`): a sub-unit `teaching` grant only sees its subtree's roster/overview counts; a root-anchored grant remains unrestricted (regression guard for the subtlety above).
  - **Explicitly deferred, not attempted this session** (each is a separate design problem — see TODO P(c) slice B for the full reasoning): RPD monitor subtree scoping (its schema has no join path from `dept_code` to `org_units` at all — needs new schema, not a filter); criteria/rubrics share-target scoping (currently hardcoded to always share to root); and true "platform narrowing" for sub-unit admins (org tree CRUD, invites, roster edit, LTI/model settings) — invites in particular have no unit concept today, and what a division admin should be allowed to touch on platform settings is a product question, not plumbing.
- **Domain-scoped grants, Phase 2 — the `teaching` domain (Research.md §7.10; TODO P(c)).** ПР УР (or similar) can now hold wide **read-only** access to usage analytics, grading activity, the leadership dashboard, and teacher roster reads — `view × teaching × root` — without being an institution-root admin and without leaking into `curriculum` or `platform` surfaces.
  - **Real cross-domain leak found and closed in this change, on already-shipped Phase 1 code:** `teacherCanActOnUnit` (backing `hasLeadershipRole`, `listDirectLeadershipUnits`, and both per-target checks in `routes/leadership.ts`) predates the domain axis and matched on `role` alone. Consequence: a Phase 1 `curriculum`-domain grant (e.g. a УМЦ head with `edit × curriculum × root`) already reached the leadership dashboard — grading activity, teacher rosters, subject data, institution-wide — because `role IN ('edit','admin')` doesn't care which domain that role was granted in. Fixed by making `canActOnUnit`/`teacherCanActOnUnit`/`evaluateAccess`/`requireUnitRole` all take a **required** `domain` parameter (no unsafe "any domain" default — every caller must now say which domain it's checking). `hasLeadershipRole` and `listDirectLeadershipUnits` gained `AND our.domain IN ('all','teaching')` and widened their role check to include `view` (every route they gate is read-only, so `view` is sufficient). Covered by a dedicated regression test (`domainAccess.integration.test.ts`) asserting a curriculum-only grant is refused on `/api/leadership/overview`.
  - **`routes/institution.ts`**: `GET /overview`, `/usage/daily`, `/usage/export`, `/teachers` (roster read only) move ahead of the router-wide admin gate with their own `requireDomain('teaching', 'view')`, mirroring Phase 1's criteria/rubrics split. `PATCH /teachers/:id`, invites, `/audit`, and every settings route stay admin-only, unchanged.
  - **`routes/auth.ts`**: `adminFlags()` gains `teaching_access`, reusing the `getAccessScope` call already made for `curriculum_access` — no extra query.
  - **Frontend**: `Sidebar`, `InstitutionRoute`, and `InstitutionLayout`'s NAV/redirect logic generalised from curriculum-only to multi-domain (a lookup keyed by each NAV item's declared domain instead of one hardcoded field). `InstitutionStructure.tsx`'s domain picker gained the `teaching` option (the label already existed, unused, since Phase 1).
  - **Two more stale rename leftovers found and fixed while verifying in the browser**: `Leadership.tsx` and `InstitutionAudit.tsx` each had a `ROLE_LABEL` map still keyed on the pre-Phase-1 `head`/`viewer` values — missed by the Phase 1 rename sweep because they're unquoted object keys (`head: '...'`), not string literals, so the original `grep "'head'"` pass didn't catch them. Without this, an `edit`- or `view`-level role would have rendered as the raw English word instead of a Russian label. Also found and fixed: `listDirectLeadershipUnits` (the leadership-dashboard unit picker) had the exact same role-only, domain-blind, `edit`/`admin`-only query as `hasLeadershipRole` — without fixing it too, a `view × teaching` grant would pass the discoverability gate but see an empty picker, rendering "no leadership roles" despite being authorised. Caught by live browser verification, not by the test suite (both gaps were outside the plan's originally-identified call sites).
- **Domain-scoped grants, Phase 1 — the `curriculum` domain (Research.md §7.10; TODO P(c)).** Fixes the live УМЦ over-privilege: reaching РПД monitoring used to require full institution-root admin (teacher invites, LTI/SSO, LLM model choice, audit log — everything), because the shipped org-tree model (§7.3) only expresses line-management authority (role level × subtree containment), not functional authority. A grant is now **(level: admin/edit/view) × (domain: platform/curriculum/teaching/…) × (unit subtree)**; a УМЦ head can hold `edit × curriculum × root` and reach exactly РПД monitor + institution criteria/rubrics, nothing else.
  - **Migration `087_org_role_domains.sql`**: `org_unit_roles` gains `domain TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'all'` (pure-widening — every existing row is `'all'`, which the resolver expands across every concrete domain, so every current institution-root admin's access is untouched) and the uniqueness key becomes `(teacher_id, org_unit_id, role, domain)`. Rides along in the same migration (§7.10.3, decided this session): the level values rename `head`→`edit`, `viewer`→`view` — "head" was a job title, not a permission level, and read wrong once the same level is held by functional staff who don't "head" any unit. One mechanical rename across `orgUnits.ts`, `leadership.ts` (queries + routes), `programAccess.ts`, validation, and the frontend role-assignment UI.
  - **New `services/accessScope.ts`** (`getAccessScope`, `levelAtLeast`): resolves a teacher's full domain scope in one query, reusing `listRoleScopesForTeacher`. **New `middleware/requireDomain.ts`**: platform-admin bypass, else checks the resolved scope, same pattern as `requireProgramAccess`.
  - **Routes**: `rpdMonitor.ts` swaps `requireInstitutionAdmin` for `requireDomain('curriculum', 'edit')`. `institution.ts`'s criteria/rubrics GET/POST routes move ahead of the router-wide admin gate with their own `requireDomain` (GET needs `'view'`, POST needs `'edit'`) — everything else in that file (teachers, invites, audit, LTI, model, settings) stays admin-only, unchanged. `orgUnits.ts`'s role-grant/revoke endpoints accept an optional `domain` (default `'all'`).
  - **Privilege-escalation guard found and closed during implementation**: `isInstitutionAdmin` (which drives `requireInstitutionAdmin`) checked only `role = 'admin'`, not domain — so granting `role='admin', domain='curriculum'` at root would have silently unlocked full institution admin, defeating the whole point of scoping. Fixed by requiring `domain = 'all'` explicitly, plus a belt-and-suspenders validation rule refusing an `admin`-level grant paired with any domain other than `'all'` (admin is always full-scope in this model). `countRoleOnUnit`'s last-admin lockout guard got the same `domain='all'` filter for the same reason. Covered by a dedicated regression test (`domainAccess.integration.test.ts`).
  - **Frontend**: `InstitutionRoute`, `Sidebar`, and `InstitutionLayout` gate on a new `teacher.curriculum_access` field (computed server-side in `adminFlags()`, mirroring the existing `program_access` pattern) instead of only `is_institution_admin`. `InstitutionLayout`'s NAV filters to what the grant's level actually reaches (a `view`-only grant doesn't see the РПД tab, which requires `edit`) and redirects away from any admin-only sub-route (index Обзор, `/institution/teachers`, etc.) to the first tab the grant can use, rather than rendering a form that would just 403 on submit. The role-assignment UI (`InstitutionStructure.tsx`) gained a domain picker (disabled/forced to `'all'` for `admin`-level grants) and its overlap/redundancy hint is now domain-aware (an ancestor grant only makes a descendant grant redundant when their domains actually overlap).
  - 7 new integration tests (`domainAccess.integration.test.ts`) + 7 new unit tests (`accessScope.test.ts`) covering the grant resolution, the domain gate, and the privilege-escalation regression specifically.
  - Phases 2 (`teaching` domain, read-only ПР УР width) and 3 (`platform` narrowing + per-subtree query scoping) remain deferred — see TODO Feature P(c).
- **Bulk РПД discovery from the university's /sveden/education page (TODO Feature AD) — one paste instead of ~44.** The shipped paste-a-link pull works per document, but a programme's disclosure row lists ~40+ individual РПД links, so the РОП still pasted them one at a time. New «Импортировать со страницы сведений» on the programme's Документы tab: paste the URL of the mandated «Сведения об образовательной организации → Образование» page → server fetches it (**new `fetchPageHtml` in `services/documentFetch.ts`** — same allowlist + SSRF/redirect discipline as the document fetcher, text/html-only, 4MB cap) → **new dependency-free parser `services/svedenParser.ts`** (deliberately no cheerio/jsdom — new deps need a manual install; the tables are flat enough for tag-level segmentation) reads the Рособрнадзор-mandated `itemprop` microdata (Приказ № 831 — the same markup universities are audited on) with text/href-keyword fallbacks for imperfect markup → the programme's row is selected by направление code (then name, then only-row) → checklist manifest returned by **new `POST /:id/documents/discover`** (`routes/programs.ts`). Cell-level `itemprop` hints are what separate the РПД column from the «Аннотации» column, whose links carry *identical discipline-name texts* — annotations are skipped, never imported as РПД. РПД link texts (which ARE the discipline names, underscore convention undone) are matched against the programme's disciplines: exact normalized match (case/ё/punctuation-insensitive), else unique token-Jaccard ≥ 0.6, else null — the user picks from a dropdown, never a below-threshold guess. Практики are classified into the fixed 4-type set only on unambiguous keywords («учебная технологическая» deliberately does NOT map to the production type — user picks). **The import loop is client-driven, not a pg-boss job** (a deliberate deviation from the TODO entry's sketch): the confirmed checklist feeds each item sequentially through the *existing* `uploadProgramDocument` + `file_url` endpoint — per-item progress and retry for free, zero new job infrastructure, closing mid-run just leaves already-imported docs in place (re-running discovery is idempotent: supersede-on-reupload for РПД, replace-per-type for practices). Items that would replace an existing current file default to unchecked. New `SvedenImportModal.tsx`; 19 unit tests (`svedenParser.test.ts`) covering the annotation/РПД name collision, the «Рабочая_программа» header-link demotion, practice-type refusal cases, no-markup fallback, script-content ignoring, and ambiguous-name null returns.
- **Fixes from first real-world test of bulk РПД discovery (above), against the actual КНИТУ sveden page.** Three real bugs surfaced, none hypothetical:
  1. **Modal positioning bug, root-caused and fixed via React Portal.** The РОП reported the confirmation modal rendering off-screen/unreachable on a long discipline list. Two earlier fix attempts (`overflow:hidden` body lock, then the iOS-safe `position:fixed` body-pin technique) didn't address it — a screenshot showed the dimmed backdrop rendering correctly but the modal box itself never appearing, ruling out both scroll-lock theories. `SvedenImportModal` now renders via `createPortal(…, document.body)` instead of in-place in the component tree — makes it a DOM sibling of the whole app rather than a descendant of the programme page, so nothing in that page's ancestor chain (however it evolves) can ever again break its `position: fixed` centering. Standard pattern real modal libraries (Radix, MUI, Headless UI) use for exactly this class of bug.
  2. **Wrong text encoding — real universities are not all UTF-8.** КНИТУ's sveden page serves `windows-1251`; `fetchPageHtml` was decoding every response as UTF-8 unconditionally, which would have silently turned every Cyrillic discipline name into mojibake. New `charsetFromContentType`/`charsetFromMetaTag`/`decodeHtml` in `services/documentFetch.ts` — reads the declared charset from the `Content-Type` header, else sniffs a `<meta charset>`/legacy `http-equiv` tag from the first 4KB (always ASCII-safe regardless of body encoding), else UTF-8; an unrecognised label falls back to UTF-8 rather than throwing. Node's built-in `TextDecoder` handles `windows-1251` natively, no new dependency.
  3. **Page size/timing limits were sized for a hypothetical, not the real thing.** КНИТУ's sveden page is 13+ MB and takes 2+ minutes to generate server-side (the page's own on-screen banner warns "считывается большой объём информации за 6 лет, подождите"). `MAX_HTML_SIZE` raised 4MB → 24MB, a separate `PAGE_FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS` (240s, vs. the unchanged 30s for individual document downloads) replaces the shared timeout, the frontend's request timeout raised to 260s to clear it, and the modal now shows a "some universities' pages take a few minutes, this is normal" note during the wait.
  - **Also found and fixed while validating end-to-end against the real page:** the real markup wraps each link's `itemprop` (and the row's `eduCode`/`eduName`) in a `<span itemprop="…">` around the element rather than tagging the `<td>`/`<a>` directly — `extractAnchors` and the row-identity scan now also check a directly-wrapping `<span>`. And a «методические и иные документы» column (itemprop `methodology`, files like `Metod_KR(KP)_KNITU.pdf`) was falling through to the document-URL default and getting counted as an unmatched РПД — `methodology`/`Copy` itemprops now explicitly classify as `other`. 10 new unit tests (`documentFetch.test.ts` charset suite, `svedenParser.test.ts`'s real-markup-shape test) plus an end-to-end run against the actual downloaded КНИТУ page during implementation.
  4. **Multi-year pages silently mixed six years of rows together — a follow-up bug found when the user asked "which year is this pulling from?".** КНИТУ's sveden page bundles ALL years (2021–2026) into one server response behind client-side JS tabs (`<div class="tab-pane" id="{year}">`), not six separate requests — and critically, the panels are **not** in the same order as the tab buttons (2021→2026 ascending in the DOM, while the tab bar shows 2026 first/active). "First matching row wins" was therefore picking whichever year happened to appear earliest in 13MB of concatenated markup — verified to be the *oldest* year, 2021, not the page's own visible default. New `splitYearPanels`/multi-year-aware `parseSvedenPage` (now returns `{ rows, availableYears, selectedYear }`) scopes parsing to one year's panel — defaulting to the page's own active tab (`class="...active..."` on the panel itself), with an optional `requestedYear` param to re-run against a different one. Single-year sites (the majority) have no such markup and parse unaffected. `POST /:id/documents/discover` gains an optional `year` field and returns `available_years`/`selected_year`; the modal shows a year `<select>` (only rendered when >1 year exists) that re-runs discovery on change.
  5. **A code is not a unique key — found while validating year-scoping against real data.** КНИТУ splits one код+eduName (e.g. `01.03.02 Прикладная математика и информатика`) into several rows, one per **профиль** (specialisation, itemprop `eduProf` — e.g. «Прикладная математика и информатика» itself as the base профиль, and «Искусственный интеллект и большие данные» as a second one), each with its *own* document set. `selectProgramRow` previously took the first code match unconditionally — confirmed on real 2026 data to silently return the wrong профиль's (much smaller) РПД set under the правильно-looking programme name. New `SvedenProgramRow.profile` field (same wrapping-`<span>`-aware extraction as eduCode/eduName); when a code matches multiple rows, disambiguates by matching the programme's own name against профиль first, then eduName, **only** acting on a unique match — otherwise refuses and surfaces all candidates (now labelled with профиль) for the user to pick manually rather than ever guessing among them. 7 new tests covering the real base-профиль-shares-eduName shape and the genuinely-ambiguous case. Sanity-checked across 2022–2025 (each: 48–49 РПД, mature curricula) vs. 2026 (1 РПД — a brand-new intake year with most files not yet uploaded, confirmed as real site state, not a parsing gap).
- **Loading feedback for the bulk РПД discovery wait (above).** A static "Ищем документы…" label sitting motionless for the 2+ minutes some universities' pages take reads as broken. The discovering phase now shows a spinner, a live elapsed-time counter (ticking every second), and an indeterminate sliding progress bar (`prefers-reduced-motion`-aware) — honest "still working" feedback since a single request has no real percentage to report.
- **Мониторинг РПД — turn a weekly ~2hr manual Excel rebuild for the head of УМЦ into a ~5min upload.** She currently downloads the «Заполнение РПД и ФОС» table from АСУ Университет (as a legacy .doc — its cell contents are hand-transcribed into a personal Excel workbook, split into 8 per-institute sheets from a кафедра→институт mapping that only exists in her head, with hand-computed rollups, growth-vs-last-week deltas, and manual highlighting) and writes separate долг reminder notices to lagging institutes. New institution-admin page **Мониторинг РПД** (`frontend/src/pages/institution/RpdMonitor.tsx`, `/institution/rpd`, gated by new `rpdMonitor` plan flag — institution tier only) replaces the whole loop: upload the АСУ export (.xlsx **or** the .doc she actually downloads) → parsed and stored as a dated snapshot (`rpd_snapshots`/`rpd_snapshot_rows`, migration `086_rpd_monitor.sql`) → per-institute rollups + week-over-week deltas fall out of comparing snapshots automatically. **Parser** (`services/rpdMonitor.ts`): the .xlsx path (exceljs) handles both her master «сводка» workbook and a lone per-institute export by locating the «Уровень образования» header cell and reading columns positionally from there. The .doc path is the harder case — legacy Word tables encode cell boundaries as a literal separator baked into the document's own text stream (reproduced by `word-extractor`, confirmed cross-checked against `textutil -convert html` on the real production file, which additionally proved this is an intrinsic property of the .doc binary format, not a converter artifact) — split primarily on that; if it doesn't survive extraction, falls back to a constraint-based digit decoder that reconstructs the three (count, %) pairs per field from the concatenated-digits blob alone, using `done+review+debt===план` and 1-decimal percent rounding as constraints (round-half-up done via integer-cents arithmetic to dodge float drift at `.x5` boundaries) — validated against every one of 221 real rows including negative-долг anomalies (на-проверке exceeding план) and overcompletion (план revised down after work already logged, done > план). Percentages are always recomputed server-side, never trusted from АСУ's own % columns (confirmed to contain rounding drift and the anomalies above) — data-quality flags are surfaced per row, never silently corrected. The кафедра→институт mapping (`rpd_dept_groups`/`rpd_dept_group_members`) is assigned once via inline UI chips, or bootstrapped by uploading one of her existing per-institute files so the code list is learned automatically (`learnDeptCodesFromWorkbook`) — reused the same parser. Dashboard: KPI totals with delta badges, per-institute table sorted by долг with drill-through downloads, a «проблемные кафедры» panel (outstanding долг + stalled-since-last-snapshot flag), snapshot history (editable date, delete, re-select). **Exports**: a master workbook (exceljs) mirroring her hand-built one — «Итоги» rollup + dynamics table, full «Сводка», one sheet per institute, colour-coded (green/red/yellow) same as her manual scheme — plus a standalone per-institute workbook, and per-institute reminder letters (.docx via the existing `docx` lib pattern from `fosExport.ts` — a долг table is deterministic, only the opening paragraph is LLM-drafted via the multi-provider registry, new `rpd_reminder` feature tag; a plain-text/copy view ships alongside since she may not want to send from a downloaded file). Verified end-to-end against Daniel's real production files (`backend/src/services/__fixtures__/rpd/`, also `rpdMonitor.test.ts`): parsed totals cross-checked by hand against her own workbook's `SUM()` formulas and side-panel dashboard numbers, matched exactly for 7 of 8 institutes (the 8th differed because her side-panel snapshot was from a different week than the standalone file — confirmed by independently hand-summing that file's own rows).
- **Add programme documents by pasting a link instead of downloading + re-uploading.** Users kept having to find a document in their university's web system (e.g. `https://www.kstu.ru/getpilot?id=478501`), download it, then re-upload it to ИСПУМ. Now they can paste that link and the server fetches the file itself, feeding it into the exact same pipeline an upload uses (extract text → store original → insert `program_documents` / parse intake). Covers all programme document types: per-discipline РПД (рабочая программа), practices, and the intake-form описание ОП + учебный план. **Security — two independent layers.** (1) **Private-IP SSRF guard** (`documentFetch.ts`): before connecting, the host is DNS-resolved and refused if any address is private / loopback / link-local / CGNAT / reserved / multicast — IPv4 and IPv6, including IPv4-mapped (`::ffff:…`) and NAT64 (`64:ff9b::/96`) forms and the `169.254.169.254` cloud-metadata endpoint — via a pure, fail-closed `isBlockedIp` classifier. The connection is **pinned to the validated IP** through a custom agent `lookup` (net connects to the exact address we vetted while TLS still verifies the original hostname), so DNS rebinding can't swap in an internal IP between check and connect; the check runs on every redirect hop. This is the real anti-SSRF control — it holds **even when the allowlist itself is attacker-controlled** (a compromised institution-admin account adding a hostname like `localtest.me` that resolves to `127.0.0.1`, verified blocked end-to-end). (2) **Domain allowlist** — the usability / misconfiguration guard governing which hosts a *user* may target — managed **per-institution by the institution admin** on the org overview page (new `institutions.document_fetch_domains`, migration `085_institution_document_fetch_domains.sql`) — deliberately **decoupled from `email_domain`** (which now means email auto-join only; overloading it was both a semantic wart and a platform-admin-only bottleneck). An optional env supplement (`DOCUMENT_FETCH_ALLOWED_DOMAINS`) folds a global domain list into every institution's allowlist. The `PATCH /api/institution/document-domains` route normalises each entry to a bare hostname (`normalizeDomain` strips a pasted scheme/path/port/`www.`, validates the shape, caps at 25, audits the change). Guardrails in `services/documentFetch.ts`: https-only; host must match an allowed domain or be a subdomain of one (`isHostAllowed`, careful about the `notkstu.ru`/`kstu.ru.evil.com` look-alike traps); **every redirect hop is re-validated against the allowlist** (an allowlisted URL that 302s elsewhere must not bypass the gate — this is why redirects are followed manually with `maxRedirects: 0`); a 20 MB size cap + 30 s timeout; and the real content type is sniffed from magic bytes (new exported `detectMimeFromBuffer`, reused from the upload path) rather than trusted from the `Content-Type` header. The fetch returns a multer-file-shaped object so every downstream step (`extractText`, `attachProgramDocument`, competency auto-detect, intake parsing) is unchanged — the routes just resolve their file from an uploaded part **or** a `*_url` field. Frontend adds a reusable "или по ссылке" affordance (`UrlUploadField`, styled as a clear amber action link with a chain icon after early feedback that a muted-gray version was invisible) next to each existing file picker (РПД row, practices, intake описание/план), and an allowlist editor card on the institution overview page. The РПД document row's action cluster was also reworked from a run of same-colour text links into discrete chip buttons with hierarchy (`RowActionChip` — the review action is a filled amber chip only while unchecked, then calms to neutral; the show/hide toggles get a rotating chevron). 33 unit tests (`documentFetch.test.ts`) cover the private-IP classifier (v4/v6 ranges, mapped/NAT64 smuggling, fail-closed on garbage), allowlist host matching, redirect look-alike guards, domain normalisation (incl. rejecting raw IP literals), filename derivation, magic-byte detection, and pre-flight URL validation — plus an end-to-end check that a DNS name resolving to loopback is refused.
- **РПД year-over-year diff (Research.md §9.6) — «Что изменилось с прошлого года».** Re-uploading a discipline's рабочая программа used to hard-delete the previous `program_documents` row (`deleteWorkingProgrammeForDiscipline`), cascading away its coverage review too — there was nothing left to compare a new upload against. New `superseded_at TIMESTAMPTZ` column (migration `084_program_document_versions.sql`) switches this to a soft supersede: `supersedeWorkingProgrammeForDiscipline` marks the old row instead of deleting it (object storage keeps the file too), guarded by a partial unique index so exactly one row per discipline stays "current" (`superseded_at IS NULL`) — same convention as migration 054's practice-type uniqueness. `listProgramDocuments` now returns full history (current + superseded) so the Documents tab can build a per-discipline version count/list from data it already fetches, no new list endpoint needed; the business-logic reads that must stay pinned to the current file (`findWorkingProgrammeForDiscipline`, `listWorkingProgrammesByDiscipline`, review, студия bridge, plan-analysis embed enrichment) got an explicit `superseded_at IS NULL` filter. Once a discipline has ≥2 versions, a new **«Что изменилось с прошлого года»** button (`DisciplineDocumentRow`) calls `POST /:id/disciplines/:disciplineId/diff`, which finds the current + immediately-previous version and runs a new `services/programDiff.ts` — one `chatJSON` call modeled directly on `documentReview.ts`'s coverage check (same section-aware slicing via `selectRelevantSections`, same verbatim-quote evidence contract via the now-exported `validateEvidence`) — producing a **structured** report (added/removed/changed topics, competency-code changes, assessment-form changes), not a text diff; explicitly told to return `unchanged: true` rather than manufacture findings from paraphrasing/reformatting. Cached per `(old_document_id, new_document_id)` pair in new `program_document_diffs` so reopening the panel doesn't re-run the LLM call. **Correctness fix required by the retention change itself:** since re-upload no longer deletes the old review row, `getLatestReviewByDiscipline`/`ByDiscipline` would otherwise keep surfacing last year's coverage check against this year's un-reviewed file — `DocumentsPanel` and `DisciplineCoverageSection` now only treat a review as current when its `document_id` matches the discipline's current (non-superseded) document, falling back to "не проверено" otherwise. 6 new unit tests (`programDiff.test.ts`) covering evidence validation against the correct side (new text for added/changed, old text for removed).
- **Landing page now features the live QR quiz** as its own showcase block (between Тесты and Аналитика), with a projector-screen mockup (join code, live answer histogram) — previously the feature was only discoverable after signing up.
- **Quiz generator can now build a test straight from pasted lecture notes** instead of requiring a course with uploaded/RAG-indexed materials. New optional `source_text` field (`Quizzes.tsx` textarea, max 20k chars) on `POST /api/quizzes/generate`; when present, `services/quizzes.ts` skips the RAG chunk retrieval entirely and injects the sanitised pasted text into the prompt as the material to draw from, so questions no longer carry source citations in this mode (nothing to cite against). Course-linked RAG generation is unchanged when no text is pasted.
- **Live QR quiz results can now be saved to the grading journal, so a quiz can count toward a student's semester grade.** Previously a live-quiz score lived only in `live_sessions`/`live_participants`, completely isolated from the `assignments` table that powers История, student trajectory, and cohort analytics. New **«Сохранить в журнал»** panel on the finished screen (both `FinishedView` and `RosterView`): the teacher reviews each participant (a live-quiz participant only has a free-text nickname, not a real student identity), maps them to a real **ФИО студента**/**Группа** — prefilled from the nickname, with the exact same `<input list>`/`<datalist>` autocomplete `GradingForm.tsx` already uses, fed by `getStudents(courseId)` — picks which ones to include, and saves. Each selected participant becomes a real journal entry via the **same create → approve pipeline every other grade goes through** (`createAssignment` then `services/grading.ts`'s `approve()`, not a raw already-approved insert) — deliberately, so it's consistent with every other assignment and gets the same RAG-embedding/policy-memo/LTI-sync side effects. Score converts to the platform's Russian 5/4/3/2 scale (`scoreToGrade()` in `services/liveSessions.ts`, ≥90%→5, ≥75%→4, ≥60%→3, else→2) since student trajectory and cohort analytics are both built assuming that scale; `submission_text` (the one other `NOT NULL` column besides `teacher_id`) is synthesized (`«Живой тест «X» — N из M правильных ответов (P%).»`) since there's no essay. Course is inherited from the quiz's own `course_id` when it has one; only prompts the teacher to pick one otherwise. New `live_participants.assignment_id` (migration `083_live_session_journal_link.sql`) is the idempotency guard — the save endpoint silently skips any participant already linked, so re-opening the review screen and saving again never double-creates journal entries. 6 new unit tests for `scoreParticipant`/`scoreToGrade`.
- **Live QR quiz — self-paced mode + a batch of real-classroom bug fixes.** First live classroom test of the teacher-paced live quiz (below) surfaced one real product gap and several bugs.
  - **Self-paced mode**, chosen at launch (`Quizzes.tsx` gains a «В темпе группы» / «В своём темпе» toggle next to «Запустить в аудитории»): each participant now moves through the quiz at their own speed instead of the whole room being locked to whatever question the teacher is showing. Reuses the exact same `lobby|question|reveal|finished` client contract `LiveJoin.tsx` already had — a self-paced participant's status is *derived per-participant* server-side (`routes/liveJoin.ts`) from their own `live_participants.current_question_index`/`finished_at` (migration `082_live_session_mode.sql`) rather than the session-wide fields, so `question`/`reveal`/`finished` render with **zero changes** to the student-facing components; only a new **«Далее»** button on the reveal screen (calling `POST /api/live-join/:code/advance`) drives their own progression instead of waiting on the teacher. The teacher's projector screen gets a new `RosterView` (live per-participant progress list) replacing the histogram for this mode; «Завершить» only stops new joins — it deliberately never force-ends a student mid-quiz, their own `finished_at` does that independently.
  - **Fixed a real data bug: students saw "Сессия недоступна" instead of their results once the teacher finished the session.** `getLiveSessionByCode` excluded `status='finished'` sessions — correct for the *join* action, wrong for `/state` polling, which must keep working after finish so a student can see their score. Split: general lookup no longer filters by status; only `POST /:code/join` now explicitly rejects joining a finished session.
  - **Host projector screen was showing no question or answer text at all** — only letter badges and raw counts next to empty bars (`LiveSessionHost.tsx` never fetched the quiz). Now fetches it once via the existing `getQuiz()` and renders the actual question + option text on both the live histogram and the finished summary.
  - **Reveal now shows the student's own pick (highlighted red if wrong) and the question's explanation**, not just the correct answer — both were already generated and shown in the regular quiz-card view but missing from the live-session reveal. New `LiveJoinState.my_choice`/`.question.explanation` fields, backed by a new `getParticipantAnswers()` query reused for both this and the new final-score calculation below.
  - **Nickname is now required at join**, not optional — an anonymous roster made results unattributable to any student, defeating the purpose of the reveal/score screens. Validation only; no name-format checking.
  - **Students now see their own final score** (`N / total`) instead of just "Спасибо за участие!" — computed server-side by comparing their answers against the quiz's correct indices once the session reaches `finished`.
  - **The teacher had no way to see who got what — only a per-question aggregate (73% got Q3 right), never an individual student's score.** `LiveSessionParticipantProgress` gains `score: {correct, total}`, computed for *every* participant in *both* modes (previously the roster/score plumbing only existed for self-paced) via a new query (`getSessionParticipants`, one `json_object_agg` per participant instead of an N+1 `getParticipantAnswers` loop, since this now runs on every ~2s host poll). Teacher-paced `FinishedView` gains a **«Результаты участников»** leaderboard (highest score first) alongside the existing per-question breakdown; self-paced `RosterView` now shows each finished participant's score instead of just "готово".

- **Live QR quiz (TODO.md Feature Y, v1) — «Запустить в аудитории» runs an existing quiz live in the lecture hall.** New button on the quiz result screen (`Quizzes.tsx`) creates a session (`POST /api/live-sessions`) and opens a chrome-free projector view (`/live/host/:sessionId`, `LiveSessionHost.tsx` — reuses `ProtectedRoute` directly instead of `AppShell`, so it fills the whole screen) showing a join code + QR, a live per-option answer histogram (polled ~2s), and teacher-paced advance through `lobby → question → reveal → … → finished` (`services/liveSessions.ts`'s `nextStatus()`, a pure state machine, unit-tested in isolation). Students join anonymously — no account — via `/live/:code` (`LiveJoin.tsx`, eagerly imported like `StudentWrite.tsx` so a student on flaky venue wifi isn't stuck on a lazy-chunk fetch), answering with a server-issued `participant_token` as their sole credential; answer-once is enforced by a real DB unique constraint (`live_answers (participant_id, question_index)`), not client trust. New `live_sessions` / `live_participants` / `live_answers` tables (migration `081_live_quiz_sessions.sql`) and a fresh 6-char join-code generator (`ABCDEFGHJKMNPQRSTUVWXYZ23456789` — ambiguous characters `0/O/1/I/L` excluded — distinct from the 48-hex-char opaque tokens used for published-assignment invites, which are unusable projected on a screen). Router split mirrors `publishedAssignments.ts`/`publicWrite.ts` exactly: `routes/liveSessions.ts` (authenticated, teacher creates/controls) vs. `routes/liveJoin.ts` (wholly public, own unauthenticated axios client `api/liveJoin.ts` with no JWT/401-redirect). **New `liveLimiter`** (rate limiting, keyed by `participant_token` rather than IP — a whole lecture hall behind one campus IP would otherwise collide destructively across students, same reasoning as `ltiLimiter`'s campus-IP comment) replaces `generalLimiter` for both new routers via an extension to `generalLimiter`'s existing `skip` predicate — 200/15min would throttle a single 2s-polling device within minutes over a 45-90 minute lecture. Plan gating is a bespoke `checkLiveSessionMonthlyLimit` (new `liveSessionsPerMonth` flag: 1/month free, unlimited Pro/Institution) rather than an extension of `checkMonthlyLimit`'s closed feature union — avoids touching its shared Postgres `increment_usage` function for what's really a simple 1-vs-unlimited boundary. No new LLM calls or pg-boss job — pure CRUD/aggregation over the existing `quizzes` table, so no new generation capability, just a live delivery mechanism for what's already generated. v1 slice of the §3.1 live-lecture engagement layer (Research.md §9.5) — deliberately no WebRTC/ASR/confusion-heatmap. 6 unit tests (`liveSessions.test.ts`) covering the state machine, including the single-question-quiz edge case.
- **ФОС generator (TODO.md Feature X, v1) — «Собрать ФОС» assembles a discipline's фонд оценочных средств from existing generators.** One button on a course (`/fos`, new Materials hub card) generates паспорт ФОС, тестовые задания (reuses `services/quizzes.ts` as-is), практические задания/кейсы/проекты (reuses `services/tasks.ts` for all three kinds), экзаменационные билеты (`services/fosTickets.ts` — the one genuinely new generator: N билетов × 2 theory questions + 1 practical task, targeting full topic coverage), and критерии оценивания (reuses the teacher's own course-scoped criteria when present, else generates defaults) — all mapped to the course's topics with a **deterministic, no-LLM coverage self-check** (`services/fosCoverage.ts`) that flags any topic with zero instruments and any topic dominating >50% of tickets, rather than trusting the model's own coverage claim (same "never trust the model to self-report coverage" posture as `calcVerifier`/`citationChecker`). Runs as a pg-boss job (`fos-generation` queue, `services/fosGenerator.ts` orchestrator + `services/fosWorker.ts`, mirroring the long-review pattern exactly — 202 + poll, survives a PM2 restart mid-run, partial sections persist after every step so a crash doesn't lose earlier work) since it chains ~7 sequential LLM calls. New `fos_documents` table (migration `080_fos_documents.sql`, one row per generation run — a teacher regenerates each semester and keeps history, unlike `syllabus_studio_drafts`' single-row-per-course upsert). Sections are teacher-editable post-generation (autosave, same debounce pattern as РПД-студия) — AI drafts, teacher is author of record. Exports to branded PDF (`services/fosReportPdf.ts`, pdfkit, same design tokens as `programReportPdf.ts`) and editable DOCX (`services/fosExport.ts`, `docx` npm package v9.7.1 — its package.json says `"type": "module"` but `main` points at a UMD/CJS bundle, so it's requirable under this backend's CommonJS compile target with no shim, same shape as the pg-boss v10-vs-v12 check; verified by generating a real `.docx`, unzipping it, and confirming the Office Open XML content matches). New `fosGenerator` plan flag, Pro+. **v1 is teacher-scoped**: topics/competencies are extracted ad hoc from `courses.syllabus_text` (one cheap `chatJSON` pass) rather than sourced from the programme/org-tree competency model (`program_disciplines.competency_codes`) — that indicator-sourced, accreditation-grade version is the documented v2 follow-up. 15 unit tests (`fosTickets.test.ts`, `fosCoverage.test.ts`) covering ticket validation and the coverage/passport-row matching logic. See Research.md §9.2 for the original wow-feature framing.

### Fixed (production incident, 2026-07-15)
- **Presentation generation 500'd with an opaque "Expected ',' or ']'" JSON error on large lecture decks.** Caught via the new `CLIENT_ABORT` monitor firing alongside the `INTERNAL_ERROR` alert on `POST /api/presentations/generate` — the slide-generation `chatJSON` call in `services/presentations.ts` never set `maxTokens`, so it fell back to the provider default (~4096, same gotcha noted in `longReview.ts`'s synthesis call). A verbose lecture (each slide's notes + body runs 150–250 tokens) blew through that well before `slide_count_target`'s old ceiling of 40, truncating the model's JSON output mid-array; `JSON.parse` failed identically on both the original call and `chatJSON`'s built-in retry, since the retry re-sends the same oversized request rather than reducing scope — confirmed via `api_usage_log`, which showed `output_tokens` pinned at whatever ceiling was requested on *both* attempts. Initial fix (setting `maxTokens` from a per-slide estimate, capped at 8000) reduced but didn't eliminate the failure: a live repro with `slide_count_target: 38` still truncated, at almost the same output length as the original incident — the true bottleneck is deepseek/qwen's 8192 hard ceiling per non-reasoning completion, and no per-call `maxTokens` value fixes a request that structurally can't fit. New `presentationMaxTokens()` scales with `slideTarget` (~220 tokens/slide + 1500 buffer) capped at 8192 (yandex silently self-clamps to its own 8000 regardless of what's requested, so 8192 is safe to pass everywhere); `slide_count_target`'s validated max dropped from 40 → 30 (`validation/presentationValidation.ts`, `PresentationForm.tsx`) to match what actually fits — also now consistent with `estimateSlideCount()`'s own long-standing 30-slide ceiling for the no-override path, which had quietly never hit this bug. Verified live against the dev stack: a 38-slide request still truncates as expected (now blocked by validation before it reaches the model); the new max of 30 slides generated 33 valid slides in a single call using 6496/8192 output tokens — comfortable headroom. 4 unit tests for `presentationMaxTokens`.

### Changed
- **Feedback-email drafts open with «Добрый день!» instead of a formal «Уважаемый(ая) Имя Отчество».** `services/email.ts`'s prompt left the salutation to the model's own judgement, which defaulted to the formal patronymic form — risky when `student_name` is only a first name or the model gets the gender-agreement wrong (Уважаемый vs. Уважаемая). One added instruction line pins the greeting to a neutral, always-correct «Добрый день!» regardless of what name data is available.
- **Relaxed the "amber is the only accent" rule — it wasn't actually being followed.** Auditing it (prompted by the org-tree redesign below feeling cramped) found amber already doing double duty as brand/CTA colour *and* status colour in most of the app: a `scoreColor()` function duplicated near-verbatim across 5 files (`GradingResult.tsx`, `AssignmentDetailModal.tsx`, `StudentTrajectory.tsx`, `Students.tsx`, `InstitutionProgramDetail.tsx`) used amber for a score's middle band where `CurriculumConformance.tsx`'s own `scoreColor()` already correctly used `warning` — now all six agree, mid-band scores read as `warning`, amber is free to mean only "brand/action" again. `InstitutionProgramDetail.tsx`'s competency-coverage legend/heatmap (`LEVEL_META`) built its 3-step "введение → развитие → владение" progression from two amber alphas spliced to an unrelated `success` green; replaced with a real single-hue sequential scale (`success` at 18% → 45% → 100% opacity) so the ramp reads as one continuous "how much mastery" signal instead of an arbitrary colour jump on the last step. Left `SubmissionReview.tsx`'s `GRADE_COLOR` (the literal 5/4/3/2 Russian grade scale, success/amber/warning/danger) alone — that's a genuine 4-way categorical map, not the same duplicated-threshold bug.
- **New `ugsn` org-unit type + corrected programme-hierarchy terminology.** The taxonomy had the levels inverted relative to real Russian HE structure: `program` («Образовательная программа») was documented as the coarse УГСН grouping (e.g. 09.00.00) with `program_direction` («Направление подготовки») nested inside it — in reality УГСН (09.00.00) contains направления (09.03.04, the ФГОС level), which contain ОП/профили (e.g. «Искусственный интеллект и большие данные»). Added a dedicated `ugsn` type («УГСН» in the tree builder — grouping tier, collapsed by default, no ФГОС-metadata panel and never a programme anchor; no migration needed, `type_code` is unconstrained TEXT) and rewrote the misleading comments in `db/queries/orgUnits.ts`, `db/queries/programs.ts`, and `api/orgStructure.ts`. Nothing functional changes for existing trees: `program` ↔ `program_direction` remain an interchangeable programme-anchor pair everywhere (`programAccess`, РОП scope, pickers), hierarchy order was never enforced, and admins can re-type mislabelled units in place via the existing «Тип и размещение» panel.
