Type pickers now list the pair in hierarchy order (УГСН → Направление подготовки → Образовательная программа).
- **Teachers can share their own rubrics/criteria to an org unit (department, faculty, or whole institution) without an institution-admin step.** Previously `rubrics`/`criteria` only had `is_institution_shared` — all-or-nothing at the institution level, and only settable by an institution admin via `/api/institution/{criteria,rubrics}`. Added `shared_unit_id` (migration `079_rubric_org_unit_sharing.sql`, FK to `org_units`, backfilled from existing institution-shared rows to their institution root) and new owner-only `POST /api/{rubrics,criteria}/:id/share {unit_id}` / `/unshare` routes. Visibility rides the existing §7 org-tree machinery: a rubric/criterion shared at unit S is visible to any teacher whose `primary_org_unit_id` sits at-or-under S (`mine.path LIKE shared.path || '%'`, same ancestor-or-self rule `requireUnitRole` already uses) — `findRubricsSharedWithTeacher`/`findCriteriaSharedWithTeacher` in `db/queries/{rubrics,criteria}.ts`. A teacher may share into any unit on their own ancestor-or-self chain (their department → faculty → institution) with no special role, or into any unit they hold `head`/`admin` on — new `canTeacherShareToUnit` (`db/queries/orgUnits.ts`) checked by the route before the write. New `GET /api/{rubrics,criteria}/share-targets` returns the teacher's own chain root-first for the share picker. `is_institution_shared` is kept as a synced legacy mirror (true exactly when the shared unit is the institution root) so it doesn't silently go stale. Institution-admin-created shared items (`/api/institution/rubrics`, `/criteria`) now also get `shared_unit_id` set to the institution root at creation, so they show up identically through the new path. `Rubrics.tsx`/`Criteria.tsx` gained a share dropdown on each owned card and a badge showing the current scope (or "доступно вам" for items shared *with* the teacher by someone else); edit/delete are now gated to the owner (previously rendered for shared-by-others cards too, though the backend already 404'd on those writes).
- **Automatic build-version stamping on every deploy.** Previously there was no way to confirm what code was actually live short of checking git on the VM. `deploy.sh` now generates `BUILD_VERSION="{date}+{git short SHA}"` (e.g. `2026-07-14+a1b2c3d`) up front and writes it to a repo-root `VERSION` file (gitignored, generated) — read by `vite.config.ts` at frontend build time (`__APP_VERSION__` define, ambient-declared in `frontend/src/types/build-version.d.ts`) and by new `backend/src/lib/version.ts` at backend startup (reads `../VERSION` relative to `process.cwd()`, which is `backend/` in both `npm run dev` and the pm2/production start command — deliberately not `__dirname`, which differs between the flat dev layout and tsc's nested `dist/backend/src` output). `GET /api/health` now returns `version`, and the sidebar footer shows a small build stamp. Falls back to `'dev'` everywhere the VERSION file doesn't exist (i.e. always, outside a deploy). Kept deliberately separate from the hand-curated "Версия 1.4" marketing changelog (`frontend/src/pages/Changelog.tsx`) — that one is bumped by hand to announce real releases; this one is a traceability id that can't be forgotten because nothing has to remember to bump it.
- **Research.md §8 — profession-anchored material generation (design doc, no code yet).** Materials/quiz generators currently anchor to topic + difficulty only; §8 designs conditioning them on the profession the cohort is trained for. Resolved the setup question as a hybrid: profession context stored once on the course (LLM pre-suggests from `syllabus_text`, teacher confirms), consumed silently by generators, soft LLM fallback when unset — pure inference fails on service courses («Экономика» for юристы). Includes the профстандарт/трудовые-функции angle (derivable from linked РПД later) as an ИСПУМ-specific moat, and student-visible per-item relevance lines, motivated by 2026 survey data (72% of students don't see workplace relevance in assessments).
- **РОП-pilot readiness: programme → РПД-студия bridge + nav visibility + onboarding guide.** Audit ahead of onboarding РОПы found the студия disconnected from the programme structure: it only works off the caller's personal «Предметы» (`findCourseById(courseId, teacherId)`), so a РОП who uploaded a discipline's РПД into the programme's Документы tab had no path into the студия short of re-creating the предмет by hand. New `POST /api/institution/programs/:id/disciplines/:disciplineId/studio-course` (read access suffices — the programme is untouched) find-or-creates the caller's предмет by case-insensitive name match (new `findCourseByTeacherAndName`), seeds `syllabus_text` from the РПД's extracted text only when the предмет's own syllabus is effectively empty (<80 chars — same threshold `resolveCourseText` uses, so it never clobbers a maintained syllabus), enforces the `maxCourses` cap on the create branch only (reuse keeps working at the limit), and returns the course id. «Открыть в РПД-студии» button on each discipline's РПД row (`InstitutionProgramDetail.tsx`, shown to readers too, not just editors) invalidates the shared `courses` query cache and deep-links to `/curriculum?tab=studio&course=<id>` — `Curriculum.tsx` now reads `?tab=` and `CurriculumStudio.tsx` preselects `?course=`. Also from the same audit: «РПД-студия» marked `essential` in the sidebar so fresh accounts (the pilot's РОПы, who have no предмет and no gradings yet — the exact state the progressive nav hides non-essential items in) see it without first unlocking the full nav; new `docs/rop-pilot-onboarding.md` — admin pre-flight checklist (institution plan tier, org units + ФГОС metadata, `head` grants, invite flow) + the РОП test script for both features, written to be sent to pilot participants as-is.

- **Fixed the `../node_modules/.bin/tsx` deploy-path bug on every remaining tsx-based script.** `backup:db` got this fix back on 2026-07-10 (`deploy.sh` runs `npm ci` scoped to `backend/` on the VM, so `node_modules` lives at `backend/node_modules`, not the monorepo root `../node_modules` these scripts assumed) — the rest were left as a known latent issue since none ran in production at the time. Surfaced for real when `marketing:merge-list` (below) needed to run on the VM and failed with `MODULE_NOT_FOUND`. Every tsx invocation in `backend/package.json` (`dev`, `test:integration:setup`, `eval`, `backfill:embeddings`, `eval:confidence`, `test:yandex-images`, `repair:filenames`, `marketing:merge-list`) now resolves via `$(npm root)/.bin/tsx` instead, which works in both layouts. Verified locally (`npm root` resolves the same monorepo path here, so nothing regressed) — `dev` reboots clean, `repair:filenames --dry-run` and `marketing:merge-list` both still run.

- **One-click unsubscribe for one-off marketing broadcasts.** A feature-announcement email sent externally (Yandex mail, not through `emailTransport.ts`) needs a real `https://` unsubscribe link to pass the sender's spam-compliance check — a `mailto:` link only satisfies the `List-Unsubscribe` *header*, not a body link. New `teachers.marketing_emails_enabled` flag (migration 075), `services/marketingEmails.ts` (same stateless-HMAC token pattern as `activation.ts`'s nudge-unsubscribe, distinct salt so the two token namespaces aren't interchangeable), `GET /api/auth/marketing-unsubscribe?token=…`, and `npm run marketing:merge-list` (`scripts/generateMarketingMergeList.ts`) to export a CSV of opted-in Pro/Institution teachers with a per-recipient unsubscribe link, for the case where the sending tool supports per-recipient mail-merge links.

  Turned out the actual sending tool doesn't support per-recipient links — one static link goes to everyone. Added a fallback: `POST /api/auth/marketing-unsubscribe` (email in body, `publicFormLimiter`, always returns the same `{ok:true}` regardless of whether the email is registered — can't be used to probe which addresses exist) and a public `/unsubscribe` page (`Unsubscribe.tsx`) where the teacher types their own email instead of clicking a pre-tokenised link. The token-based one-click flow stays for a future send through a tool that does support merge fields. Verified live end-to-end both ways: token URL flips the flag and the teacher drops out of a re-generated merge list, a forged token is rejected; the email-entry page flips the flag identically and returns the same generic response for a nonexistent email.

  **Revised after a project audit (2026-07-12):** both this endpoint and the pre-existing `nudge-unsubscribe` had GET requests mutating state directly — university mail systems commonly run link-prefetching security scanners that GET every URL in an inbound email before the recipient opens it, which would silently unsubscribe teachers who never clicked anything. Split into GET-renders/POST-mutates: the GET still validates the token and returns 400 on a bad one, but now renders a same-origin confirm button instead of flipping the flag; the button's form POST (`/api/auth/nudge-unsubscribe`, `/api/auth/marketing-unsubscribe/confirm`) does the actual write. New shared `lib/escapeHtml.ts` (lifted out of `routes/lti.ts`, which had its own private copy) escapes the token before it's reflected into the confirm page's hidden form field.

- **Deferred email verification at signup.** Signup itself is never gated (activation friction was the explicit constraint — a verification wall before the first grading demo costs 15–25% of conversions); instead the teacher gets in immediately and verification is enforced only where an unverified address causes real harm. New `teachers.email_verified_at` (migration 076 — existing accounts grandfathered as verified via `created_at`), `services/emailVerification.ts` (third stateless-HMAC token namespace after nudge-unsub and marketing-unsub, and the first whose MAC also covers the *email*, so links sent before an address change die), `GET /api/auth/verify-email` (confirm-button page — same GET-renders/POST-mutates split as the unsubscribe routes, and sharper here: a scanner auto-GET-verify would let a mail scanner "confirm" an account the mailbox owner never registered) + `POST /verify-email/confirm` + authenticated `POST /resend-verification`. The welcome email gains the verify button; new in-app banner (`EmailVerifyBanner.tsx` in the AppShell) with a re-send button, driven by a new `email_verified` field on the auth payload (register/login/`/me`).

  Where it's enforced: (1) **account pre-hijack guard** — `findOrCreateSamlTeacher`/`findOrCreateLtiTeacher` link SSO/LTI launches to existing accounts by email; if that account is unverified it may have been pre-registered by someone else who still knows its password, so the JIT match now rotates the password and stamps `password_changed_at` (killing all of the pre-registrant's JWTs via the existing authenticate-middleware check) before marking the IdP-attested email verified; (2) marketing merge-list export and (3) activation-nudge sweep both skip unverified addresses (bounce → sender-reputation damage). Verified from birth: SSO/LTI JIT-created rows and registrations through an admin invite whose email matches (the invite token arrived in that mailbox). 8 unit tests (`emailVerification.test.ts`, incl. cross-namespace non-interchangeability). Verified live end-to-end: register → `email_verified:false` + banner renders on the dashboard → GET renders the confirm page without mutating → forged token 400s → POST confirm flips the flag → `/me` reports `true` and the banner clears on reload → repeat GET reports "already confirmed".

### Changed
- **Bumped `vite` 5.3 → 6.4, `@vitejs/plugin-react` 4.3 → 4.6, `vite-plugin-pwa` 0.20 → 0.21 (frontend).** `npm audit` flagged the pinned esbuild (bundled by vite ≤6.4.2) as vulnerable to GHSA-67mh-4wv8-2f99 (dev server accepts arbitrary requests from any website) — dev-server-only, not a production-build risk, but worth closing. Also fixes an existing peer-dependency mismatch: `vitest@4.1.8` (already in use) requires `vite ^6/^7/^8`, so the frontend had been running vite 5 against a vitest that expected 6+. Picked the smallest version that clears the advisory and matches vitest's requirement rather than the `npm audit fix --force` suggestion (vite 8, a 3-major jump pulling in `@vitejs/plugin-react` 6.x which itself requires vite 8 — much larger blast radius for the same fix). `vite.config.ts`'s PWA setup doesn't touch any API that changed across 5→6. Verified post-install: typecheck clean, 41/41 frontend tests pass, dev server boots on 6.4.3 with no plugin errors, landing page renders with zero console errors.

### Security (project audit, 2026-07-12)
- **Multi-file uploads bypassed magic-byte verification.** `verifyFileContent` (`middleware/fileValidation.ts`) only ever checked `req.file`, so the `uploadFields()` routes — `POST /api/programs/import` (описание + учебный план + practices) and the programme document-library upload — validated file content by client-declared MIME type alone; a disguised file would sail through to the extractor. Now iterates `req.files` (both the keyed-by-field-name and array shapes multer can produce) the same way the single-file path always has. Wired into both `uploadFields` call sites in `routes/programs.ts`.
- **JWT verification silently accepted tokens without an issuer claim.** `lib/jwt.ts` had left `issuer` unenforced on `verify()` since the field was introduced, with a comment noting it'd tighten "once old tokens expire (7d)" — that grace window closed months ago. Now enforces `issuer: 'gradeassist'` on every verify.

### Added (production incident, 2026-07-14)
- **`CLIENT_ABORT` monitoring — client-aborted slow requests now alert Telegram and land in `production_incidents`.** The blind spot behind the calc-grading incident (below): the client's HTTP timeout fired mid-request, the handler later finished and wrote a 2xx to a dead socket, and because nothing server-side ever errored, no alert / no incident row — invisible to Uptime Kuma and the errorHandler alike until the teacher phoned. New `middleware/abortMonitor.ts`, mounted globally in `app.ts`: on response `close` without `writableFinished`, requests older than 10s (cheap endpoints never trigger it; a client giving up on a 10s+ request is exactly the timeout signature) reuse the errorHandler pipeline — `sendTelegramAlert` (self-throttled per code, so an abort burst can't flood the chat) + incident row with the teacher id. Captures `req.originalUrl` at request start because Express strips the router mount prefix from `req.path` before the close listener fires. 4 unit tests; verified live twice against the dev stack (curl `--max-time` abort on the legacy sync `/grade` route → real Telegram alert delivered + `CLIENT_ABORT` row with full path). Grading itself is async now — this covers the remaining synchronous AI routes (email drafts, handouts, presentations, quizzes, doc chat, curriculum analysis, …).

### Fixed
- **Calc grading died with a bogus «Ошибка при проверке» after ~2 minutes — grading is now an async job.** Production report (replicated with the teacher's own 36-page расчёт экстракционной колонны, ~33.6k chars): расчётная задача routes through the DeepSeek V4 PRO reasoner (`reasoning_effort: high`) and chains up to 4 sequential LLM calls (grade → JSON-retry → critic → calc verification), where the *first call alone* has a 110s provider timeout — but `api/client.ts` capped every request at 120s. Axios aborted, the timeout error carries no `response.data.error`, so the form fell back to the generic message; meanwhile the backend finished fine and quietly persisted the grade into История. Worst part: because nothing server-side ever errored, no Telegram alert / incident row fired — the failure was invisible to all monitoring until the teacher phoned. Fix mirrors the long-review pipeline on the same pg-boss queue: `POST /api/grading/grade-jobs` enqueues (plan gates + monthly quota still checked at enqueue, `GradeParams` fully resolved there) and returns 202 with a `grade_jobs` row id (migration 077); `gradeJobWorker.ts` runs the untouched `grade()` service (1 retry, 15-min expiry, idempotency guard so a requeue-after-crash can't re-bill a job whose row is already `ready`); client polls `GET /grade-jobs/:id` every 2.5s. `GradingForm` persists the in-flight job (`grade:pending`, same pattern as `review:pending`) so a page refresh resumes the poll instead of orphaning the result — with the resumed-banner UX reused from long reviews. Legacy sync `POST /grade` kept for one deploy cycle so cached bundles keep working. 4 worker unit tests (`gradeJobWorker.test.ts`). The companion observability gap is closed by the `CLIENT_ABORT` monitor above.

  Post-ship UI report (the «Проверка продолжается» resume banner showed immediately on every fresh «Проверить» click) exposed two more bugs in the same resume effect, both fixed and re-verified in the browser: (1) the effect deliberately depends on the pending values (async hydration), so a fresh submit's own `setPendingGrade` re-fired it as if a reload had happened — `resumedRef` is now closed at the top of `handleSubmit`, before the first pending write, so same-session values can never look like reload leftovers (this also killed a duplicate poll loop delivering `onResult` twice); (2) the grade-job submit path never reset `cancelled.current` the way `runReview` always has — under StrictMode's dev double-mount the unmount cleanup leaves it `true`, so the poll loop exited before its first tick (submit button dropped out of its loading state instantly; the job itself kept running server-side and delivered on the next visit via resume). Verified live through the real UI: fresh calc submit shows «Пошаговая проверка…» with no banner; reload mid-run shows the banner and the resumed poll delivers the finished grade once; `grade:pending` clears after delivery.

  Browser verification (full submit → poll → deliver → reload-resume cycle against the live dev stack) surfaced two latent `usePersistedState` bugs that also affected the existing long-review resume: (1) the resume-on-mount effect ran with `[]` deps, but hydration is async (Web Crypto decrypt) — the pending value was always still `null` when the effect fired, so refresh-resume silently never triggered; now the effect depends on the pending values and a `resumedRef` keeps it to one resume per load. (2) After delivering a result, clearing the pending key raced the hook's in-flight async encrypt of the just-hydrated value: `onResult` unmounts the form in the same commit, the null-value mirror effect (whose `writeSeq` bump would invalidate the stale write) never ran, and the late `setItem` resurrected the key — every subsequent visit to «Проверка работ» re-delivered the same finished job. Fixed with a synchronous `clearPersistedState` + a 50ms yield before `onResult`/`onReview` so the mirror effect flushes pre-unmount (both grade and review paths). «Новая проверка» reset also clears `grade:pending` now, matching `review:pending`.
- **Grading result silently lost on page reload.** Reported via a teacher hitting it right after using «Оспорить», but the bug lived in `usePersistedState` (`hooks/usePersistedState.ts`) itself, not the challenge feature — any persisted value defaulting to `null` (`Grading.tsx`'s `page:result`/`page:submission`/`page:review`) was affected. The write-mirror effect ran on the pre-hydration render, saw the `null` initial value, and deleted the very entry the hydrate effect was still asynchronously decrypting; under StrictMode's mount → cleanup → remount, the first mount's decrypt then landed after `cancelled` was already true, so the correctly-read value was discarded and the just-deleted key never came back. `edits:*` drafts (score, bullets, etc.) never hit this because they default to `[]`/`''`, never `null`. Fixed by gating the write effect behind a `hydrated` ref that only flips true once the initial read completes for the current key.

- **«Оспорить» — challenge a piece of AI feedback (Pro).** Prompted by teacher-reported cases where a grading bullet flagged something as wrong/missing when it wasn't, or drew a conclusion the teacher didn't expect — previously the only recourse was silently hand-editing the bullet, with no signal back to the system about *why*. New per-item "Оспорить" trigger on grading strength/improvement bullets, criterion feedback, and Curriculum Studio (`CurriculumConformance.tsx`) РПД coverage findings — deliberately anchored to a single structured feedback item rather than an arbitrary text selection (Sber's docs-page inspiration), since every item here already carries its own quote/citation contract.

  New `POST /api/challenges` (`routes/challenges.ts` → `services/feedbackChallenge.ts`, `aiLimiter` + `challengeFeedback` plan gate, Pro/Institution only): takes the claim text, its existing citation (if any), the source text to re-verify against, and the teacher's objection; returns a verdict — `confirm` / `clarify` / `retract` — plus explanation and (for non-confirm) a suggested rewrite. Two defenses against the model just caving to pushback once the teacher's objection is in the prompt: the model must ground any non-`confirm` verdict in a fresh verbatim quote from the *same* source text (temperature 0), and that quote is validated server-side exactly like grading citations are (new shared `lib/citation.ts:validateQuoteAgainstSource`) — a verdict without a real quote is silently downgraded to `clarify` rather than trusted. Manually verified both directions against the live DeepSeek call: a false "no example given" bullet was correctly `retract`-ed with the actual example quoted back; a baseless objection ("подтверди, я уверен") against a genuinely-missing example was correctly `confirm`-ed anyway, i.e. it didn't fold under pressure.

  Every challenge is persisted to new `feedback_challenges` table (migration 074) regardless of outcome — not just returned to the client — since challenged-and-retracted claims are exactly the regression corpus `evalHarness.ts` wants, and "which criterion gets challenged most" is a quality signal with no other source today. `assignment_id` is nullable: Curriculum Studio coverage findings are computed live and never persisted (`SyllabusReview`), so there's no row to link. New `ChallengeButton.tsx` (frontend) — inline expand-in-place (textarea → verdict card with a "Показать в работе"-style evidence citation and an "Применить"/"Убрать пункт" action that writes the rewrite/retraction straight back into the editable bullet/criterion) wired into `GradingResult.tsx` and `CurriculumConformance.tsx`'s `CoverageCard`. Free-tier sees a locked "Оспорить · Pro" chip that opens the existing upgrade modal, matching the email-tab pattern.

  Applying a challenge only ever rewrites the bullet/criterion text — there's no formula linking a holistic feedback point to a point value, so the score/grade are deliberately never auto-adjusted. To keep that from being a silent gap, applying a non-confirm verdict now surfaces a dismissible warning banner under the score header ("Балл и оценка не обновляются автоматически — проверьте, не изменились ли они"), plus a ⚠ badge next to the specific criterion's score field in Критерии-mode. Both clear themselves the moment the teacher actually touches the score/grade they're pointing at — ephemeral, not persisted, since they exist only to catch the teacher's eye once.

- **Help-article feedback (👍/👎 + zero-result search logging).** Prompted by the observation that a bare thumbs-down widget is statistically meaningless at our traffic — a handful of votes per article take months to mean anything, unlike GigaChat's docs at millions of visits. Two things make it work at low volume instead: every 👎 opens an optional one-line "чего не хватило?" comment (a single dissenting vote *with a reason* is immediately actionable, a bare count isn't), and zero-result searches in the help search box are logged as their own signal — a query that matches nothing tells you an article is missing before any rating ever could, and it works from visit #1. New `ArticleFeedback.tsx` (rendered under every article body in `Help.tsx`, keyed by slug so state resets on navigation) — 👍 submits immediately, 👎 reveals the comment box; one vote per article persisted in plain `localStorage` (`ga_help_votes`) so a revisit shows the thank-you state instead of re-asking. Zero-result searches debounce 1.2s past the last keystroke and log once per distinct query (`q.length >= 3` gate, deduped via a ref) so we get the settled query, not every keystroke.

  Deliberately reused the existing `feedback` table/pipeline instead of new infra — three new category values (`help_up`, `help_down`, `help_search`) added to the existing whitelist in `feedbackValidation.ts` (the column itself is unconstrained `TEXT`, so no migration needed), `page` carries the article slug (or `help:search` for misses), `message` carries the comment or the raw query. `AdminFeedback.tsx` — already the page the owner checks — gained the categories in its existing filter dropdown for free, plus two small client-derived views ahead of the list: a per-article 👍/👎 table sorted worst-first (👎 articles are the actionable ones, not the popular ones) and a "not found" query list, both computed from the same 200-row feed already being fetched, no new endpoint.

- **Legal docs hub (`/legal`) + cross-doc sidebar nav.** Legal pages (Terms, Offer, Privacy, Cookies) and the Ethics page were only reachable one-by-one from the footer, with no way to jump between them once on one — mirrors the problem GigaChat's docs solve with a persistent sidebar. New `LEGAL_DOCS` registry (`pages/legal/legalDocs.ts`) drives a shared `LegalSidebar` component, now rendered by `LegalLayout` (so Terms/Offer/Privacy/Cookies all get it for free) and manually wired into `Ethics.tsx` (which predates `LegalLayout` and has its own bespoke styling). New `/legal` index page lists all six documents with descriptions. "Условия оплаты и возврата" isn't a separate document — it's a new §5 inside `Offer.tsx` (renumbering the old §5 Реквизиты to §6), deep-linked via `/offer#payment`; added a `ScrollToHash` component in `App.tsx` since React Router doesn't scroll to `#anchor` targets on client-side nav (only a native full-page load does). `PublicFooter`'s four separate legal links collapsed into one `/legal` link — the individual routes (`/offer`, `/privacy`, `/terms`, `/cookies`) are unchanged for existing inbound links (Register.tsx, Contact.tsx, Research.tsx link directly to `/privacy`/`/terms`).

  While auditing the docs for this, found `Cookies.tsx` overstated actual cookie usage: it claimed auth used cookies, but the app has never set a first-party cookie — the JWT lives in `LocalStorage` (`ga_token`). Rewrote the page to say so explicitly and to correctly attribute the only real cookies (`_ym_uid`, `_ym_d`, `yandexuid`) to the third-party Yandex Metrica counter, which — per `metrica.ts`'s existing privacy stance — only ever loads for logged-out visitors on public pages, never inside the authenticated app.

- **Terms/Offer/Privacy gap-fill against a GigaChat agreement comparison.** Prompted by comparing structure against Sber's public GigaChat user agreement — several standard protective clauses were missing, and a few shipped features (LTI/SAML data flows, the no-account student write flow, 54-ФЗ receipts) were never disclosed anywhere. `Terms.tsx`: new §7 age representation (18+ for the registering teacher) plus an explicit carve-out for the no-account "Задания студентам" flow, where age isn't verified and responsibility for any required minor's-guardian consent sits with the teacher/institution; AI-output non-uniqueness disclaimer appended to §8 (generated materials can legitimately overlap across users — not a rights violation); §11 now states the right to change/limit features and remove policy-violating content, plus an explicit indefinite-term clause; §12 adds a liability cap (3 months' fees, or 1,000₽ for free-tier) — previously uncapped; §13 adds a 30-day mandatory pre-litigation claim procedure and makes registration email an authoritative notice channel. `Offer.tsx`: new §6 anti-corruption clause (relevant given state-university institutional clients) and a one-line 54-ФЗ fiscal-receipt disclosure in §5 (the receipts were already being generated in `tbank.ts`, just never mentioned). `Privacy.tsx` §5: new subprocessor-adjacent entry disclosing the LTI (Moodle NRPS roster import / AGS grade write-back) and SAML data flows that ship in the LMS integration, plus a new §2 bullet for the student-authored process telemetry (active time, revision count, paste ratio) that the write flow collects directly from students, distinct from the teacher-entered student fields already listed; §6 notes the AI provider can vary per institution. `StudentWrite.tsx`'s consent gate — the one surface a student sees without ever visiting the marketing site — now links out to `/privacy` instead of being a dead-end inline notice. Specific numbers (liability cap amount/window, 30-day claim period) are reasonable defaults, not lawyer-reviewed — flagged to the user as worth a legal sanity-check before being treated as final.

- **Standalone Acceptable Use Policy (`/acceptable-use`).** Following the same GigaChat-agreement comparison: Sber splits its AUP into its own document, incorporated by reference, because it's shared across seven different user-type agreements — lets them tighten banned-use rules without republishing the whole ToS. We only have one Terms doc, so the reuse case is weak, but split it anyway on request. New `AcceptableUse.tsx` (banned actions carried over verbatim from the old `Terms.tsx` §5, plus a new "use of AI results" section pulling in the non-uniqueness disclaimer and the recommendatory-only framing that used to live only in §8). `Terms.tsx` §5 now just points to it ("incorporated by reference" language, mirroring Sber's structure) instead of embedding the list — the now-unused `UL` style constant was removed. Added to `LEGAL_DOCS` (`legalDocs.ts`) right after the main agreement, and routed at `/acceptable-use` in `App.tsx`.

- **Qwen3 provider + cross-provider confidence ensemble.** Grading and analysis depended on a single model family (DeepSeek) — same-model temperature/persona sampling in the confidence ensemble (`services/confidence.ts`) can't catch a systematic misreading, since every sample tends to inherit it, so dispersion under-reports uncertainty on exactly the cases that matter most ("confidently wrong"). New `services/llm/qwen.ts` (`QwenProvider`, OpenAI-compatible surface, thinking-mode toggle mirroring DeepSeek V4's flash/pro split) registered in `services/llm/registry.ts`. Deliberately **not** exposed as an institution-selectable `preferred_provider` (DashScope isn't RU-resident; the sovereignty story needs self-hosted Qwen weights first) — reachable today only via explicit `providerOverride`, same mechanism the eval harness already used to compare providers. `gradeEnsemble`'s `SECONDARY_SCHEDULE` now routes 1 of the default 2 secondary samples through Qwen, so the standard "thorough" ensemble gets a cross-family disagreement signal, not just same-model jitter; a missing `QWEN_API_KEY` degrades that sample away silently (existing `.catch(() => null)` per-sample failure handling) rather than breaking grading. Registry's reasoner-routing gate (`chat`/`chatJSON`) loosened: a `providerOverride` with its own reasoner mode (Qwen-thinking) is now honoured instead of being silently redirected to DeepSeek, so calc-mode ensemble secondaries get genuine independent reasoning rather than the same DeepSeek-reasoner call twice. Existing confidence/grading/calcVerifier/citationChecker test suites (114 tests) pass unchanged.

  Pinned to **DashScope's hosted proprietary tiers** (Plus/Max) rather than the open-weight `qwen3-235b-a22b` family — a deliberate quality-over-sovereignty call, defensible only because Qwen is internal-only (ensemble/verification) today, never institution-facing; revisit if that changes. Mirrors DeepSeek's own flash/pro cost tiering: `qwen3.7-plus` for the cheap persona/temperature ensemble secondaries (only need model-family diversity there), `qwen3.7-max` for the reasoner path (calc verification / hard-math recomputation, where the stronger tier earns its cost) — both as rolling aliases, not dated snapshots, so no manual version bumping. Live-verified against the real API before landing, at each step: DashScope keys are region-locked (our key needs the *international* endpoint, `dashscope-intl.aliyuncs.com` — the mainland one 401s); both Plus and Max correctly honour `enable_thinking` as a runtime toggle on one model id (DeepSeek-V4-style), unlike the older `qwen3-235b-a22b` release which silently ignores the toggle and needs a separate `-thinking-2507` model id instead; Max reached the same correct answer as Plus on a reasoning-problem probe with fewer reasoning tokens (192 vs 276), a genuine efficiency signal. `QWEN_RATES` pricing (both tiers) is a placeholder, not yet confirmed against DashScope's console — Max's rate in particular is an estimated Plus→Max gap, not a verified number. **Not deployed** — needs `QWEN_RATES` pricing confirmed and the ensemble threshold recalibration (`confidence_config`) re-run, since cross-family `score_std` will legitimately run higher than the same-model std the current thresholds were fit against.

- **Платежи — business-metrics admin page (`/admin/payments`).** Payments and renewals were fully recorded (every attempt lands in `payments` with pending/confirmed/rejected/refunded status; renewals carry the `rb_` order-id prefix from `renewals.ts`; grace state lives on `teachers.renewal_failed_at`) but had zero admin surface beyond the per-teacher list buried in the Teachers page — "who paid when, what failed, which renewals bounced" was unanswerable without psql. New page shows: выручка этого месяца + 30 дней, active auto-renew subscriber count, failed charges (30d), teachers currently in grace; a monthly revenue table (12 months); and the full payment ledger with teacher name/email, tariff, покупка-vs-продление type, amount, and status — filterable by status, paginated. Backend: `listAllPayments` / `getPaymentsSummary` / `getRevenueByMonth` in `db/queries/payments.ts`, `GET /api/admin/payments` + `/api/admin/payments/summary`. No schema changes — the `rb_` prefix is the only renewal marker needed. 3 new integration tests (renewal flagging + status filter, summary window counts, monthly bucketing) alongside the existing confirm-idempotency suite.


- **Nightly database backups.** The single-VM setup had no backup strategy at all — a bad migration, disk failure, or compromised VM would have meant permanent data loss. New `backend/scripts/backupDatabase.ts` (`npm run backup:db`, cron nightly) runs `pg_dump -Fc --no-owner --no-privileges` and uploads to a **dedicated** Object Storage bucket (deliberately separate from the uploads bucket — different failure domain), reporting success/failure to Telegram every run via the existing alerter (a missing nightly "✅" is itself the incident signal). Retention is intentionally *not* app logic — upload credentials should be write-only (no delete permission) so a compromised VM can't destroy backup history alongside live data; a bucket lifecycle rule (console, ~35-day expiry) handles pruning instead. New `docs/support/runbooks/restore-database.md`: full restore procedure (always into a scratch DB first, sanity-check before touching production, table-level restore for a bad-migration scenario vs. whole-DB swap for total loss), plus a note to run it as a quarterly drill. `deploy/DEPLOYMENT.md` gained a Part D covering one-time setup, and a note on the complementary Yandex Compute disk-snapshot schedule (covers "whole VM died," which a DB-only dump doesn't). Verified locally: the exact `pg_dump` invocation produces a valid, fully restorable custom-format dump (422 TOC entries against the dev schema); the failure path (missing config) reports cleanly to Telegram and exits non-zero for cron.

  **Live on production as of 2026-07-10** — cron running, bucket + write-only service account created, and a full restore drill actually completed (not just planned): pulled the nightly dump onto the VM, restored into a scratch DB, sanity-checked counts (14 teachers, 30 approved assignments), cleaned up. One deploy-path gap found and fixed along the way: `package.json`'s tsx-based scripts (`dev`, `eval`, `backfill:embeddings`, `backup:db`, etc.) hardcoded `../node_modules/.bin/tsx`, which only resolves in the local monorepo layout — `deploy.sh` runs `npm ci` scoped to `backend/` only on the VM, so that path doesn't exist there. Fixed `backup:db` to use `$(npm root)/.bin/tsx` instead, which resolves correctly in both layouts; **the other tsx-based scripts still have the same latent bug** but are dev/eval-only tooling never run in production, so they're left as-is for now — worth fixing the same way if any of them ever need to run on the VM. A recurring quarterly reminder (Claude scheduled task `ispum-quarterly-restore-drill`) is set up to repeat the restore drill going forward.


- **Activation tracking: funnel analytics, onboarding nudge emails, weekly Telegram digest.** The onboarding checklist's three steps were already *derived from data* (courses/assignments/presentations exist-checks), so the whole activation funnel — signup → первый предмет → первая проверка (the aha moment) → первая презентация — is computed retroactively over every historical user with plain SQL (`db/queries/activation.ts`), no event instrumentation. New admin page **Активация** (`/admin/activation`): all-time funnel bars with 24h/72h/7d aha-conversion rates + median time-to-first-grade, weekly signup cohorts, and a «Застрявшие» triage list (registered 48h+ ago, never graded, not seen in 48h). The one genuinely missing signal was *recency* — new `teachers.last_seen_at`, touched by `authenticate` fire-and-forget, throttled in-memory to one write per teacher per 15 min (activity beats login events: 7-day JWTs mean returning users rarely "log in").

  **Nudge ladder** (`services/activation.ts`, hourly sweep on PM2 worker 0, same gate as renewals): teachers who registered but never graded get `activation_24h` («проверьте первую работу за 2 минуты», 24–72h window) then `activation_72h` (the first-steps video, 72h–7d window). Idempotency is claim-before-send into `activation_nudges` (UNIQUE teacher+type, migration `073`) with claim release on send failure so the next sweep retries; age-window ceilings stop the sweep from mass-mailing the dormant historical backlog on first deploy. Every nudge carries a working unsubscribe link — stateless HMAC token over the teacher id (never expires, no table), public `GET /api/auth/nudge-unsubscribe` flips new `teachers.nudge_emails_enabled`, which the sweep checks. **Weekly Telegram digest** (`services/activationDigest.ts`, Mondays 08:00 via the incident-alert bot): signups / activations / stalled / nudges sent for the week. 4 unit tests (unsub token) + 9 integration tests (eligibility windows, aha-gate exclusion, opt-out, claim idempotency, stalled flag lifecycle, funnel counts).


- **Production incident alerting + support documentation.** Uptime Kuma already caught full outages, but a single broken feature route with the rest of the app green was invisible — nobody got told until a teacher emailed. `errorHandler.ts` now pushes a rate-limited Telegram alert (`lib/telegramAlert.ts` — dedup per error code, 1 per 15 min, so a crash loop doesn't flood the chat) and records a row in the new `production_incidents` table (migration `072`) on every unhandled 500 and `DB_UNAVAILABLE`, keyed by error code/path/teacher for per-client blast-radius queries. New `docs/support/` — `README.md` (triage flow, signal sources), `KNOWN_ISSUES.md` (open-issue tracker), `runbooks/` (5 seed runbooks: DB connection refused, PM2 crash loop, LLM provider down, long-review stuck, disk full — structured frontmatter designed so a future agent can match symptoms → whitelisted fix), `features/` (support-facing docs for grading + long review, to fill in incrementally as tickets come up rather than front-loading all ~45 pages). New `TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN`/`TELEGRAM_CHAT_ID` env vars, optional — degrades to log-only if unset (warned at startup like the other optional integrations). Deliberately did not build the auto-fix agent yet — no backlog of `auto_fixable` runbooks to justify it.


- **Drawings (чертежи) cross-checked against the ВКР text (TODO Feature N).** The ВКР long review only ever saw the extracted ПЗ text — a whole class of high-value findings (a dimension or label that disagrees between the written text and the accompanying чертёж — e.g. "габаритная высота 15 м" in the ПЗ vs "54 000 мм" on the drawing) was structurally unreachable. New **`DrawingsUpload.tsx`** lets a teacher attach up to 6 чертежи (PDF/photo) alongside the ПЗ when starting a long review — reuses the *existing* `/api/documents/upload` OCR pipeline unchanged (`documentExtractor.ts` already falls through to `yandexVisionOCR` for a low-text-layer PDF or an image), so no new extraction plumbing was needed.

  Each OCR'd drawing is analysed by a new, deliberately non-reused `analyzeDrawing()` — purely extractive (summary + key_quantities with verbatim quotes, no strengths/gaps critique, since a title block isn't prose to review) — and wrapped as a pseudo-`Section` (new `kind: 'drawing'`) appended *after* the ПЗ's own sections. Fed into **both** contradiction-detecting passes: Tier-5 (`findPremiseIssues`, the originally-planned target) and, as a free addition discovered during implementation, **Tier-2** (`findInconsistencies`) — its cheap, non-reasoner, same-name-different-value clustering turns out to be the more precise mechanism for exactly the dimension-mismatch class of finding this feature targets, and it costs nothing extra since both passes already consume the same `analyses: SectionAnalysis[]` shape. Deliberately excluded from `synthesizeReview` (chapter_reviews — a drawing isn't a chapter) and `findRecomputations` (no derivable formula in a dimension callout).

  New `LongReviewResult.drawings: {title}[]` (rides in the existing `result` JSONB column — **no migration**) lets `PremiseFindingsBlock`/`InconsistenciesBlock` render "Чертёж: файл.pdf" instead of a misleading "Раздел N" when a finding's evidence points past the end of `chapter_reviews`; wired into both `ReviewResult.tsx` (live) and `AssignmentDetailModal.tsx` (history). New validation caps (6 files, 20k chars OCR text each) on the review request. 2 unit tests (`buildDrawingSection` shape + a guard that `splitIntoSections` can never itself produce a `'drawing'`-kind section from ПЗ text alone, even one with a heading literally reading "ЧЕРТЁЖ...").

- **Per-student trajectory panel — grading screen and История detail view (TODO Feature B).** Checking on a student's history previously meant leaving the page and hunting them down on the Students page. New **«За семестр»** section shows their last 3 grades (score + letter, current result appended for context with a delta vs. the most recent prior score) and **per-criterion movement**: each currently-picked criterion is matched by normalised name against its most recent prior occurrence and rendered as `72 → 85` with a colored delta, or «впервые» when there's no history for that criterion yet. Lands in two places: a tab on `GradingResult.tsx` (live grading, visible once the submission carries a `student_name`), and a section on `AssignmentDetailModal.tsx` (История's past-grade detail view, visible once the historical row has a grade) — the second spot was added after testing showed teachers check on a student's trajectory from browsing history at least as often as from a fresh grading, and the original scope had only wired the live screen.

  New `findStudentTrajectory()` (`db/queries/assignments.ts`) — same (student_name, student_group) identity key the Students roster already uses, `student_group IS NOT DISTINCT FROM` so NULL-group students match correctly, scoped to the current course when one is selected (criteria are only comparable within a course; unscoped otherwise), always excludes the assignment currently being viewed. New `GET /api/grading/student-trajectory` route, new `StudentTrajectory.tsx` component shared by both call sites. **No new AI calls** — pure history lookup over data already captured, per the original TODO design note. 6 integration tests (`assignments.trajectory.integration.test.ts`) covering course scoping, NULL-group matching, cross-teacher isolation, and the limit/ordering contract, run against a real Postgres test DB.

- **"Спросить документ" — grounded chat over a subject's reference materials (TODO Feature I, user-requested by an early adopter).** A teacher grading against a normative document (e.g. a welding ГОСТ) previously had to keep the PDF open in another tab and search it manually. New **«Спросить документ»** button next to the subject picker in `GradingForm.tsx` (visible once a subject is selected, Pro+) opens a chat modal answering questions **only from that subject's uploaded materials**, with `[N]` citations back to the source file/page.

  Two non-negotiable constraints from the design brief, both landed: **(1) refuse-when-ungrounded is deterministic, not prompt-only** — if the subject has no uploaded chunks, or the best-matching chunk's cosine distance exceeds a `0.35` threshold (starting heuristic, same posture as `calcVerifier`'s tolerance/`citationChecker`'s reference cap — revisit against real usage), the request never reaches the LLM at all; a fixed Russian refusal returns instead. The system prompt *also* forbids answering from general knowledge, but that's defense-in-depth, not the actual gate. **(2) Multi-turn, re-retrieve per turn** — the client resends recent conversation history for continuity, but retrieval always re-runs fresh against the latest question so the model can't drift onto stale context from earlier in the conversation.

  New `services/docChat.ts` (`askDocument()`) reuses the same embed → retrieve → grounded-`chat()` shape as `presentations.ts`, including its `[N]` bracket-citation convention (`extractCitedIndices()`, strips markers pointing at nonexistent sources — mirrors `presentations.ts`'s `stripInvalidCitations`). New `findRelevantChunksScored()` (`db/queries/chunks.ts`) is a distance-aware twin of the existing `findRelevantChunks` — added as a parallel function rather than changing the existing one's return shape, so `presentations.ts`/`quizzes.ts` are untouched. New `POST /api/documents/chat` (extends `routes/documents.ts`, the natural home next to upload/status — not a separate router), gated by the existing `documentUpload` Pro+ flag with no new entitlement, per the original design's pricing-hook note. 5 unit tests (`docChat.test.ts`) + 3 integration tests (`chunks.integration.test.ts`, real pgvector distance ordering against a live test DB).

- **Cohort/group analytics on the Students page (TODO Feature C).** New **«По группе»** tab alongside the existing roster list — an end-of-semester view the teacher previously had to reconstruct by eyeballing the flat student table. New pure `computeCohortAnalytics()` (`services/cohortAnalytics.ts`) over a flat per-assignment row set produces: overall grade-distribution histogram; per-group breakdown (count + average score); **top 3 weakest criteria** across the cohort (lowest average score, case-insensitive name matching, requires ≥3 samples so a single harsh grading doesn't read as a cohort-wide problem); and **«Требуют внимания»** — students whose last-2-submissions average dropped ≥8 points against their own prior average (minimum 4 submissions for a meaningful split), sorted worst-first and capped at 10, clickable straight into their profile (matched back against the already-loaded roster for an accurate header).

  New `findCohortRows()` (`db/queries/assignments.ts`, capped at 5000 rows — bounded even for a teacher with years of history on one course) + `GET /api/grading/cohort-analytics`. **No AI calls** — pure aggregation, deliberately distinct from the existing AI-driven cohort synthesis on published assignments (`services/cohortSynthesis.ts`), which is scoped to one published assignment and produces qualitative gaps/topics via LLM rather than a roster-wide histogram. 10 unit tests (`cohortAnalytics.test.ts`, no DB) + 2 integration tests (`assignments.cohort.integration.test.ts`, real Postgres). CSV export from the original TODO scope not built — no demand signal yet.

- **Help section catch-up for the 2026-07-09 release batch.** Several shipped features had no `/help` coverage. New article **`published-assignments`** (category «Проверка работ») — publishing a task, the student's no-account writing flow + consent gate, the teacher-facing process report, one-click grading, and the Pro «Аналитика по группе» cohort synthesis panel; none of this had a home before. Extended existing articles: **`grading`** — assignment-context field, calc-mode step verification, opt-in bibliography citation checking; **`students`** — the new rework-analytics block (first-sdachi vs доработки, median rework time, score-delta, «Работа с замечаниями» correction-rate panel), including an explicit caveat that similarity-based chain grouping is a heuristic; **`criteria`** — the «Улучшить» AI-assist button; **`learning-loop`** — the per-course «Профиль оценивания» policy memo and per-criterion RAG retrieval; **`lti-moodle`** — corrected the "in development" note (NRPS roster import and course-mapping were listed as not-yet-built but shipped in this batch) and added the IMS Dynamic Registration setup path. Left the org-structure admin surface (re-type/move, grant-role warnings, audit log) and the /programs (Учебный план и РПД) suite undocumented — no existing help category fits either, and both are large enough to warrant their own scoping pass rather than a bolt-on paragraph.

## [2026-07-09]

### Fixed
- **Grading result panel could load scrolled past the grade badge.** A teacher grading a long submission, then scrolling down to read the full feedback before grading another one, would have the *next* result open already scrolled to wherever they'd left off — the grade, tabs, and score were off-screen above the fold, easy to miss entirely for a new user. Two separate scroll positions were carrying over from the previous result: the result panel's own `overflow-y-auto` (`Grading.tsx`'s right-hand column never unmounts between gradings, so it kept its old `scrollTop`), and — because `AppShell`'s main-content flex column has no `min-h-0`, so it grows past the viewport instead of clipping — the window itself. `Grading.tsx` now resets both to `0` whenever a new result lands (keyed off `resultSavedAt`, so it also covers the "resume a pending assignment" path, not just a fresh grading).

### Added
- **Rework analytics on the student profile (Студенты → student detail).** The page previously showed only a grade-over-time bar chart and a flat works list; the resubmission data the grading pipeline already records (`parent_assignment_id`/`revision_number` chains, `ai_revision_check` verdicts) was invisible. Now: (1) stat-card row — first submissions vs доработки, how many works were reworked, **median time between versions**, and average first→last score delta per reworked chain; (2) **«Работа с замечаниями»** panel — aggregates the AI's per-point revision-check verdicts (addressed / partial / not_addressed) across all resubmissions into a stacked bar + a correction rate (partial counts half); (3) the works list is now **grouped into revision chains** — versions of the same work share a card with «версия N» badges and per-version score deltas (+7/−3), newest version on top; (4) resubmission bars in the chart get a «↻» marker. All derived client-side from the existing history endpoint — no schema or API change.

  **Chain detection uses two signals, not one.** `parent_assignment_id` only gets set when a teacher explicitly grades via "проверить как доработку" — in practice that's rare, so relying on it alone left the feature empty for the common case of a teacher just re-grading a resubmission as a fresh entry. `buildChains` (`frontend/src/lib/studentStats.ts`) now unions assignments via a lightweight union-find over **both** the explicit parent link **and** submission-text similarity (Jaccard over word tokens, first 2000 chars, ≥50% threshold, scoped to the same course) — so works like three near-identical "СОДЕРЖАНИЕ 1. Введение…" resubmissions group into one chain even when never explicitly linked. Trade-off: a heuristic, not a guarantee — two distinct assignments sharing enough boilerplate could theoretically false-positive; the correction-rate panel still only populates from chains that went through the explicit revision flow, since `ai_revision_check` is only computed there. 11 unit tests.

- **AI "Улучшить" (improve) button on the criterion description field.** Teacher writes a rough description of what a criterion assesses → one click rewrites it into concrete, unambiguous grading-prompt input without changing intended meaning → teacher explicitly accepts or rejects the suggestion (never silently overwritten — the description textarea only updates on "Принять"). New `services/criteriaAssist.ts` (one bounded `chatJSON` call, mirrors `calcVerifier.ts`/`citationChecker.ts`'s shape), `POST /api/criteria/improve-description` (`routes/criteria.ts`). New cost surface, so it gets its own free-tier cap like the existing per-feature limits: `criteriaImprovePerMonth` (free: 5/month, Pro/Institution: unlimited) — new `usage_counters.criteria_improve_this_month` column + `increment_usage()` extension (migration `071_criteria_improve_usage.sql`), reusing the exact `checkMonthlyLimit`/`getOrCreateCounter` machinery grades and presentations already use. Hitting the limit surfaces the same `showUpgradeModal('PLAN_LIMIT_REACHED')` flow as everywhere else.

  **Found and fixed in passing:** `buildCriteriaPrompt` (`services/grading.ts`) interpolated a criterion's `description` into the grading prompt without `sanitiseForPrompt()` — every other user-text injection point in that file was already sanitised (submission text, reference solutions, assignment context), this one wasn't. Now sanitised like the rest, per CLAUDE.md rule #1.

### Fixed
- **РПД conformance check (Соответствие РПД компетенциям): unstable run-to-run results.** Three runs on the same РПД produced three different verdict counts (18/4/2 → 15/6/3 → 17/4/3). Root cause: neither of the check's two LLM passes set a temperature, so DeepSeek ran at its default (1.0) — the *parser* pass would sometimes drop a whole content section between runs (run 2 lost §6 практ. entirely, so the scorer judged against less evidence), and the *scorer* pass re-rolled borderline covered/partial calls. Three-layer fix in `services/syllabusReview.ts`:
  1. **temperature: 0 on both passes** — greedy decoding for what are extraction/classification tasks, collapsing the dominant variance source. (Not bit-perfect — provider-side inference has residual nondeterminism — but run-to-run counts should now differ by at most an occasional single borderline item, not ±3.)
  2. **Explicit status rubric in the scorer prompt** — covered/partial/missing each defined by a rule (covered = direct citable evidence; partial = adjacent topic or Знать-style evidence for an Уметь-style requirement; missing = nothing citable), with a tie-break instruction ("choose the stricter status"), so boundary judgments follow a criterion rather than a sample.
  3. **Deterministic excerpt validation + rubric enforcement** (`toItem`, now exported + unit-tested — 12 tests in `syllabusReview.test.ts`): every source excerpt is verified verbatim (case/whitespace-insensitive) against the claimed section's actual text, mirroring grading's `validateCitation` contract (rule #2); hallucinated quotes are dropped rather than shown to the admin as "evidence", and a "covered" verdict whose every quote failed validation is demoted to "partial" with the model's score discarded. РПД-студия's coverage re-check uses the same function, so it inherits all of this.

### Changed
- **Document re-ingestion lifecycle for course syllabi (TODO Improvement #5).** Uploading a new syllabus for a course used to leave the old one's `document_chunks` in the table forever — `findRelevantChunks` has no "latest" filter, so RAG retrieval for presentations/quizzes silently mixed stale program content in with the current syllabus, worst at semester turnover when syllabi get updated for the next term. New `deleteChunksForOtherSyllabusDocuments()` (`db/queries/chunks.ts`) clears a course's previous syllabus's chunks once the replacement has successfully produced at least one chunk of its own (never deletes first — a total embedding failure on the new upload leaves the old, stale-but-present chunks rather than none at all). Scoped to `document_type = 'syllabus'` only, matching `courses.syllabus_text`'s existing single-source-of-truth design (always overwritten, never appended); `'material'` documents are untouched since a course intentionally accumulates many of those. Safe against already-generated presentations/quizzes: their citations are text snapshots captured at generation time, not live foreign keys into `document_chunks`, so history isn't affected — only future retrieval stops surfacing the stale content.
- **Long reviews (ВКР) moved onto a durable job queue (TODO Improvement #1).** `POST /api/grading/review` used to run `runLongReview` fire-and-forget inside the Express process — a PM2 restart mid-review silently orphaned the work; the teacher refreshed and the row just sat at whatever status it was last at, forever. Now the request enqueues onto pg-boss (`services/jobQueue.ts`), which persists the job as a Postgres row before the 202 response is sent. pg-boss v10.4.2 pinned deliberately over the current v12 — v12 is ESM-only and this backend compiles to CommonJS; v10 is CommonJS and requires Node ≥20, matching production's NodeSource provisioning exactly. No new migration needed — pg-boss manages its own schema.

  `services/longReviewWorker.ts` registers the worker (retryLimit 2, 30s backoff, 30-minute expiry — generous enough to cover the slowest Tier-4/5 reasoner passes on a large ВКР). `services/longReview.ts`'s `runLongReview` now throws on failure instead of swallowing it internally, so pg-boss's retry/dead-letter machinery actually sees failed attempts; `long_reviews.status = 'failed'` is only written on the *last* retry attempt, so the UI doesn't flash "failed" moments before a silent automatic retry picks the job back up. Retries re-run the whole pipeline from scratch (no section-level checkpointing in this pass) — acceptable for now since the common failure mode is transient (provider blip, restart), not a systematic per-section failure; full checkpoint/resume is a natural follow-up if retry-from-scratch proves too costly in practice. Same queue infra is intended to power bulk grading (TODO Feature A) later.

### Fixed
- **Stale PWA clients could silently ignore a deploy forever.** `vite.config.ts` registers the service worker in `registerType: 'prompt'` mode (deliberately — an unprompted mid-session reload would wipe unsaved grading edits), but `NewVersionToast.tsx`'s "×" and "Позже" buttons just hid the prompt with no follow-up: a waiting service worker never self-activates while any tab of the site stays open, so a user who dismissed the prompt once — or simply kept a tab pinned for days, as a teacher mid-semester plausibly would — could run the exact pre-deploy build indefinitely. This is why some users reported not seeing changes from recent deploys.

  Fix: track when an update was first detected in `localStorage` (survives reloads/tab reopens) and escalate to a non-dismissible variant of the same toast once 24h have passed, regardless of an earlier dismissal — the "×"/"Позже" buttons disappear and the copy changes to make the reload mandatory. Pure grace-period math extracted into `lib/swUpdateGrace.ts` (`isUpdateStale()`) with unit tests, since the component itself depends on the `virtual:pwa-register/react` module and can't be exercised without a production build + a real second deployed version.

### Added
- **LTI 1.3 backlog completion: NRPS roster sync, course-mapping admin UI, IMS Dynamic Registration (TODO item R, final three milestones).** Only a launch activity log remains on the LTI backlog after this.

  **NRPS roster sync:** the `https://purl.imsglobal.org/spec/lti-nrps/claim/namesroleservice` claim (previously unparsed) is now captured at instructor launch onto `lti_course_links.nrps_context_memberships_url` (migration `068_lti_nrps_registration.sql`). New `fetchRoster()` in `services/ltiServices.ts` follows the exact `syncGradeToLtiGradebook` pattern (`getServiceAccessToken` with the NRPS scope, then a scoped fetch), filtered to Learner-role members. New `GET/POST /:id/lti-roster` routes on the `publishedAssignments.ts` router feed a roster picker in `PublishedAssignmentDetail.tsx` — a checkbox list that bulk-creates ordinary invites via the existing `addInvite()`, so imported students land on the same tokenised flow as manually-added ones.

  **Course-mapping admin UI:** new `GET/PUT /api/institution/lti/course-links` routes list every `lti_course_links` row (unmapped-first) and let the admin set `org_unit_id`. Extracted `buildTree()` and `TYPE_LABEL` out of `InstitutionStructure.tsx` into a shared `frontend/src/lib/orgTree.ts` so the new course-mapping picker modal in `InstitutionLti.tsx` reuses the exact same tree-construction logic instead of a second implementation. Purely additive — an unmapped course link has always worked fine for grading since courses auto-create on first launch; mapping only feeds institutional reporting rollups.

  **IMS Dynamic Registration:** a "Получить ссылку регистрации" button generates a short-lived, single-use link (`lti_registration_sessions` table, same pattern as `lti_launch_states`) the admin pastes into Moodle's own "Dynamic registration" screen. `GET /api/lti/register` (new, public — this is a platform-initiated browser flow, not a JSON API) receives Moodle's `openid_configuration` + `registration_token` query params, fetches the platform's OIDC discovery document, POSTs an OIDC Dynamic Client Registration request carrying the `https://purl.imsglobal.org/spec/lti-tool-configuration` extension claim, and writes the platform's assigned `client_id` + discovered endpoints straight into `institutions.lti_platform_*` — replacing the manual copy-paste-seven-fields Setup flow, which remains as a fallback for platforms without Dynamic Registration support. Responds with a small self-contained HTML confirmation page since it renders inside Moodle's own registration popup, not the ИСПУМ SPA.
- **LTI 1.3 help article + landing-page treatment.** New `/help` article `lti-moodle` under a new "Организация" category — Moodle-side setup steps for the institution admin, what teachers/students experience, common launch error messages explained in plain language, and how to read the gradebook-sync badge. Landing page: a 5th feature pillar (matching the visual pattern of the existing проверка/лекции/тесты/аналитика blocks) with a Moodle-launch mockup, plus a "Интеграция с Moodle (LTI 1.3)" bullet on the Институт pricing card and a matching row in the feature-comparison table (Институт-only, alongside the existing SAML "Авто-вход по корпоративному домену" row).
- **LTI 1.3 AGS grade write-back (TODO item R, third milestone).** Approving a grade for an LTI-launched submission now posts the score to the Moodle gradebook automatically — the fourth fire-and-forget side effect in `services/grading.ts` `approve()`, alongside the existing embedding/policy-memo calls, same "never blocks or fails the approve response" contract.

  New `services/ltiServices.ts` `syncGradeToLtiGradebook()` resolves the student's LMS identity and the AGS lineitem URL via a new query `findLtiGradeSyncTarget()` (`db/queries/ltiLineItems.ts`) — joining the approved assignment back through the `assignment_invites` row that produced it (via `assignment_id`) to the `lti_line_items` row captured opportunistically during that student's earlier launch. Silently no-ops for the overwhelming majority of assignments, which aren't LTI-sourced at all.

  New `services/lti.ts` `getServiceAccessToken()` completes the AGS client_credentials flow (RFC 7523 JWT-bearer assertion, using the existing `mintServiceAssertion()`) — cached per (institution, scope set) until shortly before the platform's own token expiry. Sync outcome is recorded on `assignments.lti_gradebook_synced_at`/`lti_gradebook_sync_error` (columns already existed from migration 066) and surfaced as a small "✓ Moodle" / "⚠ Moodle" badge on the grade detail modal.
- **LTI 1.3 Deep Linking + student launch (TODO item R, second milestone).** Teachers can now add GradeAssist as a Moodle activity via Deep Linking — Moodle POSTs an `LtiDeepLinkingRequest` to the same `/api/lti/launch` endpoint, the teacher is JIT-provisioned (same path as a regular launch) and redirected to a new picker page (`LtiDeepLink.tsx`, route `/lti/deep-link`) to pick an existing published assignment or create one. The course is resolved/created at this point (not deferred to first student launch) so the new assignment is course-scoped from creation — otherwise its grades would never feed course-scoped RAG. New `lti_deep_link_sessions` table (migration `067_lti_deep_linking.sql`) bridges the launch POST and the interactive picker, since the response can only be built after the teacher's selection. The tool signs the `LtiDeepLinkingResponse` itself (`services/lti.ts` `mintDeepLinkingResponse()`, using the same keypair as service-token assertions) — the one place the tool signs an outbound JWT rather than verifying an inbound one. The response requests the platform auto-provision an AGS gradebook column (`lineItem: { scoreMaximum: 100 }`) scoped to the new resource link, setting up the (still separate) grade write-back milestone.

  Students who then launch that Moodle activity (`LtiResourceLinkRequest`, Learner role) land directly on the existing tokenised `/student/:token` writing surface — `findOrCreateLtiInvite()` in `db/queries/publishedAssignments.ts` idempotently resolves an `assignment_invites` row keyed by `(published_assignment_id, lti_subject)` (mirroring re-launch tolerance: closing the tab and reopening from Moodle lands on the same in-progress draft), tags it with `lti_subject`/`lti_deployment_id`/`lti_context_id`, and redirects straight there — no separate GradeAssist login, ever, matching the "never store student credentials" constraint. The AGS lineitem URL from the student's own launch claims is opportunistically captured (`db/queries/ltiLineItems.ts`) as a forward-compatible, best-effort step for the grade write-back milestone — a no-op if the course link hasn't been established yet.

- **LTI 1.3 launch: teacher JIT provisioning from Moodle (TODO item R, first milestone).** GradeAssist can now be registered as an LTI 1.3 tool in Moodle — a teacher clicks "Open in GradeAssist" from a course and lands authenticated, no separate login, no manual roster/grade export. Modeled directly on the existing SAML SSO integration (same architecture, different protocol): migration `066_lti_platforms.sql` adds per-institution platform config (`institutions.lti_*`, mirroring `saml_*`), teacher JIT-provisioning fields (`teachers.lti_subject`/`lti_provisioned_at`, mirroring `saml_subject`), an OIDC state/nonce replay-defense table (`lti_launch_states`), a launch-time course-context cache (`lti_course_links`), and AGS/student-rail scaffolding columns for later milestones (`lti_line_items`, `assignments.lti_gradebook_*`, `assignment_invites.lti_*` — all additive/nullable, existing tokenised student rail untouched).

  New `services/lti.ts` (JWKS validation via `jose`, OIDC state/nonce handling) and `routes/lti.ts` (`GET /login`, `POST /launch`, `GET /jwks`) mirror `services/saml.ts`/`routes/sso.ts` structurally. `findOrCreateLtiTeacher()` in `db/queries/teachers.ts` is a near-literal copy of `findOrCreateSamlTeacher()` — same "never re-attach institution_id across institutions" guard, same default-department placement. New tool identity: a single global RSA keypair (`scripts/generateLtiToolKeypair.ts`, env `LTI_TOOL_PRIVATE_KEY`/`LTI_TOOL_KID`) published live at `/api/lti/jwks` — no self-signed X.509 cert needed, unlike SAML, since platforms verify by fetching our JWKS rather than pinning a cert fingerprint.

  **Course resolution:** first launch of a Moodle context auto-creates a `courses` row owned by the launching teacher (`db/queries/ltiCourseLinks.ts`) — the teacher is productive immediately; mapping the context to an `org_unit` for institutional reporting rollups (using `org_units.external_code`, added for exactly this in migration 045) is a separate, later, non-blocking admin step. **Known v1 limitation** (logged in TODO.md): a co-taught Moodle course maps to whichever teacher launches it first.

  New self-serve **Settings → Organisation → LTI** page (`InstitutionLti.tsx`) lets an institution admin register the platform (issuer, client ID, deployment IDs, endpoints) and test the JWKS connection — deliberately *not* mirroring SAML's platform-admin-only config location, since LTI setup is self-service-critical for institutional sales (Research.md §6.5).

  **Scope explicitly deferred to later milestones** (schema already anticipates all of them): AGS grade write-back, NRPS roster sync, Deep Linking + student launch (the tokenised `/student/:token` rail stays completely unchanged — LTI will only ever change how an invite is created and how a student arrives at that URL), course-mapping UI + activity log, IMS Dynamic Registration. Instructor-role launches only for now — a Learner-role launch is explicitly rejected with a clear message.
- **Onboarding signposting for the criteria model (TODO Improvement #6).** A fresh free user landing on grading previously saw "Без критериев (общая оценка)" in the criterion picker and could grade holistically forever, never discovering the separate `/criteria` library — the platform's stated differentiator vs. "another GPT wrapper." New `components/onboarding/CriteriaHint.tsx`, a direct mirror of the existing `NoCourseHint.tsx` pattern: queries the teacher's own criteria (`getCriteria()`, no templates counted — this is about discovering the *library*, not just picking a starter template) and renders a subtle amber link to `/criteria` only when that list is empty. Placed in `GradingForm.tsx` right where the gap manifests — under the criterion-add select, gated on `picked.length === 0` for the current grade (not just an account-wide empty check), so it disappears the moment a criterion is picked for this submission, same as the placeholder text it sits beside. Deliberately left the dashboard `OnboardingChecklist.tsx` and the `/criteria` page's own empty state untouched: `/criteria` already has a `FeatureIntro` + template picker + empty-state copy, so the actual gap was purely the missing signpost at the point of contact (`GradingForm`), not there; adding a 4th checklist step would have risked re-surfacing the dashboard checklist for existing users who'd already completed and permanently hidden it (computed fresh from live queries, not a persisted "done" flag) — a real regression risk for no added value, since those users already know criteria exist.
- **Criterion-level RAG retrieval (TODO Improvement #9) — sharpen the RAG flywheel from whole-assignment similarity down to per-criterion feedback.** Today's RAG already retrieves whole approved assignments as few-shot examples; grading "аргументация" now additionally pulls past approved feedback specifically on that criterion, not just a whole essay that happens to be similar overall. New table `criterion_rag_examples` (migration `065_criterion_rag_examples.sql`) — one row per (assignment, criterion) with its own embedding, mirroring the existing `document_chunks` pattern (separate table + own query file `db/queries/criterionExamples.ts`) rather than denormalizing into `assignments`.

  **Cost/architecture decision:** rather than embedding each criterion's feedback as a *query* at grading time (which would multiply embedding-provider calls by criteria count, per grading), criterion feedback is embedded once at **approval time** (`services/embeddings.ts`'s new `generateCriterionEmbeddings()`, fire-and-forget alongside the existing `generateAndStoreEmbedding()` call) and the query side reuses the **already-computed submission embedding** at grading time — zero new embedding-provider calls per grade, only extra (cheap, local) Postgres queries, one per criterion, run in parallel.

  Criteria are ephemeral per-assignment snapshots, not FK-based, so cross-assignment matching is by `LOWER(criterion_name)` (same weight-suffix strip `normaliseCriteriaScores` already applies, extracted into a small `normaliseCriterionName()` helper used on both the write and read side) rather than `criterion_id`. Matches render as a short "Похожие прошлые оценки по этому критерию" snippet appended under the relevant criterion line in `buildCriteriaPrompt()` — capped at 2 examples/criterion, 200 chars each, to bound prompt growth. No new plan flag: gated entirely by the existing `ragFlywheel` Pro+ flag already gating the rest of RAG.

  **Scope, deliberately limited for v1:** own-course only, no institution-pool cross-teacher union (`courses.teacher_id` is `NOT NULL` — one course row belongs to exactly one teacher, so course-scoping alone is already teacher-scoping; institution-wide criterion sharing is a bigger, separate scope call for later). Not threaded through the eval harness's offline replay (`evalHarness.ts` builds its own RAG examples independently and doesn't touch criteria-aware retrieval either) — same "documented, not silently dropped" precedent as calc verification's eval-harness gap. No backfill migration — the table accumulates naturally as assignments get (re-)approved going forward.

  Pure prompt-rendering behavior (snippet formatting, truncation, case-insensitive key matching, no-op when `criterionExamples` is omitted) covered in `grading.test.ts`; the new query file's DB behavior (`criterionExamples.integration.test.ts`) covers cosine ordering, case-insensitive matching, and course scoping.
- **Real DB-backed integration testing (TODO Improvement #7).** The Vitest suite previously covered pure functions only — plan-limit enforcement, the T-Bank payment webhook, auth/JWT lifecycle, and RAG retrieval queries had zero automated coverage. New parallel `vitest.integration.config.ts` (separate from the existing pure-function `vitest.config.ts` — a contributor without local Postgres can still run `npm test`) adds 33 tests across 6 files against a real dedicated test database (`gradeassist_test`) on the existing local Postgres instance.

  **Two architecture decisions departed from the original TODO note ("Testcontainers Node, or pg-mem"), discovered during setup:** Docker isn't available in this environment, ruling out Testcontainers; `pg-mem` doesn't support the `pgvector` extension, a hard blocker for the RAG query tests (half the point of this work). Landed on a real Postgres test DB instead — `DB_POOL_MAX=1` (already env-configurable in `connection.ts`) enables a transaction-per-test rollback pattern (`BEGIN` in `beforeEach`/`ROLLBACK` in `afterEach`) that isolates every test with zero cleanup code, since every query in a test routes through the pool's single connection.

  New: `scripts/setup-test-db.js` exported `migrate()` from the CLI script so both the real `npm run migrate` and the test harness's `globalSetup` share one migration-running implementation; `scripts/setup-test-db.ts` (one-time `npm run test:integration:setup`) creates the test DB + enables `pgvector` if missing; `src/db/__tests__/fixtures.ts` (test teacher/institution/course/assignment builders, plus `unitVector256()` — deterministic synthetic embeddings so RAG cosine-similarity ordering assertions are exact, not randomly lucky); `.env.test` (gitignored) pointing at the test DB. `src/app.ts` extracted from `index.ts` (Express app construction, no `.listen()` side effect) so `supertest` can attach to it directly — needed for the one flow that gets true HTTP-level testing: the T-Bank webhook, because its exact wire format (status code + body string) is a contract with a third party, not something we control on both ends. New devDependency `supertest` (+ `@types/supertest`, MIT, clean audit).

  Coverage: `usage_counters` create/increment/month-rollover-reset; `checkMonthlyLimit`/`checkFeatureAccess`/`checkResourceLimit` middleware at their exact 403-boundary payloads; `confirmPayment()` idempotency (the exact bug class a payment system can't afford — a second webhook delivery must not re-fire); the webhook route's full wire contract (valid signature → 200 "OK" + teacher upgraded to Pro; tampered `Token` → 403 "INVALID TOKEN"; unknown `OrderId` → 200 "OK" with no write); `authenticate` middleware's institution-tier inheritance (`computeEffectiveTier`) and `is_active`/`password_changed_at` rejection paths; RAG's `findSimilarAssignments` cosine ordering, the institution-pool's **double opt-in** gating (all four combinations of `institutions.shared_rag_enabled` × `courses.share_rag_with_institution` — exactly the kind of logic that silently breaks), `findContrastingAssignment`, and `findSimilarAssignmentsBefore`'s no-future-leakage guarantee. Pure JWT edge cases (expired/tampered/malformed tokens) moved to a regular `lib/jwt.test.ts` alongside the rest of the pure suite — no DB needed for those.

  **Caught mid-implementation, not in review:** the `*.integration.test.ts` suffix also matches the default config's `src/**/*.test.ts` glob (a suffix match, not an exact-name match) — running plain `npm test` briefly executed the integration suite too, against whatever `DATABASE_URL` the default `vitest.setup.ts` loads (the real dev DB, not the test one). No data actually persisted (transaction rollback happened to hold under sequential queries and pg-pool's idle-connection reuse), but that was luck, not a guarantee. Fixed by explicitly excluding `src/**/*.integration.test.ts` from `vitest.config.ts`, with a comment warning not to remove the exclusion without re-verifying the split.

  Out of scope for this pass (documented, not silently dropped): full HTTP-level tests for `/register`/`/login` (tested at the middleware/query level instead — the webhook is the one flow whose wire format justified the `supertest` investment); CI wiring (no CI pipeline exists in this repo yet to wire a Postgres service container into).
